### 6. user (2026-05-31T08:15:08.467Z)

1	# Current State
2	
3	> **Last Updated:** 2026-06 (post tech-debt audit + ruff cleanup)
4	> This document summarizes the current capabilities, completed features, and near-term next steps for `muneem`.
5	> See [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) for the authoritative milestone tracking.
6	
7	## 1. Core Ingestion Pipeline
8	The ingestion pipeline supports a generic `SearchCriteria` interface and multiple providers (Track B plumbing):
9	
10	* **Gmail** (`muneem.ingestion.gmail`): Full OAuth2 read-only + search + attachment download implemented.
11	* **IMAP** (`muneem.ingestion.imap`): Basic auth + search implemented; `get_headers` / attachment methods are NotImplemented (experimental).
12	* **Outlook** (`muneem.ingestion.outlook`): Device-flow auth + basic search implemented; attachment/header/download NotImplemented (experimental).
13	
14	Companion one-time auth tools live in `tools/`. No high-level `muneem fetch` CLI command yet (see Roadmap B.4).
15	
16	## 2. Statement Parsers (Track A + C progress)
17	Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).
18	
19	* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). Extracts date, narration, value date, ref, withdrawal, deposit, closing balance. **Self-validating:** balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own end-of-statement SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Verified complete on real statements (37 & 31 txns) without inspecting any value.
20	* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings (`axis_transactions`) + Credit Card (`axis_cc_transactions`). ⚠️ The savings `extract_tables()` mapping is **unreliable on real Axis layouts** — column counts and date placement vary between statements (e.g. Jun vs Oct 2025), so it can mislabel rows. Reconciliation catches this, so as a **stop-gap the savings parser refuses to store rows that don't reconcile** (marks the document `unreconciled`, inserts 0) instead of persisting garbage. **Pending real fix: an HDFC-style coordinate-clustering rewrite** validated by total reconciliation (`opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing`). Credit-card has no running balance (parse/persist split + count only).
21	* **ICICI Bank** (`icici.py`): Transaction / savings statements (`icici_transactions`); **reconciles** + records status.
22	
23	All balance-bearing parsers share one reconciler (`muneem.parsers.validation`) and split parsing from DB-insert, so the row logic is unit-testable without a PDF/DB and a parse is validated before it's trusted. Parsers use `pdfplumber` (with optional password); HDFC parses by word-position clustering, Axis/ICICI by `extract_tables()` (reconciliation will flag it if that drops rows on a real statement). **Password resolution** (password_rules + hybrid unlock) is **not yet wired** into the main `muneem ingest --doc-type bank_statement` path; it is exercised only via the `@pytest.mark.local_statements` test in `tests/parsers/test_local_axis.py`. Unlock hybrid logic exists in `unlock.py` (pypdf + candidates/prompt) and is tested.
24	
25	See `password_rules.py` (now prefers user config dir) and `cli.py:ingest`.
26	
27	## 3. Database & CLI
28	* Schema v4 with `documents`, `offers`, and per-bank txn tables (`axis_*`, `icici_transactions`, `hdfc_transactions`). Dedup on sha256. No versioned migration scripts yet (IF NOT EXISTS + user_version pragma).
29	* CLI: `ingest <pdf>` (promo or bank_statement via parser), `offers list`, `txns list <bank>`.
30	* Config uses platformdirs for local dirs; secrets via keyring.
31	
32	## 4. Testing & Hygiene
33	* Strong offline regression on `testdata/promo-emails/` (T1/T3 in default CI).
34	* Security guards in `test_repo_hygiene.py` + license allowlist test.
35	* Local-only tests for real statements (gitignored data).
36	
37	## 5. Next Steps (Roadmap Alignment)
38	Focus remains on completing Milestone C (HDFC vertical slice + unlock wiring) then D (harden, logging, evaluation) before generalize. Open items (DESIGN §8.3 LLM for sensitive, §8.5 merchant canonicalization) stay parked.
39	
40	**Tech debt reduced in recent cleanup PR** (see PR for details): deduped helpers, DRY DB inserts, improved password rule loading, ruff format enforcement, doc accuracy.
41	
42	The project stays **local-first**, secrets in OS keychain, promo corpus safe for CI/LLM experiments.
43

### 7. user (2026-05-31T08:15:08.949Z)

1	# muneem — Project Plan & Roadmap
2	
3	> Status: **active plan, pre-implementation.** This is the "how and in what order" companion to [`DESIGN.md`](../DESIGN.md) (the "what and why"). DESIGN.md owns the architecture and the threat model; this doc owns sequencing, milestones, and concrete tasks. Where they disagree, DESIGN.md wins and this doc should be corrected.
4	>
5	> Created 2026-05-30.
6	
7	---
8	
9	## ⚠️ Known issues — must-fix
10	
11	- **Axis savings parser needs a thorough rewrite.** The current `extract_tables()`-based
12	  mapping is **wrong and unreliable on real Axis layouts**: the real columns are
13	  `Txn Date | Transaction | Withdrawals | Deposits | Balance | Other Information` (not the
14	  assumed `Debit/Credit`), the column count and date placement **vary between statements**
15	  (Jun 2025 = 8 cols, per-row balance, date merged into the narration cell; Oct 2025 = 9
16	  cols, clean date in col 0, no per-row balance), `can_handle` matches "Statement of Account"
17	  but the text says "Account Statement", and the account-number regex misses. **Stop-gapped
18	  in PR #19** — `AxisParser` now refuses to persist a parse that doesn't reconcile (stores 0
19	  rows, marks the document `unreconciled`), so no mislabeled data is saved, but it parses
20	  nothing useful yet. **Real fix:** an HDFC-style **coordinate-clustering** rewrite, validated
21	  by **total reconciliation** (`opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing`, confirmed TRUE
22	  on the Oct 2025 statement) plus the opening/closing bookend. Validate against the June +
23	  October 2025 statements and any future Axis files. Credit-card (`AxisCreditCardParser`) is
24	  separate and unverified on real data. **The rewrite should land on the new concept-core
25	  architecture — see [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md) (it's step 3 of that
26	  plan and the first real test of "generic-first").**
27	
28	---
29	
30	## 1. Decisions locked (2026-05-30)
31	
32	These resolve open decisions from DESIGN.md § 8. They were made by the user explicitly; this section is the start of the decision log.
33	
34	| DESIGN.md ref | Decision | Choice |
35	|---|---|---|
36	| § 8.1 Language | Implementation language for v1 | **Python-only.** Accept the heavier deployment story in exchange for the best PDF/OCR/LLM ecosystem and fastest path on the hard parsing work. Revisit only if the ingestion daemon outgrows Python. |
37	| § 8.4 First slice | What to build first | **Both tracks in parallel** — the promo-corpus parse→store→query spine *and* the Gmail ingestion plumbing, developed as two decoupled tracks that converge at the first bank slice. |
38	| § 8.2 Passwords | Password sourcing strategy | **Hybrid: candidate dictionary → per-sender rule template → interactive prompt.** Cache the winning method per sender. Secrets never touch the repo or logs. |
39	| (practical) | First real bank parser target | **HDFC** — born-digital statements, name+DDMM-style PDF password (per CLAUDE.md), and the issuer with the longest consistent statement history to test against. |
40	
41	**Still parked** (do not pick silently — see § 9 below): § 8.3 LLM model for *sensitive* categories, § 8.5 merchant canonicalization, and the timing of encryption-at-rest (SQLCipher).
42	
43	---
44	
45	## 2. Guiding principles (inherited from DESIGN.md § 5 + CLAUDE.md)
46	
47	1. **Local by default.** All raw statement data and the DB stay on the machine. OCR/LLM work runs locally for any sensitive category. No cloud sync of staging or DB, ever.
48	2. **No cloud LLM on sensitive contents** (bank, CC, broker, MF, insurance) without explicit per-category approval in-session. The **promo corpus is the one non-sensitive playground** where cloud LLMs are policy-permitted — so it's where we validate any LLM-extraction pattern *before* it could ever see a statement.
49	3. **Test on safe, committed data wherever possible.** `testdata/promo-emails/` is the regression corpus and runs in CI. Statement parsing gets CI coverage via **synthetic/redacted fixtures** in the issuer's layout — never real statements.
50	4. **Secrets discipline.** OAuth tokens → OS keychain (Windows Credential Manager). Nothing sensitive in git; don't weaken `.gitignore`. Logs are redacted (no account numbers, balances, line items, or statement text).
51	5. **Decisions parked for humans stay parked.** Don't write code that silently locks in § 9's open items.
52	
53	---
54	
55	## 3. Proposed Python stack
56	
57	User-locked is Python-only. The libraries below are *recommendations* (well-known OSS, per CLAUDE.md's "batteries-included stdlib + well-known OSS over novel dependencies") — open to override, not parked decisions. Anything that is a cloud **service** is flagged and needs a nod before adoption.
58	
59	| Concern | Proposed | Notes |
60	|---|---|---|
61	| Packaging / deps / venv | **uv** + `pyproject.toml` | Fast, modern. Poetry is an equally fine fallback. |
62	| Lint + format | **ruff** | One tool, fast. |
63	| Tests | **pytest** | Regression suite runs offline in CI on the promo corpus + synthetic fixtures. |
64	| PDF text + image extraction | **PyMuPDF (`fitz`)** | Carried from Email-Mining; confirmed the right library. |
65	| Table recovery (born-digital statements) | **pdfplumber** | Coordinate/word-level extraction for HDFC's transaction tables. |
66	| PDF decryption | **pikepdf** (libqpdf) | Robust standard-security-handler support. `pypdf` is the fallback. |
67	| Image dedup | **Pillow + scikit-image SSIM** | Carried from Email-Mining (MD5 hash + SSIM ≥ 0.95). |
68	| OCR (image PDFs, later) | **Tesseract** via `pytesseract` | Local. Windows needs the Tesseract binary installed (setup task). |
69	| Gmail | **google-api-python-client + google-auth-oauthlib** | Read-only scope `gmail.readonly`. |
70	| Secret storage | **keyring** | Uses Windows Credential Manager on win32. |
71	| DB | **sqlite3 (stdlib)** + lightweight versioned migrations | Encryption-at-rest (SQLCipher via `sqlcipher3`) deferred to Milestone D per DESIGN.md. |
72	| CLI | **Typer** | Clean ergonomics; `argparse` (stdlib) is the zero-dependency fallback. |
73	| LLM (promo only, non-sensitive) | deterministic-first; cloud LLM **only if needed and approved** | See § 5 Track A. Sensitive-doc LLM is parked (§ 9 / DESIGN § 8.3). |
74	
75	---
76	
77	## 4. Build order at a glance
78	
79	```
80	Milestone 0 — Decisions & scaffolding  (no sensitive data)
81	        │
82	        ├──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
83	        ▼                               ▼                               
84	  TRACK A: Promo spine            TRACK B: Gmail ingestion         (run in parallel,
85	  parse → store → query           fetch → stage → catalog          fully decoupled)
86	  (committed corpus, CI)          (real email, local only)
87	        │                               │
88	        └───────────────┬───────────────┘
89	                        ▼
90	        Milestone C — HDFC vertical slice  (CONVERGENCE)
91	        unlock (hybrid passwords) → HDFC parser → transactions → CLI
92	        ▷ completes DESIGN.md § 7
93	                        │
94	                        ▼
95	        Milestone D — Harden & evaluate  (the pre-generalize checkpoint)
96	                        │
97	                        ▼
98	        Generalize  (more issuers, local-LLM fallback, holdings, items, analytics, UI)
99	```
100	
101	Tracks A and B are genuinely independent: A needs no OAuth and no secrets; B needs no parsing. As a single developer you can interleave them in any order — neither blocks the other. **Both must land before Milestone C**, where a fetched-and-unlocked HDFC PDF flows through the parser for the first time.
102	
103	Effort tags below are rough (S / M / L), for relative sequencing only — not calendar estimates.
104	
105	---
106	
107	## 5. Milestones & tasks
108	
109	### Milestone 0 — Decisions & scaffolding · no sensitive data
110	
111	- [ ] **0.1 (S)** Project skeleton: `pyproject.toml` (uv), package dir (name TBD, e.g. `muneem/`), `ruff` + `pytest` configured. *Do not over-materialize the source tree (DESIGN § 9) — create only what 0.x needs.*
112	- [ ] **0.2 (S)** Offline CI: GitHub Actions (or local pre-commit) that installs deps and runs `pytest` against committed data only. A trivial `test_smoke` proves the loop is green.
113	- [ ] **0.3 (S)** Config + secrets foundation: a config loader (env + a gitignored `config.toml`), and a `keyring`-backed secret accessor stub. Confirm `.gitignore` covers `config.toml` and any new secret paths.
114	- [ ] **0.4 (S)** `gitignore` audit: verify the proposed local-only dirs (`testdata/statements/`, staging dir, `*.db`) are blocked. Add a `testdata/statements/.gitkeep`-style note or README so the expected-but-absent dir is documented.
115	- **Exit:** `uv run pytest` is green in CI on a clean checkout; repo layout agreed.
116	
117	### Track A — Promo spine: parse → store → query · committed corpus, fully CI-testable
118	
119	> Resurrects the Email-Mining deals feature as muneem's first working output. Zero secrets, zero OAuth.
120	
121	- [ ] **A.1 (M)** Port Email-Mining's extraction into a real module: PyMuPDF per-page `get_text` + `get_images`, size filter (≥300×300), MD5 + SSIM dedup. Replace the edit-line-55 hardcoded path with proper arguments/config. Replace one-shot stdout with a return value.
122	- [ ] **A.2 (S)** Minimal schema slice + migration v1: `documents` (id, source, external_id, sender, subject, received_date, doc_type, sha256, path, status, created_at) and `offers` (id, document_id, merchant, promo_code, description, expiry_date, redeemed, source_text). Full schema is future work (`docs/schema.md`).
123	- [ ] **A.3 (M)** Promo extraction, **deterministic-first**: regex/heuristics for promo codes + expiry dates over the unified text. Where deterministic extraction misses, an **LLM fallback** is permitted *because promo is non-sensitive* — but that pulls in a cloud service, so it's gated on a quick okay (reuse Email-Mining's Gemini path, go local via Ollama, or use Anthropic — your call when we get there).
124	- [ ] **A.4 (M)** `muneem ingest <pdf>` writes a `documents` + `offers` row; `muneem offers list` reads them back. **Ground-truth + regression test:** establish the known promo codes for each of the 10 corpus PDFs, then a `pytest` that runs the pipeline over `testdata/promo-emails/` and asserts recovery. (Building the ground-truth answer key is itself part of this task.)
125	- **Exit:** `ingest → SQLite → offers list` works end-to-end; green regression test on the committed corpus. Stages 3→4→5 proven with zero risk.
126	
127	### Track B — Gmail ingestion: fetch → stage → catalog · real email, local only
128	
129	> First contact with real (sensitive) email. Everything it writes is gitignored.
130	
131	- [ ] **B.1 (M)** Gmail OAuth2: register a Google Cloud project, `gmail.readonly` scope, store the token in `keyring`. Document the one-time consent flow.
132	- [ ] **B.2 (M)** Financial-document detection v1: a **sender allowlist** + server-side query (`has:attachment filename:pdf from:… newer_than:…`). Start narrow and precise; broaden later. Pull matching messages + PDF attachments into a gitignored staging dir.
133	- [ ] **B.3 (S)** Incremental sync: persist Gmail `historyId`; on re-run, fetch only what's new. Idempotent (re-running never duplicates `documents`, keyed by message id + attachment hash).
134	- [ ] **B.4 (S)** Catalog fetched attachments as `documents` rows (status `locked`/`unparsed`), with sha256 + staging path. `muneem fetch --since 90d` is the entry point.
135	- **Exit:** `muneem fetch` populates the staging dir + `documents` rows from a narrow real-Gmail query. (Manual/local verification — not CI; it touches a real account.)
136	
137	### Milestone C — HDFC vertical slice · CONVERGENCE · completes DESIGN.md § 7
138	
139	> A fetched HDFC statement gets unlocked and parsed into real transactions. This is the moment every stage runs once on real data the user cares about.
140	
141	- [ ] **C.1 (M)** Unlocking (Stage 2) with the **hybrid** strategy: candidate dictionary derived from user attributes (e.g. `first4(name).upper() + DDMM(dob)`) → per-sender rule template → interactive prompt. Cache the winning method per sender in the keychain. **No password material in repo or logs.** Decrypt with pikepdf; advance `documents.status` to `unlocked`.
142	- [ ] **C.2 (L)** HDFC parser (Stage 3, the hard 80%): born-digital text-layer + pdfplumber table recovery → normalized transactions (txn_date, value_date, amount, direction, narration, running balance). **Synthetic HDFC-layout fixtures** committed for CI; real statements stay gitignored locally.
143	- [ ] **C.3 (M)** Schema migration v2: `accounts` (issuer, account_type, last4, holder) + `transactions` (account_id, document_id, txn fields, category, merchant_id-nullable). Populate from the parser. `muneem txns list --last-month` prints real parsed transactions.
144	- **Exit:** **DESIGN.md § 7 vertical slice complete** — Gmail → HDFC statement → unlocked → parsed → SQLite → CLI lists last month's transactions, on real data, locally.
145	
146	### Milestone D — Harden & evaluate · the pre-generalize checkpoint
147	
148	- [ ] **D.1 (M)** Redacted structured logging (assert no statement text / account numbers leak); idempotent, resumable re-runs across all stages.
149	- [ ] **D.2 (S)** Encryption-at-rest decision (DESIGN § 5.4): adopt SQLCipher now or schedule it. (Parked item — bring evidence, then decide.)
150	- [ ] **D.3 (M)** **Evaluation writeup:** how well did the deterministic HDFC parser hold across statement vintages? Where does it break? Which categories will need the local-LLM fallback? This is the evidence DESIGN § 7 asks for *before* generalizing. Capture in `docs/parser-notes-hdfc.md`.
151	- **Exit:** the slice is robust and re-runnable; a written go/no-go on generalization with real evidence.
152	
153	---
154	
155	## 6. Generalize (post-checkpoint backlog — scope after Milestone D)
156	
157	Ordered roughly by leverage, not committed:
158	
159	- Second/third bank parsers + the first **credit-card bill** parser (reuses unlock + parser scaffolding).
160	- **Local-LLM long-tail fallback** (Ollama) for issuers that don't justify a bespoke parser — **local, because these are sensitive** (resolves DESIGN § 8.3).
161	- Broker / MF statements → `holdings` (point-in-time positions; the investment-timeline use-case).
162	- Itemized receipts (Swiggy/Amazon/BigBasket/etc.) → `items` (the price-intelligence + catalog use-cases).
163	- **Merchant canonicalization** → `merchants` (DESIGN § 8.5 — defer until real data exposes the actual mess, then decide rules-file vs. library vs. learned).
164	- Analytics surfaces for the DESIGN § 2 use-cases (CLI views first; optional small **local** web UI later).
165	
166	---
167	
168	## 7. Cross-cutting workstreams (run throughout, not a phase)
169	
170	- **Security & secrets hygiene** — keychain for tokens + cached passwords; redacted logs; gitignore audits each milestone; encryption-at-rest tracked to D.2.
171	- **Schema migrations from day one** — versioned migration scripts; the schema *will* churn as new issuers land. Keep `docs/schema.md` current.
172	- **Test-corpus curation** — ground-truth answer key for the promo corpus (A.4); synthetic/redacted statement fixtures for CI (C.2). Never commit real statements.
173	- **Decision log** — append resolutions to § 1 here and mark the corresponding DESIGN.md § 8 item resolved.
174	
175	---
176	
177	## 8. Risks & mitigations
178	
179	| Risk | Mitigation |
180	|---|---|
181	| Sensitive data leaks into git, logs, or a cloud LLM | Local-by-default; promo-only LLM playground; redacted logging (D.1); gitignore audits; the § 2 principles are non-negotiable. |
182	| HDFC parser is brittle across statement vintages | Synthetic fixtures + the D.3 evaluation gate *before* generalizing; deterministic-first with a planned local-LLM fallback for the long tail. |
183	| Password sourcing fails noisily on backfill | Hybrid strategy degrades to interactive prompt; cache per sender so it's a one-time cost. |
184	| Gmail OAuth / quota friction | Read-only scope, server-side queries (cheap), incremental `historyId` sync; narrow allowlist first. |
185	| Scope creep into the full § 2 vision before the slice works | Hard checkpoint at Milestone D; generalization is explicitly post-checkpoint. |
186	| Two parallel tracks diverge / integration surprises at C | Both write through the *same* `documents` table and schema-migration discipline from A.2/B.4, so convergence is a join, not a rewrite. |
187	
188	---
189	
190	## 9. Still-open decisions (do NOT pick silently)
191	
192	Carried from DESIGN.md § 8, not yet resolved — surface and ask when a milestone forces them:
193	
194	- **§ 8.3 — LLM model for sensitive categories.** Coupled to your hardware (GPU? RAM?) and latency tolerance. Forced at the start of the generalize phase (local-LLM fallback). Ollama + Llama/Qwen/Mistral is the likely shape, but unconfirmed.
195	- **§ 8.5 — Merchant canonicalization.** Deferred until real receipt data exposes the mess. Forced when `items`/`merchants` land.
196	- **Encryption-at-rest timing** (D.2) — SQLCipher now vs. later.
197	- **Promo-track LLM** (A.3) — only reached if deterministic extraction underperforms; cloud is policy-permitted for promo but still needs a quick okay on which vendor.
198	
199	---
200	
201	## 10. Definition of done for v1
202	
203	The DESIGN.md § 7 slice, hardened: **`muneem fetch` pulls HDFC statements from Gmail → unlocks them with the hybrid password strategy → the HDFC parser writes normalized `transactions` to SQLite → `muneem txns list` shows last month's activity — all locally, with redacted logs, idempotent re-runs, green CI on synthetic fixtures, and a written evaluation of where to go next.** The promo "forgotten deals" feature ships alongside as the Track A deliverable.

### 8. user (2026-05-31T08:15:09.247Z)

1	# muneem — Parser Architecture (concept-core, verification-first)
2	
3	> **Status: DRAFT for discussion.** Direction chosen 2026-06: *"concept core + profiles-as-data."*
4	> The deterministic core below (L0–L2, schema, profiles, verifier, traps) is settled enough to
5	> build against. The **L3 local-vision-model fallback** and the **numeric-hallucination traps** are
6	> marked _pending_ the local-VLM deep-research run, which will populate them.
7	> Companion to [`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md) (what/why) and [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) (sequencing).
8	
9	---
10	
11	## 1. Why we're rethinking this
12	
13	The current parsers are brittle for two reasons:
14	
15	1. **They encode *syntax*, not *semantics*.** HDFC hard-codes x-positions; Axis hard-codes column
16	   indices. They know *where* a value sits, not *what* it is — so any format nudge maps the wrong
17	   column and produces wrong data.
18	2. **They parse open-loop.** They emit whatever they extracted with no check, so a wrong parse looks
19	   like a right one. (The Axis parser confidently produced 15 mislabeled rows — see ROADMAP Known
20	   Issues.)
21	
22	Goals for the new architecture: **format-resilient** (small layout/label changes don't break us),
23	**extensible** (a new issuer or a changed format is mostly data, not code), **semantic** (we *know*
24	the critical placeholders — balance, spend, deposit, …), and **failsafe** (drift is loud, never
25	silent).
26	
27	## 2. The key idea — reconciliation makes parsing a *closed-loop search*
28	
29	We do **not** need to perfectly parse every format. We need to **propose candidate interpretations
30	and let arithmetic pick the correct one.** The identity
31	
32	```
33	opening_balance + Σ(deposits) − Σ(withdrawals) == closing_balance     (+ the per-row balance walk)
34	```
35	
36	is a **truth oracle that is independent of layout**. That reframes everything:
37	
38	- We can identify concepts **aggressively / fuzzily**, because a wrong guess is *rejected*, not stored.
39	- It is what makes a probabilistic extractor (a vision model) **safe on money** — we never trust the
40	  model, we trust the math that checks it.
41	- A changed format is not "rewrite the parser" — it's "the search finds a mapping that reconciles."
42	
43	**Principle: extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes.**
44	
45	## 3. The canonical concept schema (the contract)
46	
47	One bank-agnostic vocabulary that *every* extractor targets. These are the "critical placeholders."
48	
49	- **Row concepts:** `txn_date`, `value_date`, `narration`, `reference`, `debit` (spend/withdrawal),
50	  `credit` (deposit), `balance`; or `{amount, direction}` for credit cards (no running balance).
51	- **Statement concepts:** `opening_balance`, `closing_balance`, `debit_count`, `credit_count`,
52	  `account_number`, `period_start/end`, `currency`.
53	
54	Parsing = **map this bank's columns/fields onto these concepts, then verify.** Provenance + a
55	confidence verdict travel with every parse (see §7).
56	
57	## 4. The extraction ladder (cheapest first; every layer gated by the verifier)
58	
59	| Layer | How it identifies a concept (e.g. `balance`) | Resilience it buys |
60	|---|---|---|
61	| **L0 Header lexicon** | alias + fuzzy match: `balance ← {Balance, Closing Bal, Running Bal}`, `debit ← {Withdrawal, Debit, Dr, Spend, Paid Out}`, … | renamed/abbreviated labels |
62	| **L1 Structure & content** | dates by pattern; amounts are right-aligned numerics; **`balance` = the numeric column whose running-balance recurrence holds** | missing/merged headers (the Axis-June case) |
63	| **L2 Mapping search** | enumerate plausible concept→column assignments; **keep the one(s) that reconcile** | column reorder / count drift between statements |
64	| **L3 Local vision fallback** _(pending research)_ | page image → JSON in the concept schema; **accepted only if it reconciles** | scans, no text layer, novel layouts |
65	
66	The **self-identifying balance column** (L1) is the heart of it: we find "balance" not by its label
67	but by *which column makes the math work* — fully format-independent. L0/L1 usually agree; L2 is the
68	fallback search when they don't; L3 is the last resort, and the research decides which local VLM
69	(GPU, Python, commercial-safe weights) and what number-hallucination guards it needs.
70	
71	## 5. The verifier (reconciliation-as-oracle)
72	
73	Already exists in seed form as `muneem.parsers.validation`. It grows into the component that both
74	**selects** and **validates**:
75	
76	- **Per-row walk:** `balance[i] == balance[i-1] − debit[i] + credit[i]` (catches a missed/misread row
77	  *between* any two balances).
78	- **Total reconcile:** `opening + Σcredits − Σdebits == closing` (works even when there's no per-row
79	  balance, e.g. Axis savings).
80	- **Bookend:** parsed Dr/Cr counts + opening/closing vs the bank's printed SUMMARY (catches a missing
81	  first/last row — HDFC has this; others where available).
82	- **Selection:** among L0/L1/L2 candidate mappings, pick the reconciling one; if several reconcile,
83	  prefer the highest-confidence (header-backed) one.
84	- **Confidence verdict:** `reconciled` (per-row or total) + `bookended` → trust; else quarantine.
85	
86	## 6. Bank knowledge as *data* (profiles), not code
87	
88	A bank profile is a small **declarative** object (likely YAML/dict): concept→label aliases, accepted
89	date formats, anchor strings to locate the table, account-number patterns, known quirks (multi-account
90	sections, "Other Information" column), and the password rule. **Generic detectors run first; the
91	profile supplies priors/hints** to disambiguate — it is not bespoke parsing logic. Absorbing a small
92	format change = tweak a profile (often nothing — the generic path copes). A brand-new issuer = add a
93	profile, and only drop to custom code if the generic ladder genuinely can't cope.
94	
95	## 7. Failsafes — the "traps" (loud drift, never silent)
96	
97	The Axis lesson: a broken parse must *announce itself*. Built-in tripwires, each → flag + provenance,
98	never store-and-pretend:
99	
100	- header label matches no lexicon entry → "unknown field, mapped by structure — review"
101	- reconciliation breaks, or parsed counts ≠ printed summary → **quarantine** (store nothing or store
102	  flagged; today's stop-gap already refuses to persist unreconciled rows)
103	- a profile's expected anchor is missing → "format may have changed for <bank>"
104	- _(L3)_ VLM output won't reconcile, or disagrees with a deterministic parse → reject the VLM
105	- **provenance + confidence on every parsed statement:** which layer/mapping produced it, did it
106	  reconcile / bookend. Trust is *recorded*, not assumed.
107	
108	_Pending research:_ the deep-research run will enumerate VLM failure modes on financial numbers
109	(digit transposition, dropped/duplicated rows) → these become concrete L3 traps.
110	
111	## 8. Two tiers of "semantic"
112	
113	- **Tier 1 — field semantics** (this doc): what each column *means*. The resilience win. Build first.
114	- **Tier 2 — transaction semantics** (later): classify each row (UPI / NEFT / ATM / charge / interest
115	  / sweep), extract merchant/counterparty, assign category. Same schema, layered on top; ties to the
116	  parked merchant-canonicalization decision (DESIGN § 8.5).
117	
118	## 9. Data-model evolution
119	
120	Today: per-bank raw tables (`hdfc_transactions`, `axis_transactions`, …) — fine for fidelity, but the
121	concept schema points at a **unified `transactions` table** keyed on the canonical concepts, plus
122	`account`/`document` context and **`provenance` + `confidence`** columns. Migration is additive (the
123	per-bank tables can become views or be backfilled); sequence it after the core lands so we don't churn
124	storage twice.
125	
126	## 10. Implementation plan (incremental, merge-as-we-go)
127	
128	1. **Core extraction.** Promote `validation.py` into a `verifier` (per-row + total + bookend +
129	   candidate selection + confidence). Define the concept schema as dataclasses. **Refactor HDFC onto
130	   it as the reference implementation** (no behavior change — it already reconciles + bookends).
131	2. **Concept-mapping engine (L0/L1/L2)** as generic, bank-agnostic code; express HDFC + ICICI as
132	   profiles (data) on top of it.
133	3. **Axis clustering rewrite onto the core** — this is the must-fix *and* the first real test of
134	   "generic-first": coordinate clustering → concept mapping → total reconcile. Validates on Jun + Oct
135	   2025 + future Axis files.
136	4. **L3 local-vision fallback** (after the research): a verified extractor that only fires when the
137	   deterministic ladder fails to reconcile, with the number-traps from §7.
138	5. **Unify storage** (§9) + begin **Tier-2 transaction semantics**.
139	
140	Each step is a green, reversible PR; the verifier guarantees we never regress into storing garbage.
141	
142	## 11. Open questions (for discussion)
143	
144	- **Storage unification timing** — keep per-bank tables until the concept core is proven, then migrate?
145	- **Generic-vs-profile balance** — how much to lean on profiles as priors vs. pure content inference.
146	- **L3 scope** — once the research lands: which local VLM, and is it a rare fallback or a routine
147	  cross-check on every statement?
148	- **Tier-2** — when do transaction classification + merchant canonicalization become in-scope?
149	- **Confidence policy** — what does the CLI/ledger do with a `quarantined` statement (skip, show with
150	  a warning, queue for the VLM)?
151

1	"""Shared transaction-validation helpers for bank-statement parsers.
2	
3	Reconciliation proves a parse is **complete and correct without inspecting any
4	sensitive value**: if a transaction is missed or misread, the running balance
5	stops adding up. This is the generic core used by every balance-bearing parser
6	(HDFC, Axis savings, ICICI). Reports carry counts/booleans only — never an amount.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import re
12	from collections.abc import Sequence
13	from dataclasses import dataclass
14	
15	_CR_DR_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"(?i)\s*(cr|dr)\s*$")
16	
17	
18	def parse_amount(value: object) -> float | None:
19	    """Parse a statement money cell to a float.
20	
21	    Returns ``None`` only for a genuinely absent cell (None / empty / ``"-"`` /
22	    non-numeric); ``"0.00"`` parses to ``0.0``. This deliberately distinguishes
23	    *absent* from *zero* — unlike ``base.safe_amount``, which maps ``"0.00"`` to
24	    ``None`` and would corrupt a real zero balance and the reconciliation math.
25	    """
26	    if value is None:
27	        return None
28	    text = _CR_DR_SUFFIX.sub("", str(value).strip()).replace(",", "").strip()
29	    if text in ("", "-"):
30	        return None
31	    try:
32	        return float(text)
33	    except ValueError:
34	        return None
35	
36	
37	@dataclass
38	class ReconcileReport:
39	    """Result of a running-balance walk. Counts only, never an amount."""
40	
41	    n: int  # rows considered
42	    compared: int  # consecutive (balance-bearing) pairs actually checked
43	    breaks: int
44	    first_break: int | None = None
45	
46	    @property
47	    def ok(self) -> bool:
48	        return self.compared > 0 and self.breaks == 0
49	
50	
51	def reconcile_running_balance(
52	    entries: Sequence[tuple[float | None, float | None, float | None]],
53	    tol: float = 0.01,
54	) -> ReconcileReport:
55	    """Verify ``balance[i] == balance[i-1] - debit[i] + credit[i]`` across rows.
56	
57	    ``entries`` is an ordered list of ``(debit, credit, balance)``; ``debit`` and
58	    ``credit`` may be ``None`` (treated as 0.0). A row whose ``balance`` is ``None``
59	    is skipped (can't anchor on it). Because the walk spans the whole statement, a
60	    clean result also rules out any row missed *between* two balances — which is the
61	    failure mode silent ``extract_tables()`` row-drops would otherwise hide.
62	    """
63	    breaks = 0
64	    first_break: int | None = None
65	    compared = 0
66	    prev: float | None = None
67	    for i, (debit, credit, balance) in enumerate(entries):
68	        if balance is None:
69	            continue
70	        if prev is not None:
71	            expected = prev - (debit or 0.0) + (credit or 0.0)
72	            compared += 1
73	            if abs(expected - balance) > tol:
74	                breaks += 1
75	                if first_break is None:
76	                    first_break = i
77	        prev = balance
78	    return ReconcileReport(
79	        n=len(entries), compared=compared, breaks=breaks, first_break=first_break
80	    )
81	
82	
83	__all__ = ["ReconcileReport", "reconcile_running_balance", "parse_amount"]
84

### 24. user (2026-05-31T08:17:52.470Z)

1	"""HDFC savings / transaction-account statement parser.
2	
3	**Approach:** coordinate / column clustering on **pdfplumber (MIT)** — not the
4	default ``extract_tables()`` and not a ready-made library. HDFC's transaction
5	table is *semi-ruled*, where ``extract_tables()`` is unreliable; the deep-research
6	decision (see the ``hdfc-parser-tech-decision`` memory) is to cluster words by
7	position. pdfplumber's whole chain is permissive and it opens password-protected
8	statements directly.
9	
10	**Layout.** Each transaction is one *primary* line — the transaction date at the
11	far left (``x0≈52``) plus three right-aligned amount columns (Withdrawal, Deposit,
12	Closing Balance) — followed by zero or more *continuation* lines that wrap the
13	narration and carry the value date (``dd/mm/yyyy`` at ``x0>110``) and the
14	cheque/reference number. We group words into visual lines by ``top``, detect
15	primary lines by a leading date + a closing-balance amount, attach continuations,
16	and read columns by x-position.
17	
18	**Correctness is checked, not assumed.** ``reconcile`` verifies the running
19	balance (closing[i] == closing[i-1] - withdrawal + deposit) and
20	``validate_against_summary`` cross-checks the parse against HDFC's own end-of-
21	statement SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts + opening/closing balance). Together they prove a
22	parse complete without inspecting any individual value — which is how we validate
23	against real (sensitive) statements. No statement content is logged here.
24	"""
25	
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	import csv
29	import re
30	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
31	from pathlib import Path
32	
33	import pdfplumber
34	
35	from muneem import db as dbmod
36	
37	from . import register_parser
38	from .base import StatementParser
39	from .validation import ReconcileReport, reconcile_running_balance
40	
41	# --- Column geometry (PDF points; A4-portrait HDFC detailed statement) ---------
42	# Left-aligned columns key on x0; right-aligned amount columns key on their right
43	# edge (x1), which is stable regardless of the number's magnitude.
44	_DATE_MAX_X0 = 70.0  # transaction date sits at x0≈52; the value date is at x0>110
45	_NARR_MIN_X0 = 100.0  # narration band starts at x0≈111
46	_AMOUNT_MIN_X0 = 295.0  # amount columns start at x0≈306; narration never crosses ≈290
47	_WITHDRAWAL_MAX_X1 = 400.0  # withdrawal right edge ≈358
48	_DEPOSIT_MAX_X1 = 490.0  # deposit right edge ≈443; closing balance right edge ≈561
49	_LINE_TOL = 2.5  # words within this many points of each other share a visual line
50	_CONT_MAX_SPAN = 70.0  # a continuation line is at most this far below its primary line
51	
52	_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$")
53	# Indian-format money: optional minus, comma groups, exactly two decimals, optional Cr/Dr.
54	_AMOUNT_RE = re.compile(r"^-?[\d,]+\.\d{2}(?:cr|dr)?$", re.IGNORECASE)
55	_FOOTER_MARKERS = {"STATEMENT", "SUMMARY"}
56	_ACCOUNT_RE = re.compile(r"Account\s*Number\s*[:.]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE)
57	
58	
59	@dataclass
60	class Transaction:
61	    date: str  # dd/mm/yyyy, as printed
62	    narration: str
63	    withdrawal: float
64	    deposit: float
65	    closing_balance: float
66	    value_date: str | None = None
67	    ref: str | None = None
68	    page: int = 0
69	
70	
71	@dataclass
72	class Statement:
73	    path: Path
74	    transactions: list[Transaction] = field(default_factory=list)
75	    account_number: str | None = None
76	
77	
78	@dataclass
79	class StatementSummary:
80	    """The figures HDFC prints in its own end-of-statement SUMMARY block."""
81	
82	    opening_balance: float | None = None
83	    closing_balance: float | None = None
84	    debit_count: int | None = None
85	    credit_count: int | None = None
86	    debit_amount: float | None = None
87	    credit_amount: float | None = None
88	
89	
90	@dataclass
91	class ValidationReport:
92	    """Parse cross-checked against the bank's own SUMMARY. Booleans + counts only.
93	
94	    ``None`` means a figure wasn't available to check (not a failure). ``ok``
95	    requires the balance walk plus every available check to pass.
96	    """
97	
98	    n: int
99	    reconciles: bool
100	    debit_count_ok: bool | None = None
101	    credit_count_ok: bool | None = None
102	    opening_ok: bool | None = None
103	    closing_ok: bool | None = None
104	
105	    @property
106	    def ok(self) -> bool:
107	        checks = [self.debit_count_ok, self.credit_count_ok, self.opening_ok, self.closing_ok]
108	        return self.n > 0 and self.reconciles and all(c is True for c in checks)
109	
110	
111	def _to_amount(token: str) -> float | None:
112	    """Parse an HDFC money token to a float, or None if it is not money.
113	
114	    Unlike base.safe_amount we keep ``0.00`` as ``0.0`` (not None): a zero amount
115	    is real information and the reconciliation math needs it.
116	    """
117	    if not _AMOUNT_RE.match(token):
118	        return None
119	    cleaned = re.sub(r"(?i)(cr|dr)$", "", token).replace(",", "")
120	    try:
121	        return float(cleaned)
122	    except ValueError:
123	        return None
124	
125	
126	def _group_lines(words: list[dict]) -> list[list[dict]]:
127	    """Group words into visual lines by ``top`` (tolerant of sub-pixel jitter)."""
128	    lines: list[list[dict]] = []
129	    anchor_top: float | None = None
130	    for w in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
131	        if lines and anchor_top is not None and abs(w["top"] - anchor_top) <= _LINE_TOL:
132	            lines[-1].append(w)
133	        else:
134	            lines.append([w])
135	            anchor_top = w["top"]
136	    return [sorted(line, key=lambda w: w["x0"]) for line in lines]
137	
138	
139	def _line_top(line: list[dict]) -> float:
140	    return min(w["top"] for w in line)
141	
142	
143	def _has_narration(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
144	    return any(_NARR_MIN_X0 <= w["x0"] < _AMOUNT_MIN_X0 for w in line)
145	
146	
147	def _has_amount(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
148	    return any(w["x0"] >= _AMOUNT_MIN_X0 and _AMOUNT_RE.match(w["text"]) for w in line)
149	
150	
151	def _has_leading_date(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
152	    first = line[0]
153	    return bool(_DATE_RE.match(first["text"]) and first["x0"] < _DATE_MAX_X0)
154	
155	
156	def _has_footer_marker(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
157	    return any(w["text"].upper() in _FOOTER_MARKERS for w in line)
158	
159	
160	def _is_primary(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
161	    """A transaction's first line: a date at the far left + a closing-balance amount.
162	
163	    Requiring the closing-balance amount guards against stray dates in the page
164	    header / account-summary box (e.g. the statement period).
165	    """
166	    if not _has_leading_date(line):
167	        return False
168	    return any(
169	        w["x0"] >= _AMOUNT_MIN_X0 and w["x1"] >= _DEPOSIT_MAX_X1 and _AMOUNT_RE.match(w["text"])
170	        for w in line
171	    )
172	
173	
174	def _is_continuation(line: list[dict]) -> bool:
175	    """A narration-wrap line: narration-band text, no leading date, no amounts."""
176	    return _has_narration(line) and not _has_leading_date(line) and not _has_amount(line)
177	
178	
179	def _parse_transaction(
180	    primary: list[dict], continuations: list[list[dict]], page_no: int
181	) -> Transaction:
182	    withdrawal = deposit = 0.0
183	    closing: float | None = None
184	    narr: list[tuple[float, float, str]] = []  # (top, x0, text) for ordered reassembly
185	
186	    for line in (primary, *continuations):
187	        for w in line:
188	            x0, x1, text = w["x0"], w["x1"], w["text"]
189	            if x0 >= _AMOUNT_MIN_X0:
190	                val = _to_amount(text)
191	                if val is None:
192	                    continue
193	                if x1 < _WITHDRAWAL_MAX_X1:
194	                    withdrawal = val
195	                elif x1 < _DEPOSIT_MAX_X1:
196	                    deposit = val
197	                else:
198	                    closing = val
199	            elif x0 >= _NARR_MIN_X0:
200	                narr.append((w["top"], x0, text))
201	
202	    value_date: str | None = None
203	    parts: list[str] = []
204	    for _top, _x0, text in sorted(narr):
205	        if value_date is None and _DATE_RE.match(text):
206	            value_date = text  # the value date, lifted out of the narration band
207	            continue
208	        parts.append(text)
209	
210	    ref_candidates = [p for p in parts if p.isdigit() and len(p) >= 9]
211	    ref = max(ref_candidates, key=len) if ref_candidates else None
212	
213	    return Transaction(
214	        date=primary[0]["text"],
215	        narration=" ".join(parts),
216	        withdrawal=withdrawal,
217	        deposit=deposit,
218	        closing_balance=closing if closing is not None else 0.0,
219	        value_date=value_date,
220	        ref=ref,
221	        page=page_no,
222	    )
223	
224	
225	def extract_transactions_from_lines(lines: list[list[dict]], page_no: int = 0) -> list[Transaction]:
226	    """Pure, PDF-free core: turn grouped table-body lines into transactions.
227	
228	    Kept separate from PDF I/O so the column logic is unit-testable on synthetic
229	    word lists, with no real data.
230	    """
231	    primaries = [i for i, line in enumerate(lines) if _is_primary(line)]
232	    transactions: list[Transaction] = []
233	    for k, idx in enumerate(primaries):
234	        stop = primaries[k + 1] if k + 1 < len(primaries) else len(lines)
235	        primary = lines[idx]
236	        primary_top = _line_top(primary)
237	        continuations: list[list[dict]] = []
238	        for m in range(idx + 1, stop):
239	            line = lines[m]
240	            if _has_footer_marker(line) or _line_top(line) - primary_top > _CONT_MAX_SPAN:
241	                break
242	            if _is_continuation(line):
243	                continuations.append(line)
244	        transactions.append(_parse_transaction(primary, continuations, page_no))
245	    return transactions
246	
247	
248	def _table_body(lines: list[list[dict]]) -> list[list[dict]]:
249	    """Lines below the column header (drops the per-page account-summary box)."""
250	    header_top: float | None = None
251	    for line in lines:
252	        texts = {w["text"] for w in line}
253	        if "Narration" in texts and any(t.startswith("Closing") for t in texts):
254	            header_top = _line_top(line)
255	            break
256	    if header_top is None:
257	        return []
258	    return [line for line in lines if _line_top(line) > header_top + 1]
259	
260	
261	def _extract_account_number(text: str) -> str | None:
262	    match = _ACCOUNT_RE.search(text)
263	    return match.group(1) if match else None
264	
265	
266	def parse_statement(pdf_path: Path | str, password: str = "") -> Statement:
267	    """Parse an HDFC statement PDF into a :class:`Statement` of transactions.
268	
269	    ``password`` is forwarded to pdfplumber for encrypted statements and used
270	    in-memory only — never stored or logged.
271	    """
272	    path = Path(pdf_path)
273	    transactions: list[Transaction] = []
274	    account_number: str | None = None
275	    with pdfplumber.open(str(path), password=password or "") as pdf:
276	        for page_no, page in enumerate(pdf.pages):
277	            if account_number is None and page_no < 2:
278	                account_number = _extract_account_number(page.extract_text() or "")
279	            words = page.extract_words(use_text_flow=False, keep_blank_chars=False)
280	            body = _table_body(_group_lines(words))
281	            transactions.extend(extract_transactions_from_lines(body, page_no))
282	    return Statement(path=path, transactions=transactions, account_number=account_number)
283	
284	
285	def reconcile(statement: Statement, tol: float = 0.01) -> ReconcileReport:
286	    """Check the running balance via the shared reconciler (closing[i] ==
287	    closing[i-1] - withdrawal + deposit).
288	
289	    Spans page breaks (transactions are in document order), so a clean report also
290	    validates page stitching. Reports counts only — no amounts.
291	    """
292	    entries = [(t.withdrawal, t.deposit, t.closing_balance) for t in statement.transactions]
293	    return reconcile_running_balance(entries, tol)
294	
295	
296	def _parse_summary_lines(lines: list[str]) -> StatementSummary | None:
297	    """Pure core: read HDFC's SUMMARY block from extracted text lines.
298	
299	    Layout (stable across statements)::
300	
301	        SUMMARY
302	        Opening Balance  Debit Amount  Credit Amount  Closing Balance
303	        <opening>        <debitAmt>    <creditAmt>    <closing>
304	        Debit Count  Credit Count
305	        <debitCount> <creditCount>
306	
307	    The values land on their own lines, so we take the first post-SUMMARY line
308	    carrying ≥4 money tokens (the balances) and the first carrying ≥2 bare
309	    integers and no money (the counts).
310	    """
311	    start = next((i for i, ln in enumerate(lines) if ln.strip() == "SUMMARY"), None)
312	    if start is None:
313	        return None
314	    balances: list[float] | None = None
315	    counts: list[int] | None = None
316	    for ln in lines[start + 1 : start + 8]:
317	        tokens = ln.split()
318	        monies = [v for v in (_to_amount(t) for t in tokens) if v is not None]
319	        ints = [int(t) for t in tokens if t.isdigit()]
320	        if balances is None and len(monies) >= 4:
321	            balances = monies[:4]
322	        elif counts is None and not monies and len(ints) >= 2:
323	            counts = ints[:2]
324	    if balances is None and counts is None:
325	        return None
326	    opening, debit_amt, credit_amt, closing = (balances or [None, None, None, None])[:4]
327	    debit_count, credit_count = (counts or [None, None])[:2]
328	    return StatementSummary(
329	        opening_balance=opening,
330	        closing_balance=closing,
331	        debit_count=debit_count,
332	        credit_count=credit_count,
333	        debit_amount=debit_amt,
334	        credit_amount=credit_amt,
335	    )
336	
337	
338	def parse_summary(pdf_path: Path | str, password: str = "") -> StatementSummary | None:
339	    """Read the bank's own SUMMARY figures from the statement, or None if absent."""
340	    with pdfplumber.open(str(Path(pdf_path)), password=password or "") as pdf:
341	        for page in pdf.pages:
342	            text = page.extract_text() or ""
343	            if "SUMMARY" in text:
344	                summary = _parse_summary_lines(text.splitlines())
345	                if summary is not None:
346	                    return summary
347	    return None
348	
349	
350	def validate_against_summary(
351	    statement: Statement, summary: StatementSummary, tol: float = 0.01
352	) -> ValidationReport:
353	    """Cross-check a parse against the bank's SUMMARY: reconciliation + bookends.
354	
355	    Reconciliation catches any mid-statement error/omission, the Dr/Cr counts catch
356	    any extra/missing row, and the opening & closing balances catch a missing
357	    first/last row.
358	    """
359	    txns = statement.transactions
360	    reconciles = reconcile(statement, tol).ok
361	    debit_count_ok = credit_count_ok = opening_ok = closing_ok = None
362	    if summary.debit_count is not None:
363	        debit_count_ok = sum(1 for t in txns if t.withdrawal > 0) == summary.debit_count
364	    if summary.credit_count is not None:
365	        credit_count_ok = sum(1 for t in txns if t.deposit > 0) == summary.credit_count
366	    if txns and summary.closing_balance is not None:
367	        closing_ok = abs(txns[-1].closing_balance - summary.closing_balance) <= tol
368	    if txns and summary.opening_balance is not None:
369	        implied_opening = txns[0].closing_balance - txns[0].deposit + txns[0].withdrawal
370	        opening_ok = abs(implied_opening - summary.opening_balance) <= tol
371	    return ValidationReport(
372	        n=len(txns),
373	        reconciles=reconciles,
374	        debit_count_ok=debit_count_ok,
375	        credit_count_ok=credit_count_ok,
376	        opening_ok=opening_ok,
377	        closing_ok=closing_ok,
378	    )
379	
380	
381	def write_csv(statement: Statement, out_path: Path | str) -> Path:
382	    """Write parsed transactions to CSV for local QA (the 'export to a sheet' idea).
383	
384	    Writes real data — call only for a local, gitignored destination.
385	    """
386	    out = Path(out_path)
387	    out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
388	    with open(out, "w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
389	        writer = csv.writer(fh)
390	        writer.writerow(
391	            [
392	                "date",
393	                "value_date",
394	                "narration",
395	                "ref",
396	                "withdrawal",
397	                "deposit",
398	                "closing_balance",
399	                "page",
400	            ]
401	        )
402	        for t in statement.transactions:
403	            writer.writerow(
404	                [
405	                    t.date,
406	                    t.value_date or "",
407	                    t.narration,
408	                    t.ref or "",
409	                    f"{t.withdrawal:.2f}",
410	                    f"{t.deposit:.2f}",
411	                    f"{t.closing_balance:.2f}",
412	                    t.page,
413	                ]
414	            )
415	    return out
416	
417	
418	@register_parser
419	class HDFCParser(StatementParser):
420	    """Registered parser for HDFC savings / transaction-account statements."""
421	
422	    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
423	        low = (text or "").lower()
424	        is_hdfc = "hdfc bank" in low or "hdfcbank" in low or "hdfcbank" in (sender or "").lower()
425	        is_statement = "savings account details" in low or (
426	            "narration" in low and "closing balance" in low
427	        )
428	        return is_hdfc and is_statement
429	
430	    def parse_and_insert(
431	        self,
432	        pdf_path: Path,
433	        conn,
434	        doc_id: int,
435	        password: str | None = None,
436	    ) -> int:
437	        """Parse, insert rows into ``hdfc_transactions``, and record parse quality.
438	
439	        Sets ``documents.status`` to 'reconciled' / 'unreconciled' based on the
440	        balance walk (and the bank's SUMMARY when present) so a downstream consumer
441	        can tell a trustworthy parse from a suspect one. Returns the row count.
442	        """
443	        stmt = parse_statement(pdf_path, password=password or "")
444	        for t in stmt.transactions:
445	            dbmod.insert_hdfc_transaction(
446	                conn,
447	                document_id=doc_id,
448	                account_number=stmt.account_number,
449	                txn_date=t.date,
450	                value_date=t.value_date,
451	                particulars=t.narration,
452	                ref=t.ref,
453	                withdrawal=t.withdrawal,
454	                deposit=t.deposit,
455	                closing_balance=t.closing_balance,
456	            )
457	        summary = parse_summary(pdf_path, password=password or "")
458	        ok = (
459	            validate_against_summary(stmt, summary).ok
460	            if summary is not None
461	            else reconcile(stmt).ok
462	        )
463	        conn.execute(
464	            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
465	            ("reconciled" if ok else "unreconciled", doc_id),
466	        )
467	        return len(stmt.transactions)
468

1	from __future__ import annotations

3	import abc
4	import sqlite3

7	
8	def safe_amount(s: str) -> float | None:
9	    """Parse a monetary string to float or None. Shared across bank parsers."""
10	    if not s or s.strip() in ("-", "", "0.00"):
11	        return None
12	    try:
13	        return float(s.replace(",", "").strip())
14	    except ValueError:
15	        return None

18	class StatementParser(abc.ABC):
19	    @abc.abstractmethod
20	    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
21	        """Return True if this parser can handle the given document."""
22	        pass
23	
24	    @abc.abstractmethod
25	    def parse_and_insert(
26	        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
27	    ) -> int:
28	        """Extract txns and insert them into the bank-specific table. Returns count of rows."""
29	        pass

### 27. user (2026-05-31T08:17:57.706Z)

1	"""ICICI Bank statement parser (transaction / savings account).
2	
3	Same trustworthy pattern as the Axis savings parser: row-building is split from the
4	DB insert (pure, unit-testable) and the parse is **reconciled** before we trust it —
5	``documents.status`` records ``reconciled`` / ``unreconciled`` from a running-balance
6	walk, so a silent ``extract_tables()`` row-drop surfaces instead of passing quietly.
7	No ICICI statement is in the corpus yet, so this is synthetic-tested; the
8	``local_statements`` path will validate it against a real one when available.
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import re
14	import sqlite3
15	from dataclasses import dataclass
16	from pathlib import Path
17	
18	import pdfplumber
19	
20	from muneem import db as dbmod
21	
22	from . import register_parser
23	from .base import StatementParser
24	from .validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance
25	
26	_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}")  # ICICI: dd/mm/yyyy
27	_ACCOUNT_RES = [
28	    re.compile(r"Account Number\s*:\s*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
29	    re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\s*[:\s]*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
30	]
31	
32	
33	@dataclass
34	class ICICITxn:
35	    txn_date: str
36	    mode: str
37	    particulars: str
38	    withdrawals: float | None
39	    deposits: float | None
40	    balance: float | None
41	
42	
43	def _first_match(text: str, patterns: list[re.Pattern]) -> str | None:
44	    for rx in patterns:
45	        m = rx.search(text)
46	        if m:
47	            return m.group(1).strip()
48	    return None
49	
50	
51	def _rows_from_icici_tables(tables: list) -> list[ICICITxn]:
52	    """Build transactions from extracted tables. Pure: no PDF, no DB.
53	
54	    ICICI row: [Date, Mode, Particulars, Withdrawals, Deposits, Balance].
55	    Opening/closing-balance and non-transaction rows are skipped.
56	    """
57	    rows: list[ICICITxn] = []
58	    for table in tables or []:
59	        for row in table or []:
60	            if not row or len(row) < 5:
61	                continue
62	            date_str = str(row[0]).strip() if row[0] else ""
63	            low = date_str.lower()
64	            if "opening balance" in low or "closing balance" in low:
65	                continue
66	            if not _DATE_RE.match(date_str):
67	                continue
68	            rows.append(
69	                ICICITxn(
70	                    txn_date=date_str,
71	                    mode=str(row[1]).strip() if len(row) > 1 and row[1] else "",
72	                    particulars=str(row[2]).strip() if len(row) > 2 and row[2] else "",
73	                    withdrawals=parse_amount(row[3]) if len(row) > 3 else None,
74	                    deposits=parse_amount(row[4]) if len(row) > 4 else None,
75	                    balance=parse_amount(row[5]) if len(row) > 5 else None,
76	                )
77	            )
78	    return rows
79	
80	
81	def parse_icici(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[ICICITxn]]:
82	    """Open an ICICI statement and return (account_number, transactions)."""
83	    account_number = "UNKNOWN"
84	    rows: list[ICICITxn] = []
85	    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
86	        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
87	        account_number = _first_match(head, _ACCOUNT_RES) or "UNKNOWN"
88	        for page in pdf.pages:
89	            rows.extend(_rows_from_icici_tables(page.extract_tables()))
90	    return account_number, rows
91	
92	
93	@register_parser
94	class ICICIParser(StatementParser):
95	    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
96	        if sender and "icicibank.com" in sender.lower():
97	            return True
98	        return "ICICI Bank" in text and "Statement of Transactions" in text
99	
100	    def parse_and_insert(
101	        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
102	    ) -> int:
103	        try:
104	            account_number, rows = parse_icici(pdf_path, password)
105	        except Exception as exc:
106	            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse ICICI statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
107	        for t in rows:
108	            dbmod.insert_icici_transaction(
109	                conn,
110	                document_id=doc_id,
111	                account_number=account_number,
112	                txn_date=t.txn_date,
113	                mode=t.mode,
114	                particulars=t.particulars,
115	                withdrawals=t.withdrawals,
116	                deposits=t.deposits,
117	                balance=t.balance,
118	            )
119	        report = reconcile_running_balance([(t.withdrawals, t.deposits, t.balance) for t in rows])
120	        conn.execute(
121	            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
122	            ("reconciled" if report.ok else "unreconciled", doc_id),
123	        )
124	        return len(rows)
125

### 28. user (2026-05-31T08:17:58.175Z)

1	"""Axis Bank statement parsers: savings/transaction account + credit card.
2	
3	The savings parse is split from the DB insert (unit-testable) and validated by a
4	running-balance reconciliation.
5	
6	**STOP-GAP:** ``extract_tables()`` has proven unreliable on *real* Axis savings
7	layouts — column counts and date placement vary between statements (Jun vs Oct
8	2025), so the current mapping can mislabel rows. Until the real fix lands,
9	``AxisParser`` refuses to persist a parse that doesn't reconcile (marks the
10	document ``unreconciled`` and stores 0 rows) rather than save mislabeled data.
11	The real fix is a coordinate-clustering rewrite (HDFC-style), validated by total
12	reconciliation ``opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing``; see
13	docs/CURRENT_STATE.md. Credit-card statements have no running balance, so
14	``AxisCreditCardParser`` can't reconcile (parse/persist split + count only).
15	"""
16	
17	from __future__ import annotations
18	
19	import re
20	import sqlite3
21	from dataclasses import dataclass
22	from pathlib import Path
23	
24	import pdfplumber
25	
26	from muneem import db as dbmod
27	
28	from . import register_parser
29	from .base import StatementParser
30	from .validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance
31	
32	_SAVINGS_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}-\d{2}-\d{4}")  # Axis savings: dd-mm-yyyy
33	_CC_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}")  # Axis credit card: dd/mm/yyyy
34	_ACCOUNT_RES = [
35	    re.compile(r"Account No\s*:\s*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
36	    re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\s*[:\s]*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
37	]
38	_CARD_RES = [re.compile(r"(\d{4}XXXX\d{4})"), re.compile(r"(\d{6}\*+\d{4})")]
39	
40	
41	@dataclass
42	class AxisTxn:
43	    txn_date: str
44	    value_date: str
45	    particulars: str
46	    debit: float | None
47	    credit: float | None
48	    balance: float | None
49	
50	
51	@dataclass
52	class AxisCCTxn:
53	    txn_date: str
54	    particulars: str
55	    merchant_category: str
56	    amount: float
57	    type: str  # "Cr" or "Dr"
58	
59	
60	def _first_match(text: str, patterns: list[re.Pattern]) -> str | None:
61	    for rx in patterns:
62	        m = rx.search(text)
63	        if m:
64	            return m.group(1).strip()
65	    return None
66	
67	
68	def _rows_from_savings_tables(tables: list) -> list[AxisTxn]:
69	    """Build savings transactions from extracted tables. Pure: no PDF, no DB.
70	
71	    Axis savings row: [Tran Date, Value Date, Particulars, Debit, Credit, Balance].
72	    Opening/closing-balance rows and non-transaction rows are skipped.
73	    """
74	    rows: list[AxisTxn] = []
75	    for table in tables or []:
76	        for row in table or []:
77	            if not row or len(row) < 5:
78	                continue
79	            date_str = str(row[0]).strip() if row[0] else ""
80	            low = date_str.lower()
81	            if "opening balance" in low or "closing balance" in low:
82	                continue
83	            if not _SAVINGS_DATE_RE.match(date_str):
84	                continue
85	            rows.append(
86	                AxisTxn(
87	                    txn_date=date_str,
88	                    value_date=str(row[1]).strip() if len(row) > 1 and row[1] else "",
89	                    particulars=str(row[2]).strip() if len(row) > 2 and row[2] else "",
90	                    debit=parse_amount(row[3]) if len(row) > 3 else None,
91	                    credit=parse_amount(row[4]) if len(row) > 4 else None,
92	                    balance=parse_amount(row[5]) if len(row) > 5 else None,
93	                )
94	            )
95	    return rows
96	
97	
98	def _rows_from_cc_tables(tables: list) -> list[AxisCCTxn]:
99	    """Build credit-card transactions from extracted tables. Pure: no PDF, no DB."""
100	    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
101	    for table in tables or []:
102	        for row in table or []:
103	            if not row or len(row) < 9:
104	                continue
105	            date_str = str(row[0]).strip() if row[0] else ""
106	            if not _CC_DATE_RE.match(date_str):
107	                continue
108	            amount_str = str(row[8]).strip() if row[8] else ""
109	            is_cr = "Cr" in amount_str
110	            amount = parse_amount(re.sub(r"[^\d.]", "", amount_str)) or 0.0
111	            rows.append(
112	                AxisCCTxn(
113	                    txn_date=date_str,
114	                    particulars=str(row[2]).strip() if row[2] else "",
115	                    merchant_category=str(row[7]).strip() if row[7] else "",
116	                    amount=amount,
117	                    type="Cr" if is_cr else "Dr",
118	                )
119	            )
120	    return rows
121	
122	
123	def parse_axis_savings(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[AxisTxn]]:
124	    """Open an Axis savings statement and return (account_number, transactions)."""
125	    account_number = "UNKNOWN"
126	    rows: list[AxisTxn] = []
127	    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
128	        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
129	        account_number = _first_match(head, _ACCOUNT_RES) or "UNKNOWN"
130	        for page in pdf.pages:
131	            rows.extend(_rows_from_savings_tables(page.extract_tables()))
132	    return account_number, rows
133	
134	
135	def parse_axis_cc(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[AxisCCTxn]]:
136	    """Open an Axis credit-card statement and return (card_number, transactions)."""
137	    card_number = "UNKNOWN"
138	    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
139	    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
140	        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
141	        card_number = _first_match(head, _CARD_RES) or "UNKNOWN"
142	        for page in pdf.pages:
143	            rows.extend(_rows_from_cc_tables(page.extract_tables()))
144	    return card_number, rows
145	
146	
147	@register_parser
148	class AxisParser(StatementParser):
149	    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
150	        if sender and "axisbank.com" in sender.lower():
151	            return True
152	        return "Axis Bank" in text and "Statement of Account" in text
153	
154	    def parse_and_insert(
155	        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
156	    ) -> int:
157	        try:
158	            account_number, rows = parse_axis_savings(pdf_path, password)
159	        except Exception as exc:
160	            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
161	
162	        # STOP-GAP (pending the coordinate-clustering rewrite): extract_tables() is
163	        # unreliable across real Axis layouts — column counts and date placement vary
164	        # between statements, so the current mapping can mislabel rows. Rather than
165	        # persist data we can't trust, we REFUSE to insert anything that doesn't
166	        # reconcile: mark the document 'unreconciled' and store 0 rows. A correct
167	        # parse (which reconciles) inserts normally. See docs/CURRENT_STATE.md.
168	        report = reconcile_running_balance([(t.debit, t.credit, t.balance) for t in rows])
169	        if not report.ok:
170	            conn.execute("UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?", ("unreconciled", doc_id))
171	            return 0
172	
173	        for t in rows:
174	            dbmod.insert_axis_transaction(
175	                conn,
176	                document_id=doc_id,
177	                account_number=account_number,
178	                txn_date=t.txn_date,
179	                value_date=t.value_date,
180	                particulars=t.particulars,
181	                debit=t.debit,
182	                credit=t.credit,
183	                balance=t.balance,
184	            )
185	        conn.execute("UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?", ("reconciled", doc_id))
186	        return len(rows)
187	
188	
189	@register_parser
190	class AxisCreditCardParser(StatementParser):
191	    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
192	        return "Axis Bank" in text and "Credit Card Statement" in text
193	
194	    def parse_and_insert(
195	        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
196	    ) -> int:
197	        try:
198	            card_number, rows = parse_axis_cc(pdf_path, password)
199	        except Exception as exc:
200	            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis CC statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
201	        for t in rows:
202	            dbmod.insert_axis_cc_transaction(
203	                conn,
204	                document_id=doc_id,
205	                account_number=card_number,
206	                txn_date=t.txn_date,
207	                particulars=t.particulars,
208	                merchant_category=t.merchant_category,
209	                amount=t.amount,
210	                type=t.type,
211	            )
212	        # Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so reconciliation does
213	        # not apply; documents.status is left as set by the caller.
214	        return len(rows)
215

1	from __future__ import annotations
2	
3	from .base import StatementParser, safe_amount
4	
5	_REGISTRY: list[type[StatementParser]] = []
6	
7	
8	def register_parser(parser_cls: type[StatementParser]) -> type[StatementParser]:
9	    """Decorator to register a parser."""
10	    _REGISTRY.append(parser_cls)
11	    return parser_cls
12	
13	
14	def get_parser(
15	    text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None
16	) -> StatementParser | None:
17	    """Return an instantiated parser that can handle this document, or None."""
18	    for parser_cls in _REGISTRY:
19	        parser = parser_cls()
20	        if parser.can_handle(text, sender, subject):
21	            return parser
22	    return None
23	
24	
25	def _autoload_parsers() -> None:
26	    """Import every parser submodule so its ``@register_parser`` runs.
27	
28	    Registration is an import *side-effect*, so the registry is only populated for
29	    modules that have actually been imported. Without this, the production path
30	    (``from muneem.parsers import get_parser``, as cli.py uses) would leave
31	    ``_REGISTRY`` EMPTY — only the tests imported the submodules. Auto-discovery
32	    fixes that and means new parser modules register themselves with no edit here.
33	    """
34	    import importlib
35	    import pkgutil
36	
37	    for info in pkgutil.iter_modules(__path__):
38	        if info.name != "base" and not info.name.startswith("_"):
39	            importlib.import_module(f"{__name__}.{info.name}")
40	
41	
42	_autoload_parsers()
43	
44	__all__ = ["StatementParser", "register_parser", "get_parser", "safe_amount"]
45

1	"""Tests for the shared running-balance reconciler (muneem.parsers.validation)."""
2	
3	from muneem.parsers.validation import reconcile_running_balance
4	
5	
6	def test_reconciles_clean():
7	    entries = [(500.0, 0.0, 9500.0), (0.0, 2000.0, 11500.0), (1500.5, 0.0, 9999.5)]
8	    r = reconcile_running_balance(entries)
9	    assert r.n == 3 and r.compared == 2 and r.breaks == 0 and r.ok
10	
11	
12	def test_detects_break():
13	    entries = [(500.0, 0.0, 9500.0), (0.0, 2000.0, 11500.0), (1500.5, 0.0, 9999.49)]
14	    r = reconcile_running_balance(entries)
15	    assert r.breaks == 1 and r.first_break == 2 and not r.ok
16	
17	
18	def test_none_debit_or_credit_treated_as_zero():
19	    # Axis/ICICI rows: exactly one of (debit, credit) is filled; the other is None.
20	    entries = [(None, None, 1000.0), (250.0, None, 750.0), (None, 1250.0, 2000.0)]
21	    r = reconcile_running_balance(entries)
22	    assert r.compared == 2 and r.breaks == 0 and r.ok
23	
24	
25	def test_a_dropped_balance_surfaces_as_a_gap():
26	    # If a row's balance goes missing (e.g. a silent extract_tables drop), the walk
27	    # across the gap no longer adds up — exactly the failure we want surfaced.
28	    entries = [(None, None, 1000.0), (250.0, None, None), (None, None, 750.0)]
29	    r = reconcile_running_balance(entries)
30	    assert r.breaks == 1 and r.first_break == 2 and not r.ok
31	
32	
33	def test_empty_and_single_are_not_ok():
34	    assert not reconcile_running_balance([]).ok
35	    assert not reconcile_running_balance([(None, None, 100.0)]).ok  # nothing to compare

18	def W(text, x0, top, x1=None):
19	    """A minimal pdfplumber-style word dict."""
20	    return {
21	        "text": text,
22	        "x0": float(x0),
23	        "x1": float(x1 if x1 is not None else x0 + 5 * len(text)),
24	        "top": float(top),
25	        "bottom": float(top) + 8,
26	    }
27	
28	
29	def _sample_words():
30	    """A 3-transaction table whose running balance reconciles, with header, a
31	    per-page summary box above it, wrapped narration, and a footer marker."""
32	    words = []
33	    words += [W("Opening", 37, 50), W("Balance", 72, 50), W("01/02/2026", 52, 60)]
34	    words += [
35	        W("Date", 72, 80),
36	        W("Narration", 175, 80),
37	        W("Withdrawals", 296, 80),
38	        W("Deposits", 386, 80),
39	        W("Closing", 473, 80),
40	        W("Balance", 504, 80),
41	    ]
42	    words += [
43	        W("05/02/2026", 52, 100, 92),
44	        W("UPI-SWIGGY-PAY", 111, 100),
45	        W("500.00", 330, 100, 358),
46	        W("0.00", 428, 100, 443),
47	        W("9,500.00", 519, 100, 561),
48	    ]
49	    words += [W("okhdfcbank-CONTINUED", 111, 109)]
50	    words += [W("04/02/2026", 122, 118, 162), W("123456789012", 180, 118, 255)]
51	    words += [
52	        W("08/02/2026", 52, 140, 92),
53	        W("SALARY-CREDIT", 111, 140),
54	        W("0.00", 340, 140, 358),
55	        W("2,000.00", 415, 140, 443),
56	        W("11,500.00", 515, 140, 561),
57	    ]
58	    words += [
59	        W("10/02/2026", 52, 160, 92),
60	        W("ATM-WDL", 111, 160),
61	        W("1,500.50", 320, 160, 358),
62	        W("0.00", 428, 160, 443),
63	        W("9,999.50", 519, 160, 561),
64	    ]
65	    words += [W("STATEMENT", 200, 180), W("SUMMARY", 260, 180)]
66	    return words

69	def _parse_sample():

77	    )

84	    assert t1.value_date == "04/02/2026"
85	    assert t1.ref == "123456789012"

95	    assert "STATEMENT" not in txns[-1].narration and "SUMMARY" not in txns[-1].narration

103	    assert report.breaks == 1 and report.first_break == 2 and not report.ok

202	def test_real_hdfc_statement_validates(pdf_path, tmp_path):
203	    """Local-only: parse the real statements and require the parse to agree with
204	    HDFC's own SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts + opening/closing) and reconcile fully.
205	
206	    Set MUNEEM_TEST_HDFC_PW; never runs in CI. Surfaces counts/booleans only.
207	    """

209	    if not pw:

### 41. assistant (2026-05-31T08:23:22.062Z)

"""The canonical *concept schema* — one bank-agnostic vocabulary every extractor targets.

This is the **contract** at the heart of the concept-core parser architecture (see
[`docs/parser-architecture.md`](../../../docs/parser-architecture.md) §3): a parser's job is to
*map this bank's columns/fields onto these concepts, then let the verifier check them.* Because the
concepts are layout-independent, a format change becomes "re-map onto the same concepts," not
"rewrite the parser."

These are pure data — no PDF, no DB, no logic — so they import nothing and are safe to use anywhere.
The verifier (``muneem.parsers.validation``) consumes them; bank extractors produce them.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class ConceptRow:
    """One normalized transaction, in the bank-agnostic vocabulary.

    ``debit`` is money out (withdrawal / spend / Dr); ``credit`` is money in (deposit / Cr).
    Exactly one is typically non-``None`` on a real row, but both may be ``None`` for a
    non-amount marker row. ``balance`` is the running account balance *after* this row, or
    ``None`` when the statement carries no per-row balance.

    **Credit cards** (no running balance) map onto the same row: put the charge in ``debit`` or
    the payment/refund in ``credit`` according to the printed Dr/Cr direction, and leave
    ``balance=None``. That keeps one row type for the whole ladder; total reconciliation (not the
    per-row walk) is what validates a balance-less statement.
    """

    txn_date: str
    narration: str
    debit: float | None = None
    credit: float | None = None
    balance: float | None = None
    value_date: str | None = None
    reference: str | None = None


@dataclass
class StatementConcepts:
    """Statement-level concepts — the figures a bank prints in its own SUMMARY / header.

    Every field is optional: ``None`` means "this statement didn't expose it," which the verifier
    treats as *not checkable* (never as a failure). ``debit_amount`` / ``credit_amount`` are the
    bank's own printed totals; they are recorded here for completeness but are not yet asserted by
    :func:`muneem.parsers.validation.verify` (the current bookend checks counts + opening/closing,
    matching the established HDFC behavior).
    """

    opening_balance: float | None = None
    closing_balance: float | None = None
    debit_count: int | None = None
    credit_count: int | None = None
    debit_amount: float | None = None
    credit_amount: float | None = None
    account_number: str | None = None
    period_start: str | None = None
    period_end: str | None = None
    currency: str | None = None


__all__ = ["ConceptRow", "StatementConcepts"]
