### 44. assistant (2026-05-31T08:24:20.451Z)

"""The **verifier** — reconciliation as a layout-independent truth oracle.

Reconciliation proves a parse is **complete and correct without inspecting any sensitive value**:
if a transaction is missed or misread, the arithmetic stops adding up. That is what lets us trust a
parse of a statement we never eyeball, and (later) what makes a probabilistic extractor safe on
money — *extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes* (see
[`docs/parser-architecture.md`](../../../docs/parser-architecture.md) §2, §5).

This module grew from a single running-balance walk into the verifier the architecture calls for:

* **per-row walk** — ``reconcile_running_balance``: ``balance[i] == balance[i-1] − debit + credit``.
* **total reconcile** — ``reconcile_total``: ``opening + Σcredit − Σdebit == closing`` (works even
  with no per-row balance, e.g. Axis savings).
* **bookend** — ``verify``: parsed Dr/Cr counts + opening/closing vs the bank's printed SUMMARY.
* **selection** — ``select_reconciling``: among candidate concept mappings, pick the one arithmetic
  endorses (the seam the L2 mapping search plugs into).
* **confidence** — ``Confidence`` + ``BookendReport.confidence``: a recorded verdict, not an
  assumption.

Reports carry counts / booleans / a verdict only — **never an amount**.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum

from .concepts import ConceptRow, StatementConcepts

_CR_DR_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"(?i)\s*(cr|dr)\s*$")


def parse_amount(value: object) -> float | None:
    """Parse a statement money cell to a float.

    Returns ``None`` only for a genuinely absent cell (None / empty / ``"-"`` /
    non-numeric); ``"0.00"`` parses to ``0.0``. This deliberately distinguishes
    *absent* from *zero* — unlike ``base.safe_amount``, which maps ``"0.00"`` to
    ``None`` and would corrupt a real zero balance and the reconciliation math.
    """
    if value is None:
        return None
    text = _CR_DR_SUFFIX.sub("", str(value).strip()).replace(",", "").strip()
    if text in ("", "-"):
        return None
    try:
        return float(text)
    except ValueError:
        return None


@dataclass
class ReconcileReport:
    """Result of a running-balance walk. Counts only, never an amount."""

    n: int  # rows considered
    compared: int  # consecutive (balance-bearing) pairs actually checked
    breaks: int
    first_break: int | None = None

    @property
    def ok(self) -> bool:
        return self.compared > 0 and self.breaks == 0


def reconcile_running_balance(
    entries: Sequence[tuple[float | None, float | None, float | None]],
    tol: float = 0.01,
) -> ReconcileReport:
    """Verify ``balance[i] == balance[i-1] - debit[i] + credit[i]`` across rows.

    ``entries`` is an ordered list of ``(debit, credit, balance)``; ``debit`` and
    ``credit`` may be ``None`` (treated as 0.0). A row whose ``balance`` is ``None``
    is skipped (can't anchor on it). Because the walk spans the whole statement, a
    clean result also rules out any row missed *between* two balances — which is the
    failure mode silent ``extract_tables()`` row-drops would otherwise hide.
    """
    breaks = 0
    first_break: int | None = None
    compared = 0
    prev: float | None = None
    for i, (debit, credit, balance) in enumerate(entries):
        if balance is None:
            continue
        if prev is not None:
            expected = prev - (debit or 0.0) + (credit or 0.0)
            compared += 1
            if abs(expected - balance) > tol:
                breaks += 1
                if first_break is None:
                    first_break = i
        prev = balance
    return ReconcileReport(
        n=len(entries), compared=compared, breaks=breaks, first_break=first_break
    )


def _row_entries(
    rows: Sequence[ConceptRow],
) -> list[tuple[float | None, float | None, float | None]]:
    """Project concept rows onto the ``(debit, credit, balance)`` tuples the walk consumes."""
    return [(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]


def reconcile_total(
    rows: Sequence[ConceptRow], opening: float, closing: float, tol: float = 0.01
) -> bool:
    """Verify the layout-independent identity ``opening + Σcredit − Σdebit == closing``.

    This is the oracle for statements with **no reliable per-row balance** (e.g. Axis savings):
    it checks the whole statement against its own opening/closing bookends without needing each
    row's running balance. ``opening`` and ``closing`` come from the bank's SUMMARY/header
    (``StatementConcepts``); the caller must have them.
    """
    delta = sum(r.credit or 0.0 for r in rows) - sum(r.debit or 0.0 for r in rows)
    return abs((opening + delta) - closing) <= tol


class Confidence(str, Enum):
    """Recorded trust verdict for a parsed statement (mirrors ``documents.status`` values).

    ``RECONCILED`` — the parse passed every available check, so it is trusted. ``UNRECONCILED`` —
    a check failed (or none could run); the parse is suspect and is quarantined rather than trusted.
    """

    RECONCILED = "reconciled"
    UNRECONCILED = "unreconciled"


@dataclass
class BookendReport:
    """A parse cross-checked against the bank's own SUMMARY. Booleans + counts only.

    ``None`` for a check means the figure wasn't available to verify (not a failure). ``ok``
    requires the per-row balance walk *plus* every available check to pass.
    """

    n: int
    reconciles: bool
    debit_count_ok: bool | None = None
    credit_count_ok: bool | None = None
    opening_ok: bool | None = None
    closing_ok: bool | None = None

    @property
    def ok(self) -> bool:
        checks = [self.debit_count_ok, self.credit_count_ok, self.opening_ok, self.closing_ok]
        return self.n > 0 and self.reconciles and all(c is True for c in checks)

    @property
    def confidence(self) -> Confidence:
        return Confidence.RECONCILED if self.ok else Confidence.UNRECONCILED


def verify(
    rows: Sequence[ConceptRow], concepts: StatementConcepts, tol: float = 0.01
) -> BookendReport:
    """Bookend a parse against the bank's printed SUMMARY: reconciliation + the printed figures.

    The per-row walk catches any mid-statement error/omission; the Dr/Cr counts catch any
    extra/missing row; the opening & closing balances catch a missing first/last row. Each printed
    figure is only checked when present (``None`` → not checked). The opening balance is checked by
    implication (``balance[0] − credit[0] + debit[0]`` should equal the printed opening).
    """
    reconciles = reconcile_running_balance(_row_entries(rows), tol).ok
    debit_count_ok = credit_count_ok = opening_ok = closing_ok = None
    if concepts.debit_count is not None:
        debit_count_ok = sum(1 for r in rows if (r.debit or 0.0) > 0) == concepts.debit_count
    if concepts.credit_count is not None:
        credit_count_ok = sum(1 for r in rows if (r.credit or 0.0) > 0) == concepts.credit_count
    if rows and rows[-1].balance is not None and concepts.closing_balance is not None:
        closing_ok = abs(rows[-1].balance - concepts.closing_balance) <= tol
    if rows and rows[0].balance is not None and concepts.opening_balance is not None:
        implied_opening = rows[0].balance - (rows[0].credit or 0.0) + (rows[0].debit or 0.0)
        opening_ok = abs(implied_opening - concepts.opening_balance) <= tol
    return BookendReport(
        n=len(rows),
        reconciles=reconciles,
        debit_count_ok=debit_count_ok,
        credit_count_ok=credit_count_ok,
        opening_ok=opening_ok,
        closing_ok=closing_ok,
    )


def select_reconciling(candidates: Sequence[Sequence[ConceptRow]], tol: float = 0.01) -> int | None:
    """Among candidate concept-row mappings, return the index of the first that reconciles.

    This is the *selection* half of "extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes": when the
    extraction ladder can't be sure which column→concept assignment is right, it proposes several
    and lets the per-row walk pick the correct one. Returns ``None`` if none reconcile.

    The candidate *generation* (the L2 mapping search) is Step 2 of the architecture plan; this is
    the verifier-side seam it plugs into. Selection here uses the per-row walk, so candidates need a
    running balance — balance-less statements select via :func:`reconcile_total` at the call site.
    """
    for i, rows in enumerate(candidates):
        if reconcile_running_balance(_row_entries(rows), tol).ok:
            return i
    return None


__all__ = [
    "parse_amount",
    "ReconcileReport",
    "reconcile_running_balance",
    "reconcile_total",
    "Confidence",
    "BookendReport",
    "verify",
    "select_reconciling",
]

### 90. assistant (2026-05-31T08:32:39.148Z)

Done — **Step 1 is shipped.** [muneem#22](https://github.com/avidullu/muneem/pull/22) is squash-merged to `main`, branch cleaned up, offline suite green on the merged commit (**101 passed**, ruff clean).

## What's now in place
The concept-core contract that the rest of the parser rework hangs off:
- **`concepts.py`** — the bank-agnostic vocabulary (`ConceptRow`, `StatementConcepts`) every extractor will target.
- **`validation.py` is now the verifier** — per-row walk + `reconcile_total` + the concept-level `verify` bookend + `select_reconciling` (the L2 seam) + a recorded `Confidence` verdict.
- **HDFC is the reference implementation** — maps its rows to concepts and verifies through the shared verifier. Proven no-behavior-change: every prior test passes verbatim, +7 new ones cover the new capabilities.

Parked exactly as agreed: per-bank storage, ICICI/Axis, CLI/quarantine policy, L3/VLM — none touched.

## Next steps (the plan's remaining sequence)
Following [`parser-architecture.md`](docs/parser-architecture.md) §10:

1. **Step 2 — concept-mapping engine (L0/L1/L2)** as generic, bank-agnostic code, then express **HDFC + ICICI as profiles (data)** on top of it. This is where "bank knowledge becomes data, not code" gets proven.
2. **Step 3 — the Axis must-fix** 🎯: coordinate-clustering rewrite onto the core, validated by total reconcile on the Jun + Oct 2025 statements. This is the headline payoff — it kills the stop-gap and is the first real test of generic-first.
3. **Parallel, anytime** — the **local-VLM deep-research run** (still pending) that populates L3 + the numeric-hallucination traps for Step 4. Non-sensitive, doesn't block Steps 2–3.

My recommendation is to **continue to Step 2** next (it's the prerequisite that makes the Step 3 Axis rewrite a profile + generic-path exercise rather than another bespoke parser). Want me to pick up Step 2, jump straight to the Axis must-fix (Step 3), or kick off the local-VLM research in parallel first?

### 93. assistant (2026-05-31T08:34:06.282Z)

---
name: scope-all-personal-finance
description: muneem's scope is the user's ENTIRE financial life, not just banks; investment-statement parsing + instrument-event classification are core near-term goals, not stretch backlog.
metadata:
  type: project
---

A *muneem* is a bookkeeper/accountant — conceptually it handles **all** of the user's finances. **Bank statements are only the starting point / first vertical slice, not the boundary of scope.** Squarely in scope (the user's words: "well within scope," not hypothetical): parsing statements for **equities, mutual funds, ETFs, government & private bonds**, and similar instruments. **Tier-2 transaction classification** is a **near-term** goal — planned for **after the current concept-core parser phase** (the step 1–3/5 rework begun 2026-05) — and must classify **instrument events**: stock / MF / ETF **buy / sell**, **dividend**, **fees**, etc.

**Why:** the repo (DESIGN/ROADMAP) currently frames brokers/MF/holdings as a *post-checkpoint "generalize" backlog* item; the user is elevating it to **conceptually central and near-term**. This sets the altitude for design decisions made *now*.

**How to apply:** don't over-fit the concept core to bank-statement rows (`txn_date/debit/credit/balance`). Keep a clear path open for a **second concept family** — instrument, ISIN, quantity, unit price, event-type, holdings snapshots — with **its own reconciliation oracle** (e.g. units/holdings continuity, cost-basis math) distinct from the bank running-balance walk. The "extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes" principle ([[parser-validation-pattern]], parser-architecture.md) generalizes, but the *oracle* differs per family. Treat broker/MF/ETF/bond parsing as planned milestones, not "maybe later." Per-bank raw tables stay for now; storage unification is deferred (parser-architecture.md step 5).

17	def test_can_handle():
18	    parser = ICICIParser()
19	    assert parser.can_handle("Some text", sender="statements@icicibank.com")
20	    assert parser.can_handle("ICICI Bank Statement of Transactions")
21	    assert not parser.can_handle("HDFC Bank")
22	
23	
24	def test_rows_from_icici_tables_skips_non_txn_rows():
25	    tables = [
26	        [
27	            ["Date", "Mode", "Particulars", "Withdrawals", "Deposits", "Balance"],  # header
28	            ["OPENING BALANCE", "", "", "", "", "1,000.00"],  # explicit skip
29	            ["12/05/2023", "UPI", "Amazon", "250.00", "", "750.00"],
30	            ["13/05/2023", "NEFT", "Salary", "", "1,250.00", "2,000.00"],
31	            ["bad", "x", "y", "", "", ""],  # not a date -> skipped
32	        ]
33	    ]
34	    rows = icici._rows_from_icici_tables(tables)
35	    assert len(rows) == 2
36	    assert rows[0].withdrawals == 250.0 and rows[0].deposits is None and rows[0].balance == 750.0
37	    assert rows[1].deposits == 1250.0 and rows[1].balance == 2000.0
38	    report = reconcile_running_balance([(r.withdrawals, r.deposits, r.balance) for r in rows])
39	    assert report.ok and report.compared == 1  # 750 - 0 + 1250 == 2000

50	        ]

### 118. assistant (2026-05-31T08:45:55.578Z)

"""The generic concept-mapping engine — columns in, concepts out, arithmetic decides.

This is the *generic-first* half of the concept-core architecture (see
[`docs/parser-architecture.md`](../../../docs/parser-architecture.md) §4, §6): bank-agnostic code that
takes a **cell-matrix** (the shape `pdfplumber.extract_tables()` returns, or what a coordinate-
clustering extractor emits) and maps its columns onto the canonical concepts via a cheap-first
ladder, with a profile supplying priors:

* **L0 — header lexicon.** Match header-row labels against the profile's concept↔label aliases.
* **profile-order.** If there is no header, fall back to the profile's known column order (a prior).
* **L1/L2 — content + search.** Classify columns by content (dates, money), then enumerate the
  plausible amount-column → {debit, credit, balance} assignments and **keep the one the reconciliation
  oracle endorses** — the self-identifying balance column, found by *which mapping makes the math work*
  rather than by its label.

The engine is **family-agnostic**: it is parameterized by a :class:`FamilySpec` (the target concept
names, which are date/amount/balance, and a typed-row builder) and an injected `oracle`
(`rows -> bool`). Only the *bank* family ships today; an instrument family (equity/MF/ETF/bond — see
the project scope) would reuse this control flow with its own concepts + oracle. Nothing here logs a
value; provenance is the winning layer + column map.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import itertools
import re
from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from .concepts import ConceptRow
from .validation import parse_amount

Cell = object  # a table cell: str | None (we coerce defensively)
Row = Sequence[Cell]

# Generic Indian-statement date shapes; a profile can pin its own to avoid false positives.
_GENERIC_DATE_PATTERNS: tuple[re.Pattern, ...] = (
    re.compile(r"^\d{2}[/-]\d{2}[/-]\d{4}$"),  # dd/mm/yyyy or dd-mm-yyyy
    re.compile(r"^\d{2}[- ][A-Za-z]{3}[- ]\d{2,4}$"),  # dd-Mon-yyyy / dd Mon yy
)
# "Money" requires a decimal point so bare integers (e.g. a long reference number) are NOT mistaken
# for an amount column during content classification.
_MONEY_RE = re.compile(r"(?i)^-?[\d,]+\.\d{1,2}(?:\s*(?:cr|dr))?$")
_BALANCE_MARKERS = ("opening balance", "closing balance", "balance b/f", "balance c/f")

# The generic bank lexicon. Profiles extend/override per issuer; keys are concept names, values are
# lower-cased header labels matched exactly (not by substring) during L0.
_BANK_ALIASES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "txn_date": ("date", "txn date", "tran date", "transaction date", "posting date"),
    "value_date": ("value date", "val date", "value dt"),
    "narration": (
        "narration", "particulars", "description", "transaction details", "details",
        "remarks", "transaction", "transaction remarks",
    ),
    "reference": (
        "ref", "reference", "ref no", "reference no", "cheque no", "chq no", "instrument no",
    ),
    "debit": ("debit", "withdrawal", "withdrawals", "dr", "paid out", "debit amount"),
    "credit": ("credit", "deposit", "deposits", "cr", "paid in", "credit amount"),
    "balance": ("balance", "closing balance", "running balance", "closing bal", "available balance"),
}


def _text(cell: Cell) -> str:
    return "" if cell is None else str(cell).strip()


def _norm(label: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", label.strip().lower())


def _matches_date(text: str, patterns: Sequence[re.Pattern]) -> bool:
    return any(p.match(text) for p in patterns)


def _looks_like_money(text: str) -> bool:
    return bool(_MONEY_RE.match(text))


@dataclass
class FamilySpec:
    """A concept *family*: the target vocabulary + how to build typed rows from a column mapping.

    The engine's L0/L1/L2 logic is written against this spec rather than against bank concepts, so a
    future instrument family (with its own concepts + reconciliation oracle) reuses the same engine.
    ``build`` turns per-row ``{concept: raw_cell}`` dicts into typed rows the oracle understands.
    """

    name: str
    concepts: tuple[str, ...]
    date_concepts: tuple[str, ...]
    amount_concepts: tuple[str, ...]
    balance_concept: str | None
    default_aliases: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]
    build: Callable[[list[dict[str, str]]], list]


def _build_bank_rows(mapped: list[dict[str, str]]) -> list[ConceptRow]:
    """Turn mapped cells into :class:`ConceptRow`s (amounts parsed; absent stays ``None``)."""
    rows: list[ConceptRow] = []
    for m in mapped:
        rows.append(
            ConceptRow(
                txn_date=_text(m.get("txn_date")),
                narration=_text(m.get("narration")),
                debit=parse_amount(m.get("debit")),
                credit=parse_amount(m.get("credit")),
                balance=parse_amount(m.get("balance")),
                value_date=_text(m.get("value_date")) or None,
                reference=_text(m.get("reference")) or None,
            )
        )
    return rows


BANK_FAMILY = FamilySpec(
    name="bank",
    concepts=("txn_date", "value_date", "narration", "reference", "debit", "credit", "balance"),
    date_concepts=("txn_date", "value_date"),
    amount_concepts=("debit", "credit", "balance"),
    balance_concept="balance",
    default_aliases=_BANK_ALIASES,
    build=_build_bank_rows,
)


@dataclass
class MappingResult:
    """The outcome of mapping one table: typed rows + how we got there (provenance)."""

    rows: list  # typed rows from family.build (e.g. list[ConceptRow])
    mapped: list[dict[str, str]]  # per-row {concept: raw cell}, incl. profile extras (e.g. ICICI mode)
    column_map: dict[str, int]  # the winning concept -> column-index assignment
    layer: str  # "L0" / "profile-order" / "L2" — which rung produced it
    verified: bool  # did the oracle endorse it? (False if no oracle was supplied)


@dataclass
class Profile:
    """Declarative per-issuer knowledge — *data, not code* (architecture §6).

    Generic detectors run first; the profile supplies priors: extra concept↔label aliases, the
    accepted date shape, a positional fallback when there's no header, table anchors, account-number
    patterns, quirks, and (placeholder, wiring parked) the password rule.
    """

    name: str
    aliases: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = field(default_factory=dict)
    date_patterns: tuple[re.Pattern, ...] = ()
    column_order: tuple[str, ...] | None = None
    table_anchors: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    account_patterns: tuple[re.Pattern, ...] = ()
    quirks: frozenset[str] = frozenset()
    password_rule: str | None = None  # data only; sourcing is parked (DESIGN §8.2)

    def first_account(self, text: str) -> str | None:
        for rx in self.account_patterns:
            m = rx.search(text)
            if m:
                return m.group(1).strip()
        return None


def _alias_index(profile: Profile, family: FamilySpec) -> dict[str, str]:
    """Build ``normalized-label -> concept``. Profile entries win over family defaults on conflict."""
    index: dict[str, str] = {}
    for concept, labels in family.default_aliases.items():
        for label in labels:
            index[_norm(label)] = concept
    for concept, labels in profile.aliases.items():
        for label in labels:
            index[_norm(label)] = concept
    return index


def _date_patterns(profile: Profile) -> tuple[re.Pattern, ...]:
    return profile.date_patterns or _GENERIC_DATE_PATTERNS


def _detect_header(rows: list[list[Cell]], alias_index: dict[str, str]) -> tuple[int | None, dict[str, int]]:
    """Find the header row (most exact alias hits, >=2) and its concept->column map."""
    best_idx: int | None = None
    best_map: dict[str, int] = {}
    for i, row in enumerate(rows):
        cmap: dict[str, int] = {}
        for col, cell in enumerate(row):
            concept = alias_index.get(_norm(_text(cell)))
            if concept and concept not in cmap:
                cmap[concept] = col
        if len(cmap) > len(best_map):
            best_map, best_idx = cmap, i
    if len(best_map) < 2:
        return None, {}
    return best_idx, best_map


def _is_balance_marker(row: Row) -> bool:
    return any(any(mk in _norm(_text(c)) for mk in _BALANCE_MARKERS) for c in row)


def _is_transaction_row(row: Row, date_col: int | None, patterns: Sequence[re.Pattern]) -> bool:
    if date_col is not None:
        cell = row[date_col] if date_col < len(row) else None
        return _matches_date(_text(cell), patterns)
    return any(_matches_date(_text(c), patterns) for c in row)


def _classify_columns(
    data: list[list[Cell]], ncols: int, patterns: Sequence[re.Pattern]
) -> dict[int, str]:
    """Tag each column date / amount / text / empty by majority content."""
    kinds: dict[int, str] = {}
    for c in range(ncols):
        cells = [_text(r[c]) for r in data if c < len(r) and _text(r[c])]
        if not cells:
            kinds[c] = "empty"
            continue
        thresh = max(1, int(len(cells) * 0.6))
        if sum(1 for x in cells if _matches_date(x, patterns)) >= thresh:
            kinds[c] = "date"
        elif sum(1 for x in cells if _looks_like_money(x)) >= thresh:
            kinds[c] = "amount"
        else:
            kinds[c] = "text"
    return kinds


def _base_map(col_kinds: dict[int, str], data: list[list[Cell]]) -> dict[str, int]:
    """Date + narration columns from content (the non-ambiguous part of an L1 mapping)."""
    base: dict[str, int] = {}
    date_cols = [c for c, k in sorted(col_kinds.items()) if k == "date"]
    text_cols = [c for c, k in sorted(col_kinds.items()) if k == "text"]
    if date_cols:
        base["txn_date"] = date_cols[0]
    if len(date_cols) > 1:
        base["value_date"] = date_cols[1]
    if text_cols:
        base["narration"] = max(
            text_cols, key=lambda c: sum(len(_text(r[c])) for r in data if c < len(r))
        )
    return base


def _search_candidates(
    col_kinds: dict[int, str], data: list[list[Cell]], family: FamilySpec
) -> list[tuple[str, dict[str, int]]]:
    """Enumerate plausible amount-column assignments (the L2 search space)."""
    base = _base_map(col_kinds, data)
    amount_cols = [c for c, k in sorted(col_kinds.items()) if k == "amount"]
    cands: list[dict[str, int]] = []
    if family.balance_concept and len(amount_cols) >= 3:
        for bal in amount_cols:
            rest = [c for c in amount_cols if c != bal]
            for d, cr in itertools.permutations(rest, 2):
                cands.append({**base, "debit": d, "credit": cr, family.balance_concept: bal})
    if len(amount_cols) >= 2:  # no per-row balance (total-reconcile families, e.g. Axis Oct)
        for d, cr in itertools.permutations(amount_cols, 2):
            cands.append({**base, "debit": d, "credit": cr})
    return [("L2", c) for c in cands[:24]]


def _apply_map(data: list[list[Cell]], col_map: dict[str, int]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    out: list[dict[str, str]] = []
    for row in data:
        out.append({c: (_text(row[i]) if i < len(row) else "") for c, i in col_map.items()})
    return out


def map_table(
    table: list[Row] | None,
    profile: Profile,
    family: FamilySpec = BANK_FAMILY,
    *,
    oracle: Callable[[list], bool] | None = None,
) -> MappingResult | None:
    """Map one extracted table onto ``family``'s concepts; ``oracle`` selects among candidates.

    Resolution order: header lexicon (L0) → profile column order → content search (L2). With an
    ``oracle`` the first candidate it endorses wins (``verified=True``); without one, the first
    structurally-complete candidate is returned unverified (the caller reconciles separately).
    Returns ``None`` if no transaction rows are found, or the best unverified candidate if an oracle
    was supplied but nothing reconciled.
    """
    if not table:
        return None
    rows_in = [list(r) for r in table if r is not None]
    if not rows_in:
        return None
    alias_index = _alias_index(profile, family)
    patterns = _date_patterns(profile)

    header_idx, col_map_hdr = _detect_header(rows_in, alias_index)
    start = header_idx + 1 if header_idx is not None else 0
    date_col_hint = col_map_hdr.get("txn_date")
    if date_col_hint is None and profile.column_order and "txn_date" in profile.column_order:
        date_col_hint = profile.column_order.index("txn_date")

    data = [
        r
        for r in rows_in[start:]
        if not _is_balance_marker(r) and _is_transaction_row(r, date_col_hint, patterns)
    ]
    if not data:
        return None
    ncols = max(len(r) for r in data)

    candidates: list[tuple[str, dict[str, int]]] = []
    if "debit" in col_map_hdr and "credit" in col_map_hdr:  # L0: header pinned the amounts
        col_kinds = _classify_columns(data, ncols, patterns)
        candidates.append(("L0", {**_base_map(col_kinds, data), **col_map_hdr}))
    elif col_map_hdr:  # header present but didn't pin amounts: keep its hints, search the rest
        candidates.append(("L0", col_map_hdr))
    if profile.column_order:  # positional prior (no/partial header)
        candidates.append(("profile-order", {c: i for i, c in enumerate(profile.column_order)}))
    candidates += _search_candidates(_classify_columns(data, ncols, patterns), data, family)

    best: MappingResult | None = None
    for layer, cmap in candidates:
        if "txn_date" not in cmap or not ({"debit", "credit", "balance"} & cmap.keys()):
            continue
        mapped = _apply_map(data, cmap)
        typed = family.build(mapped)
        if oracle is None:
            return MappingResult(typed, mapped, cmap, layer, verified=False)
        if oracle(typed):
            return MappingResult(typed, mapped, cmap, layer, verified=True)
        if best is None:
            best = MappingResult(typed, mapped, cmap, layer, verified=False)
    return best


__all__ = ["Profile", "FamilySpec", "BANK_FAMILY", "MappingResult", "map_table"]

### 121. assistant (2026-05-31T08:46:34.818Z)

"""Per-issuer profiles — bank knowledge expressed as **data, not code** (architecture §6).

A profile carries the priors the generic engine needs to disambiguate one issuer: extra
concept↔label aliases, the accepted date shape, a positional fallback when there's no header,
table anchors, account-number patterns, quirks, and (parked) the password rule. Adding an issuer is
ideally *adding a profile*, not writing a parser.

`ICICI_PROFILE` drives ICICI fully through `engine.map_table`. `HDFC_PROFILE` is data consumed by
HDFC's parser, whose *extraction* stays bespoke coordinate-clustering — the documented exception
where `extract_tables()` fails on a semi-ruled table (see the `hdfc-parser-tech-decision` memory).
The Axis profile lands with the Step 3 coordinate-clustering rewrite.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from .engine import Profile

ICICI_PROFILE = Profile(
    name="icici",
    aliases={
        "narration": ("particulars",),
        "debit": ("withdrawals",),
        "credit": ("deposits",),
        "mode": ("mode",),  # ICICI-specific extra (UPI/NEFT/...); rides along in MappingResult.mapped
    },
    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
    # Known ICICI layout, used when a table has no detectable header row.
    column_order=("txn_date", "mode", "narration", "debit", "credit", "balance"),
    account_patterns=(
        re.compile(r"Account Number\s*:\s*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
        re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\s*[:\s]*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
    ),
)

HDFC_PROFILE = Profile(
    name="hdfc",
    aliases={
        "debit": ("withdrawal", "withdrawals"),
        "credit": ("deposit", "deposits"),
        "balance": ("closing balance",),
    },
    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
    table_anchors=("Narration", "Closing Balance"),
    account_patterns=(re.compile(r"Account\s*Number\s*[:.]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),),
    # HDFC's transaction table is semi-ruled; extract_tables() is unreliable on it, so HDFC extracts
    # by word-position clustering rather than through the generic cell-matrix engine.
    quirks=frozenset({"semi-ruled-table", "coordinate-clustering"}),
)

__all__ = ["ICICI_PROFILE", "HDFC_PROFILE"]

### 124. assistant (2026-05-31T08:47:24.795Z)

"""ICICI Bank statement parser (transaction / savings account).

Row-building runs through the **generic concept-mapping engine** (``muneem.parsers.engine``) driven
by ``ICICI_PROFILE`` — the engine maps each extracted table's columns onto the canonical concepts
(header lexicon, else ICICI's known column order), so the parser is now mostly *data*. Parsing is
still split from the DB insert and the parse is **reconciled** before we trust it
(``documents.status`` records ``reconciled`` / ``unreconciled`` from a running-balance walk), so a
silent ``extract_tables()`` row-drop surfaces instead of passing quietly. No ICICI statement is in
the corpus yet, so this is synthetic-tested; the ``local_statements`` path will validate it against a
real one when available.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

import pdfplumber

from muneem import db as dbmod

from . import register_parser
from .base import StatementParser
from .engine import map_table
from .profiles import ICICI_PROFILE
from .validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance


@dataclass
class ICICITxn:
    txn_date: str
    mode: str
    particulars: str
    withdrawals: float | None
    deposits: float | None
    balance: float | None


def _rows_from_icici_tables(tables: list) -> list[ICICITxn]:
    """Build transactions from extracted tables via the generic engine. Pure: no PDF, no DB.

    The engine maps columns onto the canonical concepts; ICICI's ``mode`` column rides along in the
    mapping. Reconciliation happens in :meth:`ICICIParser.parse_and_insert`, so this stays
    oracle-free (pure row-building). Opening/closing-balance and non-transaction rows are dropped by
    the engine (no date / balance marker).
    """
    rows: list[ICICITxn] = []
    for table in tables or []:
        result = map_table(table, ICICI_PROFILE)
        if result is None:
            continue
        for m in result.mapped:
            rows.append(
                ICICITxn(
                    txn_date=m.get("txn_date", ""),
                    mode=m.get("mode", ""),
                    particulars=m.get("narration", ""),
                    withdrawals=parse_amount(m.get("debit")),
                    deposits=parse_amount(m.get("credit")),
                    balance=parse_amount(m.get("balance")),
                )
            )
    return rows


def parse_icici(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[ICICITxn]]:
    """Open an ICICI statement and return (account_number, transactions)."""
    account_number = "UNKNOWN"
    rows: list[ICICITxn] = []
    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
        account_number = ICICI_PROFILE.first_account(head) or "UNKNOWN"
        for page in pdf.pages:
            rows.extend(_rows_from_icici_tables(page.extract_tables()))
    return account_number, rows


@register_parser
class ICICIParser(StatementParser):
    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
        if sender and "icicibank.com" in sender.lower():
            return True
        return "ICICI Bank" in text and "Statement of Transactions" in text

    def parse_and_insert(
        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
    ) -> int:
        try:
            account_number, rows = parse_icici(pdf_path, password)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse ICICI statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
        for t in rows:
            dbmod.insert_icici_transaction(
                conn,
                document_id=doc_id,
                account_number=account_number,
                txn_date=t.txn_date,
                mode=t.mode,
                particulars=t.particulars,
                withdrawals=t.withdrawals,
                deposits=t.deposits,
                balance=t.balance,
            )
        report = reconcile_running_balance([(t.withdrawals, t.deposits, t.balance) for t in rows])
        conn.execute(
            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
            ("reconciled" if report.ok else "unreconciled", doc_id),
        )
        return len(rows)

### 149. assistant (2026-05-31T08:53:21.272Z)

"""Tests for the generic concept-mapping engine (muneem.parsers.engine).

Exercises the ladder on synthetic cell-matrices (no PDF/DB): L0 header lexicon, the profile-order
fallback, L1/L2 content search with the oracle picking the self-identifying balance column, and a
deliberately non-bank ``FamilySpec`` to prove the engine is family-agnostic.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from muneem.parsers.engine import BANK_FAMILY, FamilySpec, Profile, map_table
from muneem.parsers.validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance

DMY = (re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),)
PROFILE = Profile(name="t", date_patterns=DMY)


def _walk(rows):
    return reconcile_running_balance([(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]).ok


# --- L0: header lexicon (generic labels, no custom aliases needed) ----------------------------


def test_l0_header_lexicon_maps_generic_labels():
    table = [
        ["Date", "Particulars", "Withdrawals", "Deposits", "Balance"],
        ["01/01/2026", "Opening Balance", "", "", "1000.00"],  # marker -> skipped
        ["02/01/2026", "UPI Amazon", "250.00", "", "750.00"],
        ["03/01/2026", "Salary", "", "1250.00", "2000.00"],
    ]
    r = map_table(table, PROFILE, BANK_FAMILY)
    assert r is not None and r.layer == "L0"
    assert [row.txn_date for row in r.rows] == ["02/01/2026", "03/01/2026"]
    assert r.rows[0].debit == 250.0 and r.rows[0].balance == 750.0
    assert r.rows[1].credit == 1250.0 and r.rows[1].balance == 2000.0
    assert r.column_map["balance"] == 4


def test_l1_detects_value_date_as_second_date_column():
    table = [
        ["Tran Date", "Value Date", "Particulars", "Debit", "Credit", "Balance"],
        ["02/01/2026", "01/01/2026", "UPI", "250.00", "", "750.00"],
        ["03/01/2026", "02/01/2026", "Salary", "", "1250.00", "2000.00"],
    ]
    r = map_table(table, PROFILE, BANK_FAMILY)
    assert r is not None
    assert r.column_map["txn_date"] == 0 and r.column_map["value_date"] == 1
    assert r.rows[0].value_date == "01/01/2026"


# --- profile-order: positional prior when there is no header ----------------------------------


def test_profile_order_fallback_when_no_header():
    p = Profile(
        name="t",
        date_patterns=DMY,
        column_order=("txn_date", "narration", "debit", "credit", "balance"),
    )
    table = [
        ["02/01/2026", "UPI", "250.00", "", "750.00"],
        ["03/01/2026", "Salary", "", "1250.00", "2000.00"],
    ]
    r = map_table(table, p, BANK_FAMILY)
    assert r is not None and r.layer == "profile-order"
    assert r.rows[0].debit == 250.0 and r.rows[1].credit == 1250.0


# --- L2: the self-identifying balance column, found by the oracle, not by a label -------------


def test_l2_search_finds_self_identifying_balance_via_oracle():
    # Unusual order, no header, no column_order: balance sits BEFORE the flows. Only the assignment
    # whose running balance walks cleanly should survive — the oracle picks it.
    table = [
        ["02/01/2026", "UPI", "750.00", "250.00", ""],  # date, narr, BALANCE, DEBIT, CREDIT
        ["03/01/2026", "Salary", "2000.00", "", "1250.00"],
    ]
    r = map_table(table, PROFILE, BANK_FAMILY, oracle=_walk)
    assert r is not None and r.verified and r.layer == "L2"
    assert r.column_map["balance"] == 2
    assert r.rows[0].balance == 750.0 and r.rows[0].debit == 250.0
    assert r.rows[1].credit == 1250.0 and r.rows[1].balance == 2000.0


def test_returns_unverified_when_nothing_reconciles():
    # The Axis-style safety path: with an oracle, a non-reconciling parse comes back verified=False
    # so the caller can refuse to persist it.
    table = [
        ["02/01/2026", "UPI", "250.00", "", "750.00"],
        ["03/01/2026", "Salary", "", "1250.00", "9999.00"],  # balance broken
    ]
    r = map_table(table, PROFILE, BANK_FAMILY, oracle=_walk)
    assert r is not None and not r.verified


def test_returns_none_when_no_transaction_rows():
    table = [["Date", "Particulars", "Balance"], ["x", "y", "z"]]
    assert map_table(table, PROFILE, BANK_FAMILY) is None


# --- family-agnostic seam: the same engine, a different concept family + oracle ----------------


@dataclass
class PointsRow:
    txn_date: str
    earned: float | None
    redeemed: float | None
    balance: float | None


def _build_points(mapped):
    return [
        PointsRow(
            txn_date=m.get("txn_date", ""),
            earned=parse_amount(m.get("earned")),
            redeemed=parse_amount(m.get("redeemed")),
            balance=parse_amount(m.get("balance")),
        )
        for m in mapped
    ]


POINTS_FAMILY = FamilySpec(
    name="points",
    concepts=("txn_date", "earned", "redeemed", "balance"),
    date_concepts=("txn_date",),
    amount_concepts=("earned", "redeemed", "balance"),
    balance_concept="balance",
    text_concepts=(),
    default_aliases={
        "txn_date": ("date",),
        "earned": ("earned", "points in"),
        "redeemed": ("redeemed", "points out"),
        "balance": ("balance", "points balance"),
    },
    build=_build_points,
)


def _points_walk(rows):
    prev, compared = None, 0
    for r in rows:
        if r.balance is None:
            continue
        if prev is not None:
            compared += 1
            if abs((prev + (r.earned or 0.0) - (r.redeemed or 0.0)) - r.balance) > 0.01:
                return False
        prev = r.balance
    return compared > 0


def test_family_agnostic_seam_drives_a_non_bank_family():
    # No debit/credit/balance anywhere — the engine maps a loyalty-points family with its own
    # concepts and reconciliation oracle, reusing the exact same L0/L1/L2 control flow.
    table = [
        ["02/01/2026", "100.00", "", "100.00"],  # earned 100 -> balance 100
        ["03/01/2026", "", "40.00", "60.00"],  # redeemed 40 -> balance 60
    ]
    r = map_table(table, PROFILE, POINTS_FAMILY, oracle=_points_walk)
    assert r is not None and r.verified
    assert r.rows[0].earned == 100.0 and r.rows[0].balance == 100.0
    assert r.rows[1].redeemed == 40.0 and r.rows[1].balance == 60.0

16	## 2. Statement Parsers (Track A + C progress)
17	Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).
18	
19	* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). Extracts date, narration, value date, ref, withdrawal, deposit, closing balance. **Self-validating:** balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own end-of-statement SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Verified complete on real statements (37 & 31 txns) without inspecting any value.
20	* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings (`axis_transactions`) + Credit Card (`axis_cc_transactions`). ⚠️ The savings `extract_tables()` mapping is **unreliable on real Axis layouts** — column counts and date placement vary between statements (e.g. Jun vs Oct 2025), so it can mislabel rows. Reconciliation catches this, so as a **stop-gap the savings parser refuses to store rows that don't reconcile** (marks the document `unreconciled`, inserts 0) instead of persisting garbage. **Pending real fix: an HDFC-style coordinate-clustering rewrite** validated by total reconciliation (`opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing`). Credit-card has no running balance (parse/persist split + count only).
21	* **ICICI Bank** (`icici.py`): Transaction / savings statements (`icici_transactions`); **reconciles** + records status.
22	
23	All balance-bearing parsers share one **verifier** (`muneem.parsers.validation`) and split parsing from DB-insert, so the row logic is unit-testable without a PDF/DB and a parse is validated before it's trusted. **The concept-core rework (parser-architecture.md step 1) has landed:** the verifier now spans the per-row walk + total reconcile (`opening + ΣCr − ΣDr == closing`) + a concept-level bookend + a recorded confidence verdict, all targeting a bank-agnostic **concept schema** (`muneem.parsers.concepts`); **HDFC is refactored onto it as the reference implementation** with no behavior change. Axis/ICICI migrate onto the core next (express as profiles — step 2). Parsers use `pdfplumber` (with optional password); HDFC parses by word-position clustering, Axis/ICICI by `extract_tables()` (reconciliation will flag it if that drops rows on a real statement). **Password resolution** (password_rules + hybrid unlock) is **not yet wired** into the main `muneem ingest --doc-type bank_statement` path; it is exercised only via the `@pytest.mark.local_statements` test in `tests/parsers/test_local_axis.py`. Unlock hybrid logic exists in `unlock.py` (pypdf + candidates/prompt) and is tested.
24	
25	See `password_rules.py` (now prefers user config dir) and `cli.py:ingest`.

### 178. assistant (2026-05-31T09:00:26.288Z)

"""Coordinate clustering — turn positioned words into a clean cell-matrix.

When ``pdfplumber.extract_tables()`` mis-groups a semi-ruled or whitespace-aligned statement (the
Axis case: a date merged into the narration cell, column counts drifting between vintages), we fall
back to reading **word positions** directly. Words are grouped into visual rows by their ``top``,
column boundaries are inferred from the vertical whitespace shared across all rows, and each word is
dropped into the column its x-centre falls in. The result is a rectangular cell-matrix the generic
``engine`` can map — and because columns come from geometry, a leading date sits in its *own* column
instead of being glued to the narration.

This is bank-agnostic extraction (no issuer constants, no x-positions hard-coded); the engine's
oracle still decides whether the result is trustworthy. Pure geometry in, strings out — no value is
logged.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

Word = dict  # a pdfplumber word: {"text", "x0", "x1", "top", "bottom", ...}


def group_lines(words: Sequence[Word], tol: float = 2.5) -> list[list[Word]]:
    """Group words into visual lines by ``top`` (tolerant of sub-pixel jitter), left-to-right."""
    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
    anchor_top: float | None = None
    for w in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
        if lines and anchor_top is not None and abs(w["top"] - anchor_top) <= tol:
            lines[-1].append(w)
        else:
            lines.append([w])
            anchor_top = w["top"]
    return [sorted(line, key=lambda w: w["x0"]) for line in lines]


def infer_columns(words: Sequence[Word], gap: float = 10.0) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
    """Infer column x-extents by merging word x-intervals separated by less than ``gap`` points.

    The gaps wider than ``gap`` are the inter-column whitespace; everything else coalesces into a
    column. Returns ``(x_start, x_end)`` per column, left to right.
    """
    intervals = sorted((float(w["x0"]), float(w["x1"])) for w in words)
    merged: list[list[float]] = []
    for x0, x1 in intervals:
        if merged and x0 <= merged[-1][1] + gap:
            merged[-1][1] = max(merged[-1][1], x1)
        else:
            merged.append([x0, x1])
    return [(a, b) for a, b in merged]


def _column_of(center: float, columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> int:
    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(columns):
        if a <= center <= b:
            return i
    return min(
        range(len(columns)),
        key=lambda i: min(abs(center - columns[i][0]), abs(center - columns[i][1])),
    )


def line_to_cells(line: Sequence[Word], columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> list[str]:
    """Place each word into its column; join words that share a column (in x order)."""
    cells = ["" for _ in columns]
    for w in line:
        idx = _column_of((float(w["x0"]) + float(w["x1"])) / 2, columns)
        cells[idx] = (cells[idx] + " " + str(w["text"])).strip()
    return cells


def cluster_to_matrix(
    words_by_page: Sequence[Sequence[Word]], *, line_tol: float = 2.5, col_gap: float = 10.0
) -> list[list[str]]:
    """Cluster words from one or more pages into a single cell-matrix with consistent columns.

    Column boundaries are inferred **once across all pages** (a statement keeps its layout), so rows
    from every page land in the same columns; rows are emitted in page-then-top order.
    """
    all_words = [w for page in words_by_page for w in page]
    if not all_words:
        return []
    columns = infer_columns(all_words, col_gap)
    matrix: list[list[str]] = []
    for page in words_by_page:
        for line in group_lines(page, line_tol):
            matrix.append(line_to_cells(line, columns))
    return matrix


__all__ = ["group_lines", "infer_columns", "line_to_cells", "cluster_to_matrix"]

### 187. user (2026-05-31T09:02:08.347Z)

1	# muneem
2	
3	**Your automated muneem — a personal-finance bookkeeper that pulls financial documents from your inbox, unlocks them, and builds a local consolidated ledger.**
4	
5	In traditional Indian households a *muneem* (मुनीम) was the trusted bookkeeper who tracked every statement, bill, and transaction on the family's behalf. This project is the same idea, automated and personal.
6	
7	## What it does (intended)
8	
9	Reads Gmail and Outlook for financial documents (bank statements, credit-card bills, brokerage statements, mutual-fund reports, utility bills, grocery and shopping receipts), decrypts the password-protected ones, parses them into a normalized local database, and surfaces insights:
10	
11	- Grocery/shopping price-tracking across merchants over time
12	- Investment portfolio timeline and cross-broker view
13	- Bank/card performance comparison
14	- Catalog of purchased items with their bills (warranty, returns, age)
15	- Forgotten deals and coupons sitting unread in your inbox
16	
17	## Status
18	
19	**Early implementation.** The design and plan are settled — see [`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md) (architecture + threat model), [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./docs/ROADMAP.md) (milestones), and [`docs/TESTING.md`](./docs/TESTING.md) (test strategy). The Python project skeleton, CLI, and offline CI are in place; build proceeds by the roadmap's milestones. Prior art: [`docs/prior-art-email-mining.md`](./docs/prior-art-email-mining.md).
20	
21	## Getting started
22	
23	Runs on Linux, macOS, and Windows. Requires **Python ≥ 3.11** and git.
24	
25	### Linux / macOS
26	
27	```bash
28	python3 -m venv .venv
29	source .venv/bin/activate
30	pip install -e ".[dev]"
31	
32	muneem --version
33	muneem info
34	muneem ingest testdata/promo-emails/dataquest.pdf   # -> JS231sz
35	muneem offers list
36	```
37	
38	Or use the `Makefile`: `make install`, `make test`, `make lint`, `make run`.
39	
40	### Windows (PowerShell)
41	
42	```powershell
43	python -m venv .venv
44	.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
45	pip install -e ".[dev]"
46	muneem info
47	```
48	
49	### Optional: OCR for codes inside banner images
50	
51	The `--ocr` flag uses **Tesseract** (Apache-2.0, fully local — no paid API):
52	
53	| OS | Install |
54	|---|---|
55	| Debian / Ubuntu | `sudo apt-get install tesseract-ocr` |
56	| Fedora | `sudo dnf install tesseract` |
57	| macOS | `brew install tesseract` |
58	| Windows | `winget install UB-Mannheim.TesseractOCR` |
59	
60	muneem finds it on `PATH`, at the usual install locations, or via the `MUNEEM_TESSERACT_CMD` env var. Then `muneem ingest <pdf> --ocr` (run OCR tests with `make test-ocr`).
61	
62	### Notes
63	
64	- **Headless Linux:** secret storage (used once Gmail/unlock land) needs a keyring backend — a desktop keyring (GNOME Keyring / KWallet), or `pip install keyrings.alt` for a file backend. Not needed yet.
65	- CI runs the offline suite on Linux across Python 3.11–3.13 on every push.
66	
67	## Privacy
68	
69	Single-user, fully local by design. All raw statement data stays on the machine. See `DESIGN.md` § Security for the threat model and standing rules.
70

1	"""Decrypt password-protected statement PDFs (Stage 2).
2	
3	Uses pypdf (BSD-3-Clause) — permissive, pure-Python. Implements the hybrid
4	password strategy from DESIGN.md § 8.2: try a candidate dictionary first, then
5	fall back to an interactive prompt. The working password is returned so the
6	caller can cache it in the OS keychain (muneem.secrets); **it is never logged**.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import shutil
12	from collections.abc import Callable, Iterable
13	from dataclasses import dataclass
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	import pypdf
17	
18	
19	@dataclass
20	class UnlockResult:
21	    ok: bool
22	    method: str | None  # "plaintext" | "candidate" | "prompt"
23	    password: str | None  # working password — do NOT log; None if plaintext/failed
24	    output_path: Path | None  # decrypted copy, if a destination was given
25	
26	
27	def is_encrypted(path: Path | str) -> bool:
28	    return pypdf.PdfReader(str(path)).is_encrypted
29	
30	
31	def password_works(path: Path | str, password: str) -> bool:
32	    reader = pypdf.PdfReader(str(path))
33	    if not reader.is_encrypted:
34	        return True
35	    try:
36	        return reader.decrypt(password) != pypdf.PasswordType.NOT_DECRYPTED
37	    except Exception:
38	        return False
39	
40	
41	def write_decrypted(path: Path | str, password: str | None, dest: Path | str) -> Path:
42	    """Write a decrypted copy of the PDF to dest (password may be '' for plaintext)."""
43	    src = Path(path)
44	    dest = Path(dest)
45	    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
46	    reader = pypdf.PdfReader(str(src))
47	    if reader.is_encrypted:
48	        reader.decrypt(password or "")
49	    writer = pypdf.PdfWriter()
50	    writer.append(reader)
51	    with open(dest, "wb") as fh:
52	        writer.write(fh)
53	    return dest
54	
55	
56	def unlock(
57	    path: Path | str,
58	    candidates: Iterable[str] = (),
59	    prompt_fn: Callable[[], str | None] | None = None,
60	    max_prompts: int = 3,
61	    dest: Path | str | None = None,
62	) -> UnlockResult:
63	    """Hybrid unlock: plaintext? -> candidate dictionary -> interactive prompt.
64	
65	    If dest is given and unlock succeeds, a decrypted copy is written there. The
66	    working password is returned in the result but must never be logged.
67	    """
68	    src = Path(path)
69	    if not is_encrypted(src):
70	        out = Path(shutil.copyfile(src, dest)) if dest else None
71	        return UnlockResult(True, "plaintext", None, out)
72	
73	    tried: set[str] = set()
74	    for pw in candidates:
75	        if pw in tried:
76	            continue
77	        tried.add(pw)
78	        if password_works(src, pw):
79	            out = write_decrypted(src, pw, dest) if dest else None
80	            return UnlockResult(True, "candidate", pw, out)
81	
82	    if prompt_fn is not None:
83	        for _ in range(max_prompts):
84	            pw = prompt_fn()
85	            if not pw:
86	                break
87	            if password_works(src, pw):
88	                out = write_decrypted(src, pw, dest) if dest else None
89	                return UnlockResult(True, "prompt", pw, out)
90	
91	    return UnlockResult(False, None, None, None)
92

1	"""Local SQLite store: the ``documents`` and ``offers`` slice of the schema.
2	
3	This is the minimal slice the promo spine needs (ROADMAP A.2). The full schema
4	(accounts, transactions, holdings, merchants, items) grows with later milestones.
5	``documents.sha256`` is UNIQUE so re-ingesting the same file is a no-op.
6	``offers.source`` records provenance ('text' = exact text layer, 'ocr' = approximate).
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import sqlite3
12	from collections.abc import Iterator
13	from contextlib import contextmanager
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	SCHEMA_VERSION = 4
17	
18	_SCHEMA = """
19	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS documents (
20	    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
21	    source        TEXT NOT NULL,
22	    external_id   TEXT,
23	    sender        TEXT,
24	    subject       TEXT,
25	    received_date TEXT,
26	    doc_type      TEXT,
27	    sha256        TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
28	    path          TEXT,
29	    status        TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'parsed',
30	    created_at    TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
31	);
32	
33	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS offers (
34	    id          INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
35	    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
36	    merchant    TEXT,
37	    promo_code  TEXT,
38	    description TEXT,
39	    expiry_raw  TEXT,
40	    expiry_type TEXT,
41	    source      TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'text',
42	    redeemed    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
43	    source_text TEXT
44	);
45	
46	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_offers_document ON offers(document_id);
47	
48	-- We use separate tables for each bank (e.g. axis_transactions, icici_transactions) 
49	-- instead of a unified table to preserve raw schema fidelity of the source PDFs.
50	-- Normalization into a unified view layer is planned for a later phase 
51	-- once all bank schemas are stabilized.
52	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS axis_transactions (
53	    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
54	    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
55	    account_number TEXT,
56	    txn_date TEXT,
57	    value_date TEXT,
58	    particulars TEXT,
59	    debit REAL,
60	    credit REAL,
61	    balance REAL
62	);
63	
64	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS axis_cc_transactions (
65	    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
66	    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
67	    account_number TEXT,
68	    txn_date TEXT,
69	    particulars TEXT,
70	    merchant_category TEXT,
71	    amount REAL,
72	    type TEXT
73	);
74	
75	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS icici_transactions (
76	    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
77	    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
78	    account_number TEXT,
79	    txn_date TEXT,
80	    mode TEXT,
81	    particulars TEXT,
82	    withdrawals REAL,
83	    deposits REAL,
84	    balance REAL
85	);
86	
87	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS hdfc_transactions (
88	    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
89	    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
90	    account_number TEXT,
91	    txn_date TEXT,
92	    value_date TEXT,
93	    particulars TEXT,
94	    ref TEXT,
95	    withdrawal REAL,
96	    deposit REAL,
97	    closing_balance REAL
98	);
99	"""
100	
101	
102	@contextmanager
103	def connect(db_path: Path | str) -> Iterator[sqlite3.Connection]:
104	    """Open a connection (commit on success, always close). Parent dirs created."""
105	    path = str(db_path)
106	    if path != ":memory:":
107	        Path(path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
108	    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
109	    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
110	    conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
111	    try:
112	        yield conn
113	        conn.commit()
114	    finally:
115	        conn.close()
116	
117	
118	def init_db(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
119	    """Create tables if absent and stamp the schema version."""
120	    conn.executescript(_SCHEMA)
121	    conn.execute(f"PRAGMA user_version = {SCHEMA_VERSION}")
122	
123	
124	def get_document_by_sha(conn: sqlite3.Connection, sha256: str) -> sqlite3.Row | None:
125	    return conn.execute("SELECT * FROM documents WHERE sha256 = ?", (sha256,)).fetchone()
126	
127	
128	def _insert_row(conn: sqlite3.Connection, table: str, fields: dict) -> int:
129	    """Internal helper for the simple INSERT pattern used by documents, offers, and bank txns."""
130	    cols = ", ".join(fields)
131	    placeholders = ", ".join("?" for _ in fields)
132	    cur = conn.execute(
133	        f"INSERT INTO {table} ({cols}) VALUES ({placeholders})",
134	        tuple(fields.values()),
135	    )
136	    return int(cur.lastrowid)
137	
138	
139	def insert_document(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
140	    """Insert into documents table. Delegates to the shared helper for DRY."""
141	    return _insert_row(conn, "documents", dict(fields))
142	
143	
144	def insert_offer(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
145	    return _insert_row(conn, "offers", fields)
146	
147	
148	def list_offers(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
149	    return conn.execute(
150	        """
151	        SELECT o.id, o.merchant, o.promo_code, o.expiry_raw, o.description, o.source,
152	               d.external_id, d.path
153	        FROM offers o
154	        JOIN documents d ON d.id = o.document_id
155	        ORDER BY o.id
156	        """
157	    ).fetchall()
158	
159	
160	def insert_axis_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
161	    return _insert_row(conn, "axis_transactions", fields)
162	
163	
164	def insert_icici_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
165	    return _insert_row(conn, "icici_transactions", fields)
166	
167	
168	def insert_axis_cc_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
169	    return _insert_row(conn, "axis_cc_transactions", fields)
170	
171	
172	def list_axis_cc_transactions(conn: sqlite3.Connection, limit: int = 50) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
173	    return conn.execute(
174	        "SELECT * FROM axis_cc_transactions ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
175	    ).fetchall()

1	"""Regression test for the parser registry (the PR #9-11 production-path bug).
2	
3	`from muneem.parsers import get_parser` must return a working selector — i.e. the
4	registry must be populated by package import alone, without anything importing the
5	axis/icici/... submodules first. We check in a FRESH interpreter (subprocess) so a
6	prior test importing a submodule can't mask a regression.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import subprocess
12	import sys
13	
14	
15	def test_registry_is_populated_by_package_import_alone():
16	    code = (
17	        "from muneem.parsers import get_parser, _REGISTRY\n"
18	        "assert len(_REGISTRY) >= 2, f'registry empty: {_REGISTRY}'\n"
19	        "assert get_parser('Axis Bank Statement of Account') is not None\n"
20	        "assert get_parser('ICICI Bank Statement of Transactions') is not None\n"
21	        "assert get_parser('a wholly unrelated document') is None\n"
22	        "print('REGISTRY_OK', len(_REGISTRY))\n"
23	    )
24	    result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-c", code], capture_output=True, text=True)
25	    assert result.returncode == 0, f"fresh-interpreter import failed:\n{result.stderr}"
26	    assert "REGISTRY_OK" in result.stdout
27	
28	
29	def test_get_parser_selects_by_sender():
30	    from muneem.parsers import get_parser
31	
32	    assert type(get_parser("", sender="alerts@axisbank.com")).__name__ == "AxisParser"
33	    assert type(get_parser("", sender="noreply@icicibank.com")).__name__ == "ICICIParser"

1	"""The ``muneem`` command-line interface.
2	
3	Track A ships the promo spine: ``ingest`` a PDF into the local store and
4	``offers list`` to read back what was found. ``--ocr`` adds local image OCR for
5	codes rasterized into banners (approximate; flagged for verification). Pipeline
6	subcommands (``fetch``, ``txns``) land with their milestones.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	from pathlib import Path
12	
13	import typer
14	
15	from muneem import __version__
16	from muneem import db as dbmod
17	from muneem.config import load_config
18	
19	app = typer.Typer(
20	    add_completion=False,
21	    help="muneem — a local-first personal-finance bookkeeper.",
22	    no_args_is_help=True,
23	)
24	offers_app = typer.Typer(no_args_is_help=True, help="Work with extracted offers.")
25	app.add_typer(offers_app, name="offers")
26	
27	txns_app = typer.Typer(no_args_is_help=True, help="Work with parsed transactions.")
28	app.add_typer(txns_app, name="txns")
29	
30	
31	# Dispatch table for supported banks (extensible as new parsers land).
32	BANK_DISPATCH = {
33	    "axis": (dbmod.list_axis_transactions, "debit", "credit"),
34	    "axis_cc": (dbmod.list_axis_cc_transactions, "amount", "type"),
35	    "icici": (dbmod.list_icici_transactions, "withdrawals", "deposits"),
36	    "hdfc": (dbmod.list_hdfc_transactions, "withdrawal", "deposit"),
37	}
38	
39	
40	def _version_callback(value: bool) -> None:
41	    if value:
42	        typer.echo(f"muneem {__version__}")
43	        raise typer.Exit()
44	
45	
46	@app.callback()
47	def main(
48	    version: bool = typer.Option(
49	        False,
50	        "--version",
51	        callback=_version_callback,
52	        is_eager=True,
53	        help="Show the muneem version and exit.",
54	    ),
55	) -> None:
56	    """muneem command-line interface."""
57	
58	
59	@app.command()
60	def info() -> None:
61	    """Show resolved local paths (no secrets, no sensitive data)."""
62	    cfg = load_config()
63	    present = "present" if cfg.config_file.is_file() else "absent"
64	    typer.echo(f"muneem {__version__}")
65	    typer.echo(f"config dir : {cfg.config_dir}")
66	    typer.echo(f"data dir   : {cfg.data_dir}")
67	    typer.echo(f"db path    : {cfg.db_path}")
68	    typer.echo(f"staging    : {cfg.staging_dir}")
69	    typer.echo(f"config file: {cfg.config_file} ({present})")
70	
71	
72	@app.command()
73	def ingest(
74	    pdf: Path = typer.Argument(
75	        ..., exists=True, dir_okay=False, readable=True, help="Path to a PDF to ingest."
76	    ),
77	    doc_type: str = typer.Option("promo", help="Document category."),
78	    db: Path | None = typer.Option(None, help="SQLite path (defaults to the local data dir)."),
79	    ocr: bool = typer.Option(
80	        False, "--ocr", help="Also OCR images locally (Tesseract) for codes in banners."
81	    ),
82	) -> None:
83	    """Extract a PDF and store its document + any offers in the local DB."""
84	    from muneem import db as dbmod
85	    from muneem.extract import extract
86	    from muneem.offers import extract_offer
87	
88	    db_path = db or load_config().db_path
89	    ex = extract(pdf)
90	
91	    if doc_type == "bank_statement":
92	        from muneem.parsers import get_parser
93	
94	        parser = get_parser(ex.text)
95	        if not parser:
96	            typer.echo(f"No parser found for bank statement: {pdf.name}", err=True)
97	            return
98	
99	        with dbmod.connect(db_path) as conn:
100	            dbmod.init_db(conn)
101	            existing = dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, ex.sha256)
102	            if existing is not None:
103	                typer.echo(f"already ingested (doc {existing['id']}): {pdf.name}")
104	                return
105	
106	            doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(
107	                conn,
108	                source="file",
109	                external_id=pdf.name,
110	                doc_type=doc_type,
111	                sha256=ex.sha256,
112	                path=str(pdf.resolve()),
113	                status="parsed",
114	            )
115	
116	            count = parser.parse_and_insert(pdf, conn, doc_id)
117	
118	        typer.echo(
119	            f"ingested {pdf.name} (doc {doc_id}); parsed {count} "
120	            f"transactions using {parser.__class__.__name__}"
121	        )
122	        return
123	    text_fields = extract_offer(ex.text)
124	    # (code, source) pairs; text-layer codes are exact, OCR codes are approximate.
125	    found: list[tuple[str, str]] = [(c, "text") for c in text_fields.codes]
126	    expiry_raw, expiry_type = text_fields.expiry_raw, text_fields.expiry_type
127	
128	    if ocr:
129	        from muneem.ocr import ocr_pdf, tesseract_available
130	
131	        if not tesseract_available():
132	            typer.echo("warning: --ocr requested but Tesseract not found; skipping OCR.", err=True)
133	        else:
134	            ocr_fields = extract_offer(ocr_pdf(pdf))
135	            known = {c for c, _ in found}
136	            for code in ocr_fields.codes:
137	                if code not in known:
138	                    found.append((code, "ocr"))
139	                    known.add(code)
140	            if expiry_raw is None:
141	                expiry_raw, expiry_type = ocr_fields.expiry_raw, ocr_fields.expiry_type
142	
143	    with dbmod.connect(db_path) as conn:
144	        dbmod.init_db(conn)
145	        existing = dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, ex.sha256)
146	        if existing is not None:
147	            typer.echo(f"already ingested (doc {existing['id']}): {pdf.name}")
148	            return
149	        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(
150	            conn,
151	            source="file",
152	            external_id=pdf.name,
153	            doc_type=doc_type,
154	            sha256=ex.sha256,
155	            path=str(pdf.resolve()),
156	            status="parsed",
157	        )
158	        for code, src in found:
159	            dbmod.insert_offer(
160	                conn,
161	                document_id=doc_id,
162	                promo_code=code,
163	                expiry_raw=expiry_raw,
164	                expiry_type=expiry_type,
165	                source=src,
166	                source_text=ex.text[:2000],
167	            )
168	
169	    summary = ", ".join(f"{c} [{s}]" for c, s in found) if found else "none"
170	    typer.echo(f"ingested {pdf.name} (doc {doc_id}); {len(found)} offer(s): {summary}")
171	
172	
173	@offers_app.command("list")
174	def offers_list(
175	    db: Path | None = typer.Option(None, help="SQLite path (defaults to the local data dir)."),
176	) -> None:
177	    """List extracted offers."""
178	    from muneem import db as dbmod
179	
180	    db_path = db or load_config().db_path
181	    if not Path(db_path).exists():
182	        typer.echo("no offers yet (database not created).")
183	        return
184	
185	    with dbmod.connect(db_path) as conn:
186	        dbmod.init_db(conn)
187	        rows = dbmod.list_offers(conn)
188	
189	    if not rows:
190	        typer.echo("no offers found.")
191	        return
192	    for row in rows:
193	        who = row["merchant"] or row["external_id"] or "?"
194	        when = f" — expires {row['expiry_raw']}" if row["expiry_raw"] else ""
195	        tag = " [ocr — verify]" if row["source"] == "ocr" else ""
196	        typer.echo(f"[{row['id']}] {who}: {row['promo_code']}{when}{tag}")
197	
198	
199	@txns_app.command("list")
200	def txns_list(
201	    bank: str = typer.Argument(

203	    ),

231	        else:

233	        part = (row["particulars"] or "")[:50]
234	        typer.echo(f"[{row['account_number']}] {row['txn_date']} : {part:<50} | {amount:>10}")

1	"""Per-issuer profiles — bank knowledge expressed as **data, not code** (architecture §6).
2	
3	A profile carries the priors the generic engine needs to disambiguate one issuer: extra
4	concept↔label aliases, the accepted date shape, a positional fallback when there's no header,
5	table anchors, account-number patterns, quirks, and (parked) the password rule. Adding an issuer is
6	ideally *adding a profile*, not writing a parser.
7	
8	`ICICI_PROFILE` drives ICICI fully through `engine.map_table`. `HDFC_PROFILE` is data consumed by
9	HDFC's parser, whose *extraction* stays bespoke coordinate-clustering — the documented exception
10	where `extract_tables()` fails on a semi-ruled table (see the `hdfc-parser-tech-decision` memory).
11	The Axis profile lands with the Step 3 coordinate-clustering rewrite.
12	"""
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import re
17	
18	from .engine import Profile
19	
20	ICICI_PROFILE = Profile(
21	    name="icici",
22	    aliases={
23	        "narration": ("particulars",),
24	        "debit": ("withdrawals",),
25	        "credit": ("deposits",),
26	        "mode": (
27	            "mode",
28	        ),  # ICICI-specific extra (UPI/NEFT/...); rides along in MappingResult.mapped
29	    },
30	    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
31	    # Known ICICI layout, used when a table has no detectable header row.
32	    column_order=("txn_date", "mode", "narration", "debit", "credit", "balance"),
33	    account_patterns=(
34	        re.compile(r"Account Number\s*:\s*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
35	        re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\s*[:\s]*([\d-]+)", re.IGNORECASE),
36	    ),
37	)
38	
39	HDFC_PROFILE = Profile(
40	    name="hdfc",
41	    aliases={
42	        "debit": ("withdrawal", "withdrawals"),
43	        "credit": ("deposit", "deposits"),
44	        "balance": ("closing balance",),
45	    },
46	    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
47	    table_anchors=("Narration", "Closing Balance"),
48	    account_patterns=(re.compile(r"Account\s*Number\s*[:.]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),),
49	    # HDFC's transaction table is semi-ruled; extract_tables() is unreliable on it, so HDFC extracts
50	    # by word-position clustering rather than through the generic cell-matrix engine.
51	    quirks=frozenset({"semi-ruled-table", "coordinate-clustering"}),
52	)
53	
54	__all__ = ["ICICI_PROFILE", "HDFC_PROFILE"]
55

### 212. assistant (2026-05-31T09:13:34.870Z)

"""Per-issuer profiles — bank knowledge expressed as **data, not code** (architecture §6).

A profile carries the priors the generic engine needs to disambiguate one issuer: extra
concept↔label aliases, the accepted date shape, a positional fallback when there's no header,
table anchors, account-number patterns, quirks, and (parked) the password rule. Adding an issuer is
ideally *adding a profile*, not writing a parser.

`HDFC_PROFILE` is data consumed by HDFC's parser, whose *extraction* stays bespoke coordinate-
clustering — the documented exception where `extract_tables()` fails on a semi-ruled table (see the
`hdfc-parser-tech-decision` memory). The Axis and AU Bank profiles land with their engine-driven
parsers (coordinate clustering → generic mapping → total reconcile).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from .engine import Profile

HDFC_PROFILE = Profile(
    name="hdfc",
    aliases={
        "debit": ("withdrawal", "withdrawals"),
        "credit": ("deposit", "deposits"),
        "balance": ("closing balance",),
    },
    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
    table_anchors=("Narration", "Closing Balance"),
    account_patterns=(re.compile(r"Account\s*Number\s*[:.]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),),
    # HDFC's transaction table is semi-ruled; extract_tables() is unreliable on it, so HDFC extracts
    # by word-position clustering rather than through the generic cell-matrix engine.
    quirks=frozenset({"semi-ruled-table", "coordinate-clustering"}),
)

__all__ = ["HDFC_PROFILE"]

### 214. assistant (2026-05-31T09:13:55.516Z)

"""Local SQLite store: the ``documents`` and ``offers`` slice of the schema.

This is the minimal slice the promo spine needs (ROADMAP A.2). The full schema
(accounts, transactions, holdings, merchants, items) grows with later milestones.
``documents.sha256`` is UNIQUE so re-ingesting the same file is a no-op.
``offers.source`` records provenance ('text' = exact text layer, 'ocr' = approximate).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from collections.abc import Iterator
from contextlib import contextmanager
from pathlib import Path

SCHEMA_VERSION = 4

_SCHEMA = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS documents (
    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    source        TEXT NOT NULL,
    external_id   TEXT,
    sender        TEXT,
    subject       TEXT,
    received_date TEXT,
    doc_type      TEXT,
    sha256        TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    path          TEXT,
    status        TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'parsed',
    created_at    TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS offers (
    id          INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    merchant    TEXT,
    promo_code  TEXT,
    description TEXT,
    expiry_raw  TEXT,
    expiry_type TEXT,
    source      TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'text',
    redeemed    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    source_text TEXT
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_offers_document ON offers(document_id);

-- We use separate tables for each bank (e.g. axis_transactions, hdfc_transactions)
-- instead of a unified table to preserve raw schema fidelity of the source PDFs.
-- Normalization into a unified view layer is planned for a later phase
-- once all bank schemas are stabilized.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS axis_transactions (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    account_number TEXT,
    txn_date TEXT,
    value_date TEXT,
    particulars TEXT,
    debit REAL,
    credit REAL,
    balance REAL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS axis_cc_transactions (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    account_number TEXT,
    txn_date TEXT,
    particulars TEXT,
    merchant_category TEXT,
    amount REAL,
    type TEXT
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS hdfc_transactions (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    document_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    account_number TEXT,
    txn_date TEXT,
    value_date TEXT,
    particulars TEXT,
    ref TEXT,
    withdrawal REAL,
    deposit REAL,
    closing_balance REAL
);
"""


@contextmanager
def connect(db_path: Path | str) -> Iterator[sqlite3.Connection]:
    """Open a connection (commit on success, always close). Parent dirs created."""
    path = str(db_path)
    if path != ":memory:":
        Path(path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
    conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
    try:
        yield conn
        conn.commit()
    finally:
        conn.close()


def init_db(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """Create tables if absent and stamp the schema version."""
    conn.executescript(_SCHEMA)
    conn.execute(f"PRAGMA user_version = {SCHEMA_VERSION}")


def get_document_by_sha(conn: sqlite3.Connection, sha256: str) -> sqlite3.Row | None:
    return conn.execute("SELECT * FROM documents WHERE sha256 = ?", (sha256,)).fetchone()


def _insert_row(conn: sqlite3.Connection, table: str, fields: dict) -> int:
    """Internal helper for the simple INSERT pattern used by documents, offers, and bank txns."""
    cols = ", ".join(fields)
    placeholders = ", ".join("?" for _ in fields)
    cur = conn.execute(
        f"INSERT INTO {table} ({cols}) VALUES ({placeholders})",
        tuple(fields.values()),
    )
    return int(cur.lastrowid)


def insert_document(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
    """Insert into documents table. Delegates to the shared helper for DRY."""
    return _insert_row(conn, "documents", dict(fields))


def insert_offer(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
    return _insert_row(conn, "offers", fields)


def list_offers(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
    return conn.execute(
        """
        SELECT o.id, o.merchant, o.promo_code, o.expiry_raw, o.description, o.source,
               d.external_id, d.path
        FROM offers o
        JOIN documents d ON d.id = o.document_id
        ORDER BY o.id
        """
    ).fetchall()


def insert_axis_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
    return _insert_row(conn, "axis_transactions", fields)


def insert_axis_cc_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
    return _insert_row(conn, "axis_cc_transactions", fields)


def list_axis_cc_transactions(conn: sqlite3.Connection, limit: int = 50) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT * FROM axis_cc_transactions ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
    ).fetchall()


def list_axis_transactions(conn: sqlite3.Connection, limit: int = 50) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT * FROM axis_transactions ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
    ).fetchall()


def insert_hdfc_transaction(conn: sqlite3.Connection, **fields: object) -> int:
    return _insert_row(conn, "hdfc_transactions", fields)


def list_hdfc_transactions(conn: sqlite3.Connection, limit: int = 50) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
    return conn.execute(
        "SELECT * FROM hdfc_transactions ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?", (limit,)
    ).fetchall()
