### 216. assistant (2026-05-31T09:13:59.751Z)

"""Regression test for the parser registry (the PR #9-11 production-path bug).

`from muneem.parsers import get_parser` must return a working selector — i.e. the
registry must be populated by package import alone, without anything importing the
axis/hdfc/... submodules first. We check in a FRESH interpreter (subprocess) so a
prior test importing a submodule can't mask a regression.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
import sys


def test_registry_is_populated_by_package_import_alone():
    code = (
        "from muneem.parsers import get_parser, _REGISTRY\n"
        "assert len(_REGISTRY) >= 2, f'registry empty: {_REGISTRY}'\n"
        "assert get_parser('Axis Bank Statement of Account') is not None\n"
        "assert get_parser('HDFC Bank savings account details') is not None\n"
        "assert get_parser('a wholly unrelated document') is None\n"
        "print('REGISTRY_OK', len(_REGISTRY))\n"
    )
    result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-c", code], capture_output=True, text=True)
    assert result.returncode == 0, f"fresh-interpreter import failed:\n{result.stderr}"
    assert "REGISTRY_OK" in result.stdout


def test_get_parser_selects_by_sender():
    from muneem.parsers import get_parser

    assert type(get_parser("", sender="alerts@axisbank.com")).__name__ == "AxisParser"

### 227. user (2026-05-31T09:14:40.198Z)

1	# muneem — Design Document
2	
3	> Status: **draft, pre-implementation**. This document captures the vision, the pipeline shape, the security posture, and the open decisions to resolve before code lands. It is intentionally opinionated where the author has a strong view, and explicitly undecided where the trade-offs need a human call.
4	
5	---
6	
7	## 1. What muneem is
8	
9	**muneem** is a personal-finance bookkeeper that ingests financial documents from the user's email accounts, unlocks the password-protected ones, parses them into a normalized schema, stores them in a local database, and surfaces analytics across the user's full financial life.
10	
11	The name is the Hindi word for the traditional household/business bookkeeper — a trusted figure who maintained the family ledger by hand. This project is the same role, automated.
12	
13	**Single user. Local-first. Privacy-critical.**
14	
15	## 2. Why
16	
17	The user's financial life today is fragmented across many silos:
18	
19	- 3–5 bank accounts (statements arrive monthly as password-protected PDFs)
20	- Multiple credit cards (monthly bills, also password-protected PDFs)
21	- Brokerage and mutual-fund accounts (quarterly statements, portfolio snapshots)
22	- Grocery and shopping receipts (Swiggy, Zomato, Amazon, Flipkart, BigBasket, Blinkit — mostly plain HTML/PDF in email body)
23	- Utility bills, insurance premiums, EMI schedules
24	- Promotional offers and coupons (often forgotten)
25	
26	Each silo has its own portal, login, and format. There is no consolidated view. **The inbox is the only place where they already converge.**
27	
28	Concrete use-cases the user wants once this exists:
29	
30	1. **Grocery & shopping price intelligence** — track what was bought, where, and at what price, over time. Detect when a merchant is no longer competitive on staples.
31	2. **Investment portfolio timeline** — a single time-series of holdings across brokers, so allocation drift and rebalancing decisions are obvious.
32	3. **Bank/card comparison** — fees paid, interest earned, reward redemption efficiency, surfaced as objective numbers.
33	4. **Item catalog** — every significant purchase linked to its receipt/invoice, so warranty windows, return windows, and replacement cycles are queryable.
34	5. **Forgotten-deals reminder** — surface the unused coupons and offers already sitting in the inbox (this is the original goal of the predecessor project; see § 11).
35	
36	## 3. Origin
37	
38	muneem is the successor to `~/Projects/Email-Mining`, a small prototype the user built earlier that extracted promo codes from manually-saved email PDFs using PyMuPDF + Gemini for image OCR. Same insight ("the inbox contains structured value I'm forgetting"), generalized scope.
39	
40	See [`docs/prior-art-email-mining.md`](./docs/prior-art-email-mining.md) for what it did, what to inherit, and what not to.
41	
42	## 4. The pipeline (five stages)
43	
44	Each stage is independent enough to develop and test in isolation. They communicate through well-defined data boundaries (raw files on disk → unlocked files on disk → structured records in DB → derived views).
45	
46	### Stage 1 — Email ingestion
47	
48	**Goal:** pull messages and attachments from Gmail and Outlook into a local staging area.
49	
50	- **Gmail:** [Gmail API](https://developers.google.com/gmail/api) over OAuth2. Read-only scope (`gmail.readonly`). Supports server-side search queries (`has:attachment filename:pdf from:hdfc newer_than:90d`) which is far cheaper than pulling everything and filtering locally. Has good rate limits for personal use.
51	- **Outlook:** [Microsoft Graph API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/mail-api-overview) over OAuth2. Read-only scope (`Mail.Read`). Similar search capability.
52	- **Why not IMAP:** worse search, no labels, harder OAuth, and we lose Gmail's powerful query syntax. Use the native APIs.
53	
54	Mechanically straightforward. The main design questions are: **how is "financial document" detected** (sender allowlist? subject patterns? attachment heuristics? hybrid?), and **how is incremental sync state tracked** (Gmail `historyId` / Graph `deltaLink`).
55	
56	### Stage 2 — Attachment unlocking
57	
58	**Goal:** decrypt password-protected PDFs.
59	
60	The decryption itself is trivial — every mainstream PDF library handles the standard security handler. In Go, `github.com/pdfcpu/pdfcpu`; in Python, `pikepdf` or `pypdf`.
61	
62	The real design question is **where the passwords come from.** Indian bank and CC statement passwords are almost always derived from user attributes:
63	
64	- "First 4 letters of name (uppercase) + DDMM of DOB" — common at HDFC, ICICI
65	- "PAN number" — common at some brokers
66	- "Last 4 digits of card number" — common for CC statements
67	- "Customer ID" — some banks
68	- "Mobile number" — utilities
69	
70	Three plausible approaches, each with trade-offs:
71	
72	| Approach | Pro | Con |
73	|---|---|---|
74	| **Per-sender rule template** the user configures once (e.g., `from:alerts@hdfcbank.net → first4(name)+ddmm(dob)`) | Deterministic, no guessing, auditable | Requires upfront setup; rules must be maintained as banks change formats |
75	| **Candidate dictionary** (try a small list of derived strings per PDF) | Zero config; "just works" | Brute-forceable feel; fails noisily on unknown senders |
76	| **Interactive prompt** (ask the user the first time, cache for that sender) | Safe, simple | Friction during initial backfill of years of statements |
77	
78	A hybrid is likely best: candidate dictionary first, fall back to rule template, fall back to interactive prompt. **Decision parked.** See § 8.
79	
80	### Stage 3 — Document parsing
81	
82	**This is the hard 80% of the project.** Every issuer's PDF layout is different and changes over time.
83	
84	The space of approaches, in increasing robustness and cost:
85	
86	1. **Per-issuer regex/template parsers.** Precise; brittle. One parser per format, breaks when the issuer redesigns. Realistic for the top ~5 senders that dominate volume.
87	2. **Text-layer extraction + structural heuristics.** Pull text with `pdfminer.six` / `pdfplumber` (both MIT); rely on coordinates and font runs to recover tables. Works well for "born digital" PDFs (most bank statements); fails on scanned/image PDFs. (We avoid PyMuPDF/`fitz` — it is AGPL; see § 5 rule 8.)
88	3. **OCR (Tesseract) for image-based PDFs and embedded raster content.** Slower; need layout reconstruction.
89	4. **LLM-assisted structured extraction.** Hand page text (+ optionally page rasters) to a model with a target JSON schema. Generalizes across layouts; expensive per page; **must be local for sensitive document categories**. Options: Ollama-hosted models (Llama 3, Qwen, Mistral), or a small purpose-built model.
90	
91	In practice this will be **a hybrid**: deterministic template parsers for the top ~5 high-volume senders (where they pay for themselves), LLM-assisted extraction as the long-tail fallback. Promo emails and other non-sensitive categories can use cloud LLMs; bank/card/investment statements **must not** without explicit per-category approval.
92	
93	Embedded image deduplication (Email-Mining used MD5 hash + SSIM) is a real win here: bank statements repeat the same logo/letterhead on every page, and we don't want to OCR that 30 times per document.
94	
95	### Stage 4 — Normalization & storage
96	
97	**Goal:** map heterogeneous parser outputs into one schema, persist locally.
98	
99	Storage: **SQLite**. Single file, no server, easy to back up, easy to encrypt at rest (SQLCipher), good enough performance for years of personal data. The schema needs to span:
100	
101	- `documents` — every ingested file with sender, date, type, hash, path
102	- `accounts` — bank accounts, cards, broker accounts, identified by issuer + last-4 or similar
103	- `transactions` — line items from statements, normalized (date, amount, currency, counterparty, category, source_document)
104	- `holdings` — point-in-time snapshots of investment positions per account
105	- `merchants` — canonicalized merchant identities (so "SWIGGY*BANGALORE", "Swiggy Instamart", and "SWIGGY BNGLR" collapse to one)
106	- `items` — line-items from itemized receipts (grocery, shopping)
107	- `offers` — promotional offers/coupons with expiry, source, redemption status
108	
109	The merchant canonicalization step is non-trivial — it deserves its own thought (fuzzy match + manual override table is the usual answer).
110	
111	### Stage 5 — Analytics & insights
112	
113	**Goal:** turn the normalized DB into the use-cases in § 2.
114	
115	Once the data is in the schema, the analytics are mostly SQL + light application logic. Likely surface: a local CLI initially, possibly a small local web UI later. Notebook-style exploration is fine for prototyping queries before they're promoted to first-class views.
116	
117	---
118	
119	## 5. Security posture (first class)
120	
121	The threat model deserves to be stated plainly: **muneem aggregates the user's entire financial life — every account, every transaction, every holding — into one local store. That store is a honeypot.** A leak would be catastrophic.
122	
123	Standing rules:
124	
125	1. **All raw statement data stays on the local machine.** No cloud sync of the staging dir or the DB.
126	2. **No cloud LLM APIs for sensitive document categories** (bank, CC, broker, MF, insurance) without explicit per-category user approval discussed in the current session. The default extraction path for these is local (Tesseract + local LLM via Ollama, or deterministic parsers).
127	3. **Non-sensitive categories** (promo emails, generic newsletters, public receipts) may use cloud LLMs after a privacy review of the specific content type.
128	4. **DB encrypted at rest** (SQLCipher or equivalent), key never in the repo.
129	5. **OAuth tokens** stored in the OS keychain (Windows Credential Manager / macOS Keychain), not on disk.
130	6. **Logs must not contain extracted statement text.** Sanitize aggressively; redact account numbers, balances, line-items.
131	7. **Nothing sensitive ever in git.** `.gitignore` blocks real PDFs, the DB file, `.env`, and password files. Do not weaken it.
132	8. **Commercial-license safety.** Only dependencies under permissive, commercial-safe licenses (MIT, BSD, Apache-2.0, ISC, HPND). No copyleft (GPL/LGPL/**AGPL**) or source-available licenses. **PyMuPDF/`fitz` is AGPL-3.0 and is banned** — use pdfminer.six (MIT) / pypdfium2 (Apache/BSD) instead. Enforced by `tests/test_dependency_licenses.py`; tracked in [`docs/DEPENDENCIES.md`](./docs/DEPENDENCIES.md).
133	9. **Minimize paid dependencies.** Prefer local / self-hosted / open-source (and, where needed, own-trained) models and tools. Paid APIs — cloud LLMs, paid OCR/vision — are a **fallback only**, gated behind explicit per-use approval. This extends rule 2 from privacy to cost.
134	
135	## 6. Non-goals (for v1)
136	
137	- **Multi-user / SaaS / hosted product.** Single user, single machine.
138	- **Cloud sync of raw data.** Period.
139	- **Cracking unknown passwords.** muneem decrypts PDFs the user is authorized to read, using passwords the user knows or can derive.
140	- **Browser scraping bank portals.** Email APIs only. Portal scraping is a fragile, ToS-fraught rabbit hole; we deliberately avoid it.
141	- **Real-time alerting / push.** Batch-pull on a schedule is sufficient.
142	- **Tax preparation, investment advice, or anything regulated.** muneem surfaces data; the user decides.
143	
144	## 7. Recommended first vertical slice
145	
146	Before generalizing, prove the end-to-end pipeline on the smallest meaningful scope:
147	
148	> **Gmail → one specific bank's monthly statements → unlocked → parsed into `transactions` → stored in SQLite → a CLI command that lists last month's transactions.**
149	
150	This exercises every stage exactly once, on a real input the user cares about, with no detours. It will surface the actual hard parts (which we can only guess at now) and force the open decisions in § 8 to get made on real evidence.
151	
152	Concretely: pick **one** Indian bank statement format the user has many examples of (likely HDFC or ICICI given the user's geography), build the parser for that one format only, and put the rest of the pipeline around it. Then evaluate before generalizing.
153	
154	## 8. Open architectural decisions
155	
156	These were parked for explicit user discussion (**don't pick silently**). Items **8.1, 8.2, and 8.4 are now resolved** — see the inline resolution notes below and [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./docs/ROADMAP.md) § 1. Items **8.3 and 8.5 remain open**.
157	
158	### 8.1 Language
159	
160	| Option | Pro | Con |
161	|---|---|---|
162	| **Go only** | Single binary, easy distribution, great concurrency for the ingestion side, the user already uses Go for the badminton-highlight-indexer project | Weak PDF parsing / OCR / ML ecosystem; rolling our own is painful |
163	| **Python only** | Best-in-class PDF, OCR, and ML libraries (pdfplumber, PyMuPDF, Tesseract bindings, Ollama clients, every parser under the sun); fastest to prototype | Heavier deployment story; less ergonomic for the long-running ingestion daemon |
164	| **Go ingestion + Python parsing** | Plays to each language's strengths | Two-language project complexity; inter-process boundary to maintain |
165	
166	Recommended default: **Python-only for v1**, accept the deployment-story cost in exchange for moving fast on the hard parts. Revisit if the ingestion side outgrows Python.
167	
168	> **Resolved (2026-05-30): Python-only for v1.** Adopted the recommended default. See [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./docs/ROADMAP.md) § 1.
169	
170	### 8.2 Password sourcing strategy
171	
172	See § 4 Stage 2. The hybrid (dictionary → template → prompt) is the natural answer, but the design of the rule-template config format and the candidate dictionary needs a user call.
173	
174	> **Resolved (2026-05-30): Hybrid — candidate dictionary → per-sender rule template → interactive prompt**, caching the winning method per sender; password material never in the repo or logs. The rule-template config format and dictionary design are deferred to implementation (ROADMAP Milestone C.1). See [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./docs/ROADMAP.md) § 1.
175	
176	### 8.3 LLM locality, per document category
177	
178	The default is "local for sensitive, cloud allowed for non-sensitive after review." But which models? Ollama with Llama 3 / Qwen / Mistral on the user's hardware? A small fine-tuned model? Cloud (Anthropic / OpenAI / Gemini) with strict redaction for the non-sensitive categories?
179	
180	This decision is closely coupled to the user's hardware capacity (GPU? RAM?) and willingness to wait on inference latency.
181	
182	### 8.4 First-slice scope confirmation
183	
184	§ 7 proposes "Gmail + one bank → CLI". Confirm with user before building.
185	
186	> **Resolved (2026-05-30): Build both tracks in parallel** — the promo-corpus parse→store→query spine *and* the Gmail ingestion plumbing, as two decoupled tracks that converge at the first bank slice; first bank = **HDFC**. This refines § 7 (which proposed a single end-to-end bank slice first). See [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./docs/ROADMAP.md) §§ 1 and 4.
187	
188	### 8.5 Merchant canonicalization approach
189	
190	Fuzzy string matching has well-known failure modes on merchant names. Do we use a curated rules file (user maintains)? A library? A learned model? Defer until real data exposes the actual mess.
191	
192	## 9. Proposed (not built) file layout
193	
194	This is a *suggestion* for once the language decision is made. Don't materialize it prematurely.
195	
196	```
197	muneem/
198	├── README.md
199	├── CLAUDE.md
200	├── DESIGN.md
201	├── docs/
202	│   ├── prior-art-email-mining.md
203	│   └── (future: parser-notes-per-issuer.md, schema.md, threat-model.md)
204	├── testdata/
205	│   ├── promo-emails/         # non-sensitive corpus (in repo)
206	│   └── statements/           # GITIGNORED — real statements, never committed
207	├── (source tree TBD based on § 8.1)
208	└── (DB file, GITIGNORED)
209	```
210	
211	## 10. Glossary
212	
213	- **Muneem** (मुनीम) — traditional Indian household/business bookkeeper.
214	- **Issuer** — the bank, card network, broker, or merchant that produced a document.
215	- **Statement** — a periodic financial summary from an issuer (bank statement, CC bill, broker statement, MF report).
216	- **Holding** — a position in an investment account at a point in time.
217	- **Line item** — a single transaction or itemized purchase row within a statement or receipt.
218	- **Born-digital PDF** — a PDF generated from a software source with a real text layer (most bank statements). Contrast with scanned/image PDFs.
219	
220	## 11. Vision continuity from Email-Mining
221	
222	The predecessor project (`~/Projects/Email-Mining`) extracted promo codes and expiry dates from manually-saved promo email PDFs, using PyMuPDF for text+image extraction and Gemini for image OCR and summarization. Its scope was just "find the coupon I forgot about."
223	
224	muneem **subsumes** that scope. The deals/coupons feature survives as **one of several extraction targets** — one of the analytics surfaces, fed by the same five-stage pipeline that handles bank statements and everything else. The promo-email PDFs from that project are preserved in [`testdata/promo-emails/`](./testdata/promo-emails/) as the first regression corpus.
225	
226	What was learned there and worth keeping: the PDF text+image extraction *shape* is right (but PyMuPDF/fitz itself is AGPL-3.0 — muneem uses pdfminer.six (MIT) instead; see § 5 rule 8); image dedup by hash + SSIM is a real win for repeated chrome; the shape "page text + per-image OCR → unified text → structured extraction" generalizes well. What we change: programmatic Gmail/Outlook fetching instead of manual save-as-PDF; local extraction by default for sensitive categories; persistent normalized DB instead of one-shot stdout.
227	
228	Full details in [`docs/prior-art-email-mining.md`](./docs/prior-art-email-mining.md).
229

### 231. user (2026-05-31T09:16:22.128Z)

1	# Current State
2	
3	> **Last Updated:** 2026-06 (post tech-debt audit + ruff cleanup)
4	> This document summarizes the current capabilities, completed features, and near-term next steps for `muneem`.
5	> See [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) for the authoritative milestone tracking.
6	
7	## 1. Core Ingestion Pipeline
8	The ingestion pipeline supports a generic `SearchCriteria` interface and multiple providers (Track B plumbing):
9	
10	* **Gmail** (`muneem.ingestion.gmail`): Full OAuth2 read-only + search + attachment download implemented.
11	* **IMAP** (`muneem.ingestion.imap`): Basic auth + search implemented; `get_headers` / attachment methods are NotImplemented (experimental).
12	* **Outlook** (`muneem.ingestion.outlook`): Device-flow auth + basic search implemented; attachment/header/download NotImplemented (experimental).
13	
14	Companion one-time auth tools live in `tools/`. No high-level `muneem fetch` CLI command yet (see Roadmap B.4).
15	
16	## 2. Statement Parsers (Track A + C progress)
17	Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).
18	
19	* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). Extracts date, narration, value date, ref, withdrawal, deposit, closing balance. **Self-validating:** balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own end-of-statement SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Verified complete on real statements (37 & 31 txns) without inspecting any value.
20	* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings (`axis_transactions`) + Credit Card (`axis_cc_transactions`). ⚠️ The savings `extract_tables()` mapping is **unreliable on real Axis layouts** — column counts and date placement vary between statements (e.g. Jun vs Oct 2025), so it can mislabel rows. Reconciliation catches this, so as a **stop-gap the savings parser refuses to store rows that don't reconcile** (marks the document `unreconciled`, inserts 0) instead of persisting garbage. **Pending real fix: an HDFC-style coordinate-clustering rewrite** validated by total reconciliation (`opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing`). Credit-card has no running balance (parse/persist split + count only).
21	* **ICICI Bank** (`icici.py`): Transaction / savings statements (`icici_transactions`); now **mapped through the generic concept-mapping engine** (`engine.py`) driven by `ICICI_PROFILE`; **reconciles** + records status.
22

### 233. assistant (2026-05-31T09:17:19.884Z)

# Current State

> **Last Updated:** 2026-06 (concept-core engine + issuer refocus: HDFC / Axis / AU Bank)
> Summarizes current capabilities, completed features, and near-term next steps for `muneem`.
> See [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) for milestone tracking and [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md) for the parser design.

> **Scope reminder — muneem is a _whole-finance_ bookkeeper, not a bank tool.** Bank & credit-card
> statements are the first vertical slice; **equities, mutual funds, ETFs, and bonds are core
> targets**, parsed into the same local ledger, with transaction/instrument-event classification to
> follow. The parser core is deliberately **family-agnostic** (see §2), so adding an instrument
> family is *new data + a reconciliation rule*, not a new pipeline.

## 1. Core Ingestion Pipeline
The ingestion pipeline supports a generic `SearchCriteria` interface and multiple providers (Track B plumbing):

* **Gmail** (`muneem.ingestion.gmail`): Full OAuth2 read-only + search + attachment download implemented.
* **IMAP** (`muneem.ingestion.imap`): Basic auth + search implemented; `get_headers` / attachment methods are NotImplemented (experimental).
* **Outlook** (`muneem.ingestion.outlook`): Device-flow auth + basic search implemented; attachment/header/download NotImplemented (experimental).

Companion one-time auth tools live in `tools/`. No high-level `muneem fetch` CLI command yet (see Roadmap B.4).

## 2. Statement Parsers (concept-core architecture)
Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).

The parser core is **concept-first and verification-first** (see [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md)): an extractor maps an issuer's columns onto a bank-agnostic **concept schema** (`concepts.py`); a generic **engine** (`engine.py`) does the column→concept mapping (L0 header lexicon → profile column-order → L2 content search); and a shared **verifier** (`validation.py`) decides trust by *arithmetic* — per-row balance walk + total reconcile + SUMMARY bookend + a recorded confidence verdict. The engine is **family-agnostic**: parameterized by a `FamilySpec` + an injected reconciliation oracle, so a future **instrument family** (equity/MF/ETF/bond holdings + buy/sell/dividend/fee events) reuses the same control flow with its own concepts + oracle. Issuer knowledge lives in `profiles.py` as **data, not code**.

**Active issuers** — we have real statements + passwords, so these get validated on real data:

* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). **Self-validating:** running-balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Refactored onto the concept core as the **reference implementation**. ✅ **Verified on the user's 4 real statements** — all reconcile *and* bookend, without inspecting any value.
* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings + Credit Card. ⚠️ The old savings `extract_tables()` mapping was **unreliable across real layouts** (column counts and date placement vary between vintages — e.g. Jun vs Oct 2025). **Rewrite in progress (step 3):** HDFC-style **coordinate clustering → generic engine mapping → total reconcile** (`opening + ΣCr − ΣDr == closing`), oracle-gated so it can never persist unreconciled rows. Credit-card has no running balance (parse/persist split + count only).
* **AU Bank** (planned, step-3 sibling): engine-driven profile on the same coordinate-clustering + total-reconcile path as Axis. 4 real statements on hand.

> _Dropped: **ICICI** — no statements on hand to validate against, so it was removed to focus effort on the issuers we can actually verify (HDFC/Axis/AU). It can return as a profile when data exists._

All balance-bearing parsers share the verifier and split parsing from DB-insert, so row logic is unit-testable without a PDF/DB and a parse is validated before it's trusted. **Concept-core steps 1–2 have landed** (the verifier + concept schema; the generic mapping engine + profiles). Parsers use `pdfplumber` (with optional password). **Password resolution** (password_rules + hybrid unlock in `unlock.py`) is **not yet wired** into the main `muneem ingest --doc-type bank_statement` path; it is exercised via the `@pytest.mark.local_statements` tests on real (gitignored) statements.

See `password_rules.py` (prefers user config dir) and `cli.py:ingest`.

## 3. Database & CLI
* Schema v4 with `documents`, `offers`, and per-bank txn tables (`axis_*`, `hdfc_transactions`). Dedup on sha256. No versioned migration scripts yet (IF NOT EXISTS + user_version pragma). A unified `transactions` table (plus `holdings` for instruments) is planned once the per-bank schemas stabilize (parser-architecture.md §9).
* CLI: `ingest <pdf>` (promo or bank_statement via parser), `offers list`, `txns list <bank>`.
* Config uses platformdirs for local dirs; secrets via keyring.

## 4. Testing & Hygiene
* Strong offline regression on `testdata/promo-emails/` + synthetic parser/engine fixtures (default CI).
* Security guards in `test_repo_hygiene.py` + license allowlist test.
* Local-only tests for real statements (gitignored data; passwords via env/keyring, never committed).

## 5. Next Steps
Near-term: finish the **Axis + AU** engine-driven parsers (step 3) and precision-test them on real statements alongside HDFC; wire password unlock into the main ingest path; then harden (Milestone D). **Beyond banks (core scope, not backlog):** a second **instrument concept family** for broker / MF / ETF / bond statements → `holdings` + buy/sell/dividend/fee classification. Parked: DESIGN §8.3 (local LLM for sensitive categories), §8.5 (merchant canonicalization).

The project stays **local-first**, secrets in the OS keychain, promo corpus safe for CI/LLM experiments.

1	# muneem — Dependency policy & license allowlist
2	
3	muneem must be **safe to use commercially** and must **minimize paid dependencies**.
4	These are standing principles (DESIGN.md § 5, rules 8–9). This doc is the allowlist;
5	`tests/test_dependency_licenses.py` enforces it (a dependency not listed here fails CI).
6	
7	## Rules
8	
9	1. Only permissive, commercial-safe licenses: **MIT, BSD (2/3-clause), Apache-2.0, ISC, HPND**.
10	2. **Banned:** GPL, LGPL, **AGPL**, and source-available / non-commercial licenses.
11	   → **PyMuPDF / `fitz` is AGPL-3.0 — do not use it.** (Its own metadata reads
12	   *"Dual Licensed - GNU AFFERO GPL 3.0 or Artifex Commercial"*; the non-AGPL option
13	   is a *paid* license, which also violates rule 4.)
14	3. **MPL-2.0** (weak, file-level copyleft) only as a fallback when no permissive option
15	   exists, and flagged here.
16	4. Prefer **local / self-hosted / open-source** runtimes over paid APIs. Paid APIs
17	   (cloud LLMs, paid OCR/vision) are a **fallback only**, gated behind explicit approval.
18	   We are willing to **train our own** image/OCR models rather than take a paid dependency.
19	5. Adding a dependency requires updating **both** this table **and** the allowlist in
20	   `tests/test_dependency_licenses.py` — otherwise the build fails.
21	
22	## Current dependencies
23	
24	| Package | License | Role |
25	|---|---|---|
26	| typer | MIT | CLI |
27	| platformdirs | MIT* | OS config/data dirs |
28	| keyring | MIT | OS keychain (secrets) |
29	| pdfminer.six | MIT | PDF text extraction (replaces AGPL PyMuPDF) |
30	| pypdfium2 | Apache-2.0 / BSD-3 | PDF page rendering for OCR |
31	| pytesseract | Apache-2.0 | local OCR (Tesseract) wrapper |
32	| Pillow | HPND | image handling for OCR |
33	| pypdf | BSD-3-Clause | decrypt password-protected statements (Stage 2) |
34	| google-api-python-client | Apache-2.0 | Gmail API (read-only) |
35	| google-auth | Apache-2.0 | OAuth credentials |
36	| google-auth-oauthlib | Apache-2.0 | installed-app OAuth consent flow |
37	| msal | MIT | Microsoft Graph API OAuth |
38	| requests | Apache-2.0 | HTTP library for Outlook provider |
39	| pdfplumber | MIT | table extraction for statements (HDFC/ICICI/Axis) |
40	| pytest *(dev)* | MIT | tests |
41	| ruff *(dev)* | MIT | lint / format |

### 262. assistant (2026-05-31T09:21:41.222Z)

"""Coordinate clustering — turn positioned words into a clean cell-matrix.

When ``pdfplumber.extract_tables()`` mis-groups a semi-ruled or whitespace-aligned statement (the
Axis case: a date merged into the narration cell, column counts drifting between vintages), we fall
back to reading **word positions** directly. Words are grouped into visual rows by their ``top``,
column boundaries are inferred from the vertical whitespace shared across all rows, and each word is
dropped into the column its x-centre falls in. The result is a rectangular cell-matrix the generic
``engine`` can map — and because columns come from geometry, a leading date sits in its *own* column
instead of being glued to the narration.

This is bank-agnostic extraction (no issuer constants, no x-positions hard-coded); the engine's
oracle still decides whether the result is trustworthy. Pure geometry in, strings out — no value is
logged.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

Word = dict  # a pdfplumber word: {"text", "x0", "x1", "top", "bottom", ...}


def group_lines(words: Sequence[Word], tol: float = 2.5) -> list[list[Word]]:
    """Group words into visual lines by ``top`` (tolerant of sub-pixel jitter), left-to-right."""
    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
    anchor_top: float | None = None
    for w in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
        if lines and anchor_top is not None and abs(w["top"] - anchor_top) <= tol:
            lines[-1].append(w)
        else:
            lines.append([w])
            anchor_top = w["top"]
    return [sorted(line, key=lambda w: w["x0"]) for line in lines]


def infer_columns(words: Sequence[Word], gap: float = 10.0) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
    """Infer column x-extents by merging word x-intervals separated by less than ``gap`` points.

    The gaps wider than ``gap`` are the inter-column whitespace; everything else coalesces into a
    column. Returns ``(x_start, x_end)`` per column, left to right.
    """
    intervals = sorted((float(w["x0"]), float(w["x1"])) for w in words)
    merged: list[list[float]] = []
    for x0, x1 in intervals:
        if merged and x0 <= merged[-1][1] + gap:
            merged[-1][1] = max(merged[-1][1], x1)
        else:
            merged.append([x0, x1])
    return [(a, b) for a, b in merged]


def _column_of(center: float, columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> int:
    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(columns):
        if a <= center <= b:
            return i
    return min(
        range(len(columns)),
        key=lambda i: min(abs(center - columns[i][0]), abs(center - columns[i][1])),
    )


def line_to_cells(line: Sequence[Word], columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> list[str]:
    """Place each word into its column; join words that share a column (in x order)."""
    cells = ["" for _ in columns]
    for w in line:
        idx = _column_of((float(w["x0"]) + float(w["x1"])) / 2, columns)
        cells[idx] = (cells[idx] + " " + str(w["text"])).strip()
    return cells


def cluster_to_matrix(
    words_by_page: Sequence[Sequence[Word]], *, line_tol: float = 2.5, col_gap: float = 10.0
) -> list[list[str]]:
    """Cluster words from one or more pages into a single cell-matrix with consistent columns.

    Column boundaries are inferred **once across all pages** (a statement keeps its layout), so rows
    from every page land in the same columns; rows are emitted in page-then-top order.
    """
    all_words = [w for page in words_by_page for w in page]
    if not all_words:
        return []
    columns = infer_columns(all_words, col_gap)
    matrix: list[list[str]] = []
    for page in words_by_page:
        for line in group_lines(page, line_tol):
            matrix.append(line_to_cells(line, columns))
    return matrix


__all__ = ["group_lines", "infer_columns", "line_to_cells", "cluster_to_matrix"]

### 267. assistant (2026-05-31T09:23:32.551Z)

"""Axis Bank statement parsers: savings/transaction account + credit card.

**Savings now runs on the concept core.** Extraction proposes candidate cell-matrices — coordinate
clustering (whole statement, consistent columns; handles the date-merged / column-count drift across
vintages) and, as a fallback, ``extract_tables()`` — and the generic ``engine`` maps each onto the
canonical concepts. **Reconciliation disposes:** a parse is trusted only if the per-row balance walks
or (when there's no per-row balance, e.g. some vintages) the totals reconcile against the printed
``opening + ΣCr − ΣDr == closing``. The parser is **oracle-gated**: it persists only an endorsed
parse, so a layout it can't yet read stores nothing (status ``unreconciled``) rather than mislabeled
rows. This replaces the old fixed-column ``extract_tables()`` mapping that mislabeled rows.

Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so ``AxisCreditCardParser`` can't reconcile
(parse/persist split + count only). No statement content is logged here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import sqlite3
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

import pdfplumber

from muneem import db as dbmod

from . import register_parser
from .base import StatementParser
from .clustering import cluster_to_matrix
from .concepts import ConceptRow, StatementConcepts
from .engine import map_table
from .profiles import AXIS_PROFILE
from .validation import Confidence, parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance, reconcile_total

_CC_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}")  # Axis credit card: dd/mm/yyyy
_CARD_RES = [re.compile(r"(\d{4}XXXX\d{4})"), re.compile(r"(\d{6}\*+\d{4})")]
_OPENING_RE = re.compile(r"opening\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
_CLOSING_RE = re.compile(r"closing\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)


def _first_match(text: str, patterns: list[re.Pattern]) -> str | None:
    for rx in patterns:
        m = rx.search(text)
        if m:
            return m.group(1).strip()
    return None


# --- Savings / transaction account (concept core) ---------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class AxisSavings:
    """Outcome of a savings parse: concept rows + whether reconciliation endorsed them."""

    account_number: str
    rows: list[ConceptRow] = field(default_factory=list)
    verified: bool = False


def parse_axis_summary(text: str) -> StatementConcepts:
    """Read opening/closing balance from the statement text (for the total-reconcile oracle).

    Best-effort regex; ``None`` when not found (the oracle then relies on the per-row walk).
    """
    opening = _first_match(text, [_OPENING_RE])
    closing = _first_match(text, [_CLOSING_RE])
    return StatementConcepts(
        opening_balance=parse_amount(opening),
        closing_balance=parse_amount(closing),
    )


def _savings_oracle(opening: float | None, closing: float | None):
    """Trust a parse if the per-row balance walks, else if the totals reconcile to the bookends."""

    def check(rows: list[ConceptRow]) -> bool:
        if reconcile_running_balance([(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]).ok:
            return True
        if rows and opening is not None and closing is not None:
            return reconcile_total(rows, opening, closing)
        return False

    return check


def parse_axis_savings(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> AxisSavings:
    """Open an Axis savings statement and return concept rows + a reconciliation verdict.

    Tries a coordinate-clustered matrix (whole statement) first, then concatenated
    ``extract_tables()`` rows, and keeps the first mapping the reconciliation oracle endorses.
    """
    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
        words_by_page = [
            page.extract_words(use_text_flow=False, keep_blank_chars=False) for page in pdf.pages
        ]
        extracted = [row for page in pdf.pages for t in (page.extract_tables() or []) for row in t]
        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))

    account_number = AXIS_PROFILE.first_account(head) or "UNKNOWN"
    summary = parse_axis_summary(head)
    oracle = _savings_oracle(summary.opening_balance, summary.closing_balance)

    candidates = [cluster_to_matrix(words_by_page), extracted]
    best: AxisSavings | None = None
    for matrix in candidates:
        result = map_table(matrix, AXIS_PROFILE, oracle=oracle)
        if result is None:
            continue
        if result.verified:
            return AxisSavings(account_number, result.rows, True)
        if best is None:
            best = AxisSavings(account_number, result.rows, False)
    return best or AxisSavings(account_number, [], False)


@register_parser
class AxisParser(StatementParser):
    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
        if sender and "axisbank.com" in sender.lower():
            return True
        t = text or ""
        return "Axis Bank" in t and ("Statement of Account" in t or "Account Statement" in t)

    def parse_and_insert(
        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
    ) -> int:
        """Persist a savings parse **only if it reconciles**; otherwise store nothing.

        Sets ``documents.status`` to ``reconciled`` / ``unreconciled`` accordingly, so a layout the
        parser can't yet read surfaces loudly instead of saving mislabeled rows.
        """
        try:
            parsed = parse_axis_savings(pdf_path, password)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc

        if not parsed.verified:
            conn.execute(
                "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
                (Confidence.UNRECONCILED.value, doc_id),
            )
            return 0

        for r in parsed.rows:
            dbmod.insert_axis_transaction(
                conn,
                document_id=doc_id,
                account_number=parsed.account_number,
                txn_date=r.txn_date,
                value_date=r.value_date or "",
                particulars=r.narration,
                debit=r.debit,
                credit=r.credit,
                balance=r.balance,
            )
        conn.execute(
            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
            (Confidence.RECONCILED.value, doc_id),
        )
        return len(parsed.rows)


# --- Credit card (no running balance; count only) ---------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class AxisCCTxn:
    txn_date: str
    particulars: str
    merchant_category: str
    amount: float
    type: str  # "Cr" or "Dr"


def _rows_from_cc_tables(tables: list) -> list[AxisCCTxn]:
    """Build credit-card transactions from extracted tables. Pure: no PDF, no DB."""
    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
    for table in tables or []:
        for row in table or []:
            if not row or len(row) < 9:
                continue
            date_str = str(row[0]).strip() if row[0] else ""
            if not _CC_DATE_RE.match(date_str):
                continue
            amount_str = str(row[8]).strip() if row[8] else ""
            is_cr = "Cr" in amount_str
            amount = parse_amount(re.sub(r"[^\d.]", "", amount_str)) or 0.0
            rows.append(
                AxisCCTxn(
                    txn_date=date_str,
                    particulars=str(row[2]).strip() if row[2] else "",
                    merchant_category=str(row[7]).strip() if row[7] else "",
                    amount=amount,
                    type="Cr" if is_cr else "Dr",
                )
            )
    return rows


def parse_axis_cc(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[AxisCCTxn]]:
    """Open an Axis credit-card statement and return (card_number, transactions)."""
    card_number = "UNKNOWN"
    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
        card_number = _first_match(head, _CARD_RES) or "UNKNOWN"
        for page in pdf.pages:
            rows.extend(_rows_from_cc_tables(page.extract_tables()))
    return card_number, rows


@register_parser
class AxisCreditCardParser(StatementParser):
    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
        return "Axis Bank" in text and "Credit Card Statement" in text

    def parse_and_insert(
        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
    ) -> int:
        try:
            card_number, rows = parse_axis_cc(pdf_path, password)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis CC statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
        for t in rows:
            dbmod.insert_axis_cc_transaction(
                conn,
                document_id=doc_id,
                account_number=card_number,
                txn_date=t.txn_date,
                particulars=t.particulars,
                merchant_category=t.merchant_category,
                amount=t.amount,
                type=t.type,
            )
        # Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so reconciliation does not apply;
        # documents.status is left as set by the caller.
        return len(rows)

### 275. assistant (2026-05-31T09:27:15.624Z)

The clustering's `infer_columns` saturates because it projects *all* 169 lines (headers, address, summary), where transactions are a tiny fraction. The fix: infer columns from **transaction-like lines only** (date + money token), and use a **coverage histogram** so an occasional wide line can't collapse columns. Let me rewrite the clustering with a row-filter and coverage-based column detection:

### 276. assistant (2026-05-31T09:27:36.640Z)

"""Coordinate clustering — turn positioned words into a clean cell-matrix.

When ``pdfplumber.extract_tables()`` mis-groups a semi-ruled or whitespace-aligned statement (the
Axis case: a date merged into the narration cell, column counts drifting between vintages), we read
**word positions** directly. Words are grouped into visual rows by their ``top``; column boundaries
are inferred from the vertical whitespace **shared across the transaction rows**; and each word is
dropped into the column its x-centre falls in. Because columns come from geometry, a leading date
sits in its *own* column instead of being glued to the narration.

Two robustness lessons baked in:

* **Infer columns from transaction-like rows only.** Headers, address blocks, and summaries span the
  page and would erase the inter-column whitespace. A caller-supplied ``keep_line`` predicate (e.g.
  "has a date *and* a money token") restricts both column inference and the emitted matrix to the
  transaction band.
* **Use a coverage histogram, not interval-merging.** A column boundary is an x-channel that is empty
  in *most* rows; a single long narration can't collapse two columns.

Bank-agnostic extraction (no issuer constants, no x-positions hard-coded); the engine's oracle still
decides whether the result is trustworthy. Pure geometry in, strings out — no value is logged.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence

Word = dict  # a pdfplumber word: {"text", "x0", "x1", "top", "bottom", ...}


def group_lines(words: Sequence[Word], tol: float = 2.5) -> list[list[Word]]:
    """Group words into visual lines by ``top`` (tolerant of sub-pixel jitter), left-to-right."""
    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
    anchor_top: float | None = None
    for w in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
        if lines and anchor_top is not None and abs(w["top"] - anchor_top) <= tol:
            lines[-1].append(w)
        else:
            lines.append([w])
            anchor_top = w["top"]
    return [sorted(line, key=lambda w: w["x0"]) for line in lines]


def infer_columns(
    lines: Sequence[Sequence[Word]], bin_w: float = 1.0, min_frac: float = 0.3
) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
    """Infer column x-extents from a coverage histogram over ``lines``.

    An x-bin is "filled" if at least ``min_frac`` of the lines have a word covering it; columns are
    the runs of filled bins, and the empty runs between them are the inter-column whitespace. Robust
    to a few full-width lines because it thresholds on the *fraction* of rows, not on any single one.
    """
    words = [w for line in lines for w in line]
    if not words:
        return []
    x0 = min(float(w["x0"]) for w in words)
    x1 = max(float(w["x1"]) for w in words)
    nbins = max(1, int((x1 - x0) / bin_w) + 1)
    cover = [0] * nbins
    for line in lines:
        marked: set[int] = set()
        for w in line:
            a = int((float(w["x0"]) - x0) / bin_w)
            b = int((float(w["x1"]) - x0) / bin_w)
            marked.update(range(max(0, a), min(nbins - 1, b) + 1))
        for k in marked:
            cover[k] += 1
    thresh = max(1.0, min_frac * len(lines))
    filled = [c >= thresh for c in cover]
    cols: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
    start: int | None = None
    for i, f in enumerate(filled):
        if f and start is None:
            start = i
        elif not f and start is not None:
            cols.append((x0 + start * bin_w, x0 + i * bin_w))
            start = None
    if start is not None:
        cols.append((x0 + start * bin_w, x0 + nbins * bin_w))
    return cols


def _column_of(center: float, columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> int:
    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(columns):
        if a <= center <= b:
            return i
    return min(
        range(len(columns)),
        key=lambda i: min(abs(center - columns[i][0]), abs(center - columns[i][1])),
    )


def line_to_cells(line: Sequence[Word], columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> list[str]:
    """Place each word into its column; join words that share a column (in x order)."""
    cells = ["" for _ in columns]
    for w in line:
        idx = _column_of((float(w["x0"]) + float(w["x1"])) / 2, columns)
        cells[idx] = (cells[idx] + " " + str(w["text"])).strip()
    return cells


def cluster_to_matrix(
    words_by_page: Sequence[Sequence[Word]],
    *,
    line_tol: float = 2.5,
    min_frac: float = 0.3,
    keep_line: Callable[[list[str]], bool] | None = None,
) -> list[list[str]]:
    """Cluster words across pages into one cell-matrix with consistent columns.

    ``keep_line`` (texts of a line → bool) restricts both column inference and the output to the
    transaction band — without it, full-width headers/footers swamp the column structure. Columns
    are inferred once across all kept lines (a statement keeps its layout); rows are emitted in
    page-then-top order.
    """
    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
    for page in words_by_page:
        lines.extend(group_lines(page, line_tol))
    if keep_line is not None:
        lines = [ln for ln in lines if keep_line([str(w["text"]) for w in ln])]
    if not lines:
        return []
    columns = infer_columns(lines, min_frac=min_frac)
    if not columns:
        return []
    return [line_to_cells(line, columns) for line in lines]


__all__ = ["group_lines", "infer_columns", "line_to_cells", "cluster_to_matrix"]

### 293. assistant (2026-05-31T09:34:07.898Z)

"""Tests for the Axis parsers (savings via the concept core + credit card).

Savings runs end-to-end on synthetic data through a pdfplumber mock (extract_tables / extract_text /
extract_words), so the per-table → engine → reconcile path is exercised without a real PDF;
``parse_and_insert``'s store-iff-reconciled gate is checked by monkeypatching ``parse_axis_savings``.
Real encrypted statements run under the ``local_statements`` marker (test_local_axis.py).
"""

from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

import muneem.db as dbmod
from muneem.parsers import axis
from muneem.parsers.axis import AxisCreditCardParser, AxisParser, AxisSavings, parse_axis_summary
from muneem.parsers.concepts import ConceptRow


def test_can_handle():
    assert AxisParser().can_handle("x", sender="alerts@axisbank.com")
    assert AxisParser().can_handle("Axis Bank Statement of Account")
    assert AxisParser().can_handle("Axis Bank Account Statement")  # the real wording on statements
    assert not AxisParser().can_handle("HDFC Bank")
    assert AxisCreditCardParser().can_handle("Axis Bank Credit Card Statement")
    assert not AxisCreditCardParser().can_handle("Axis Bank Statement of Account")


def test_parse_axis_summary_reads_opening_closing():
    s = parse_axis_summary("Opening Balance 1,000.00\n...rows...\nClosing Balance 2,500.50")
    assert s.opening_balance == 1000.0 and s.closing_balance == 2500.5


def test_is_txn_line():
    assert axis._is_txn_line(["01-02-2026", "UPI/Zomato", "500.00", "", "9500.00"])
    assert not axis._is_txn_line(["Tran Date", "Transaction", "Withdrawals", "Balance"])  # header
    assert not axis._is_txn_line(["01-02-2026", "narration but no amount"])  # date, no money


def _run_savings(page_tables, text="", page_words=None):
    """Drive parse_axis_savings against a single synthetic page via a pdfplumber mock."""
    page = MagicMock()
    page.extract_tables.return_value = page_tables
    page.extract_text.return_value = text
    page.extract_words.return_value = page_words or []
    pdf = MagicMock()
    pdf.pages = [page]
    with patch("pdfplumber.open") as m:
        m.return_value.__enter__.return_value = pdf
        return axis.parse_axis_savings(Path("x.pdf"), password="")


def test_savings_per_row_reconcile_via_tables():
    table = [
        ["Tran Date", "Transaction", "Withdrawals", "Deposits", "Balance"],
        ["01-02-2026", "UPI Zomato", "500.00", "", "9500.00"],
        ["02-02-2026", "Salary", "", "2000.00", "11500.00"],
        ["03-02-2026", "ATM", "1500.50", "", "9999.50"],
    ]
    r = _run_savings([table])
    assert r.verified and len(r.rows) == 3
    assert r.rows[0].debit == 500.0 and r.rows[0].balance == 9500.0
    assert r.rows[1].credit == 2000.0


def test_savings_total_reconcile_when_no_per_row_balance():
    # No Balance column — reconciles only against the printed opening/closing totals (Oct vintage).
    table = [
        ["Tran Date", "Transaction", "Withdrawals", "Deposits"],
        ["01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", ""],
        ["02-02-2026", "Salary", "", "2000.00"],
    ]
    r = _run_savings([table], text="Opening Balance 1,000.00 ... Closing Balance 2,500.00")
    assert r.verified and len(r.rows) == 2
    assert r.rows[0].debit == 500.0 and r.rows[0].balance is None


def test_savings_refuses_when_unreconciled():
    table = [
        ["Tran Date", "Transaction", "Withdrawals", "Deposits", "Balance"],
        ["01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", "", "9500.00"],
        ["02-02-2026", "Salary", "", "2000.00", "1.00"],  # balance breaks the walk
    ]
    r = _run_savings([table])
    assert not r.verified


def test_parse_and_insert_stores_when_verified(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    rows = [ConceptRow(txn_date="01-02-2026", narration="UPI", debit=500.0, balance=9500.0)]
    monkeypatch.setattr(axis, "parse_axis_savings", lambda *a, **k: AxisSavings("ACC1", rows, True))
    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
        dbmod.init_db(conn)
        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256="ax1", status="parsed")
        n = AxisParser().parse_and_insert(Path("x.pdf"), conn, doc_id)
        assert n == 1
        stored = dbmod.list_axis_transactions(conn)
        assert len(stored) == 1 and stored[0]["account_number"] == "ACC1" and stored[0]["debit"] == 500.0
        assert dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, "ax1")["status"] == "reconciled"


def test_parse_and_insert_refuses_when_unverified(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    rows = [ConceptRow(txn_date="01-02-2026", narration="UPI", debit=500.0, balance=1.0)]
    monkeypatch.setattr(axis, "parse_axis_savings", lambda *a, **k: AxisSavings("ACC1", rows, False))
    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
        dbmod.init_db(conn)
        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256="ax2", status="parsed")
        n = AxisParser().parse_and_insert(Path("x.pdf"), conn, doc_id)
        assert n == 0
        assert dbmod.list_axis_transactions(conn) == []
        assert dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, "ax2")["status"] == "unreconciled"


def test_rows_from_cc_tables():
    tables = [
        [
            ["Date", "c1", "Particulars", "c3", "c4", "c5", "c6", "Category", "Amount"],
            ["05/02/2026", "x", "Swiggy", "", "", "", "", "Food", "250.00 Dr"],
            ["06/02/2026", "x", "Refund", "", "", "", "", "Food", "100.00 Cr"],
            ["bad", "too", "short"],  # < 9 cols -> skipped
        ]
    ]
    rows = axis._rows_from_cc_tables(tables)
    assert len(rows) == 2
    assert rows[0].amount == 250.0 and rows[0].type == "Dr" and rows[0].merchant_category == "Food"
    assert rows[1].amount == 100.0 and rows[1].type == "Cr"

### 296. assistant (2026-05-31T09:34:24.657Z)

"""Tests for coordinate clustering (muneem.parsers.clustering).

Synthetic word geometry only — proves lines group by ``top``, columns come from the transaction
band (a full-width header must not collapse them), and a single wide cell can't merge two columns.
"""

from muneem.parsers.clustering import cluster_to_matrix, group_lines, infer_columns


def W(text, x0, top, x1=None):
    """A minimal pdfplumber-style word dict."""
    return {
        "text": text,
        "x0": float(x0),
        "x1": float(x1 if x1 is not None else x0 + 5 * len(text)),
        "top": float(top),
        "bottom": float(top) + 8,
    }


def _has_date(texts):
    return any(t.count("-") == 2 for t in texts)


def _txn_row(date, narr, amt, bal, top):
    return [W(date, 40, top, 80), W(narr, 110, top, 230), W(amt, 300, top, 340), W(bal, 420, top, 470)]


def test_group_lines_clusters_by_top():
    words = [W("a", 10, 100), W("b", 60, 101), W("c", 10, 130)]
    lines = group_lines(words)
    assert len(lines) == 2
    assert [w["text"] for w in lines[0]] == ["a", "b"]


def test_cluster_keeps_only_txn_lines_and_finds_columns():
    words = [W("ACCOUNT STATEMENT", 40, 50, 520)]  # full-width header — dropped by keep_line
    words += _txn_row("01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", "9500.00", 100)
    words += _txn_row("02-02-2026", "Salary", "2000.00", "11500.00", 120)
    m = cluster_to_matrix([words], keep_line=_has_date)
    assert len(m) == 2  # the header line is excluded
    assert all(len(row) == 4 for row in m)  # date | narration | amount | balance
    assert m[0][0] == "01-02-2026" and m[0][3] == "9500.00"


def test_one_wide_cell_does_not_collapse_columns():
    # A single long narration spanning toward the amount column must not merge the two columns;
    # the coverage histogram thresholds on the fraction of rows, so one outlier can't.
    words = _txn_row("01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", "9500.00", 100)
    words += _txn_row("02-02-2026", "Salary", "2000.00", "11500.00", 120)
    words += [W("01-02-2026", 40, 140, 80), W("a very long narration line here", 110, 140, 295),
              W("750.00", 300, 140, 340), W("9000.00", 420, 140, 470)]
    m = cluster_to_matrix([words], keep_line=_has_date)
    assert len(m) == 3
    assert all(len(row) == 4 for row in m)


def test_infer_columns_returns_runs():
    lines = [_txn_row("01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", "9500.00", 100)]
    cols = infer_columns(lines, min_frac=0.3)
    assert len(cols) == 4

### 323. assistant (2026-05-31T09:39:04.351Z)

"""Shared engine-driven savings-statement parsing (Axis, AU Bank, and future issuers).

Every issuer whose statement is a transaction table follows the same recipe, so it lives here once
and each bank supplies only a :class:`~muneem.parsers.engine.Profile`:

1. **Extraction proposes** candidate cell-matrices — one per ``extract_tables()`` table, plus a
   coordinate-clustered whole-statement matrix (for layouts ``extract_tables`` mis-segments).
2. The generic **engine** maps each candidate's columns onto the canonical concepts.
3. **Reconciliation disposes:** a parse is trusted only if its per-row balance walks, or — when a
   vintage has no per-row balance — the totals reconcile to the printed ``opening``/``closing``.

A statement splits its ledger across several extracted tables (page breaks), so we map each table
on its own, keep the rows the oracle endorses, and re-check the *combined* set (which catches a
cross-table balance break, i.e. a missing transaction). The result is **oracle-gated**: a layout we
can't yet read returns ``verified=False`` and the caller persists nothing — never mislabeled rows.

No statement content is logged; only counts/booleans travel out.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

import pdfplumber

from .clustering import cluster_to_matrix
from .concepts import ConceptRow, StatementConcepts
from .engine import Profile, map_table
from .validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance, reconcile_total

_MONEY_TOKEN = re.compile(r"^-?[\d,]+\.\d{2}(?:cr|dr)?$", re.IGNORECASE)
_OPENING_RE = re.compile(r"opening\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
_CLOSING_RE = re.compile(r"closing\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)


@dataclass
class SavingsParse:
    """Outcome of a savings parse: concept rows + whether reconciliation endorsed them."""

    account_number: str
    rows: list[ConceptRow] = field(default_factory=list)
    verified: bool = False


def parse_statement_summary(text: str) -> StatementConcepts:
    """Read opening/closing balance from statement text (for the total-reconcile oracle).

    Best-effort regex; ``None`` when not found — the oracle then relies on the per-row walk.
    """
    opening = _OPENING_RE.search(text)
    closing = _CLOSING_RE.search(text)
    return StatementConcepts(
        opening_balance=parse_amount(opening.group(1)) if opening else None,
        closing_balance=parse_amount(closing.group(1)) if closing else None,
    )


def txn_line_predicate(profile: Profile):
    """A line is transaction-like if it carries one of the profile's dates *and* a money token.

    Used to restrict coordinate clustering to the transaction band (drops headers / summaries /
    address blocks that would otherwise wreck column inference).
    """
    patterns = profile.date_patterns

    def keep(texts: list[str]) -> bool:
        has_date = any(any(p.match(t) for p in patterns) for t in texts)
        return has_date and any(_MONEY_TOKEN.match(t) for t in texts)

    return keep


def reconcile_oracle(opening: float | None, closing: float | None):
    """Trust a parse if the per-row balance walks, else if the totals reconcile to the bookends."""

    def check(rows: list[ConceptRow]) -> bool:
        if reconcile_running_balance([(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]).ok:
            return True
        if rows and opening is not None and closing is not None:
            return reconcile_total(rows, opening, closing)
        return False

    return check


def parse_savings(pdf_path: Path, profile: Profile, password: str | None = None) -> SavingsParse:
    """Parse a savings statement into concept rows + a reconciliation verdict, driven by ``profile``.

    Per-table mapping first (each extracted table mapped on its own; oracle-endorsed rows combined
    and re-checked), then a coordinate-clustering fallback. Returns ``verified=False`` if neither
    reconciles, so the caller can refuse to persist.
    """
    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
        words_by_page = [
            page.extract_words(use_text_flow=False, keep_blank_chars=False) for page in pdf.pages
        ]
        tables = [t for page in pdf.pages for t in (page.extract_tables() or [])]
        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))

    account_number = profile.first_account(head) or "UNKNOWN"
    summary = parse_statement_summary(head)
    oracle = reconcile_oracle(summary.opening_balance, summary.closing_balance)

    confirmed: list[ConceptRow] = []
    for table in tables:
        res = map_table(table, profile, oracle=oracle)
        if res and res.verified:
            confirmed.extend(res.rows)
    if confirmed and oracle(confirmed):
        return SavingsParse(account_number, confirmed, True)

    clustered = map_table(
        cluster_to_matrix(words_by_page, keep_line=txn_line_predicate(profile)),
        profile,
        oracle=oracle,
    )
    if clustered and clustered.verified:
        return SavingsParse(account_number, clustered.rows, True)

    fallback = confirmed or (clustered.rows if clustered else [])
    return SavingsParse(account_number, fallback, False)


__all__ = [
    "SavingsParse",
    "parse_savings",
    "parse_statement_summary",
    "txn_line_predicate",
    "reconcile_oracle",
]

1	"""Axis Bank statement parsers: savings/transaction account + credit card.
2	
3	**Savings now runs on the concept core.** Extraction proposes candidate cell-matrices — coordinate
4	clustering (whole statement, consistent columns; handles the date-merged / column-count drift across
5	vintages) and, as a fallback, ``extract_tables()`` — and the generic ``engine`` maps each onto the
6	canonical concepts. **Reconciliation disposes:** a parse is trusted only if its balance walks
7	or (when there's no per-row balance, e.g. some vintages) the totals reconcile against the printed
8	``opening + ΣCr − ΣDr == closing``. The parser is **oracle-gated**: it persists only an endorsed
9	parse, so a layout it can't yet read stores nothing (status ``unreconciled``) rather than mislabeled
10	rows. This replaces the old fixed-column ``extract_tables()`` mapping that mislabeled rows.
11	
12	Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so ``AxisCreditCardParser`` can't reconcile
13	(parse/persist split + count only). No statement content is logged here.
14	"""

17	
18	import re
19	import sqlite3
20	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
21	from pathlib import Path
22	
23	import pdfplumber
24

28	from .base import StatementParser

30	from .concepts import ConceptRow, StatementConcepts
31	from .engine import map_table
32	from .profiles import AXIS_PROFILE
33	from .validation import Confidence, parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance, reconcile_total
34	
35	_CC_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}")  # Axis credit card: dd/mm/yyyy
36	_CARD_RES = [re.compile(r"(\d{4}XXXX\d{4})"), re.compile(r"(\d{6}\*+\d{4})")]
37	_OPENING_RE = re.compile(r"opening\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
38	_CLOSING_RE = re.compile(r"closing\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
39	_SAVINGS_DATE = re.compile(r"^\d{2}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{4}$")  # dd-mm-yyyy or dd/mm/yyyy
40	_MONEY_TOKEN = re.compile(r"^-?[\d,]+\.\d{2}(?:cr|dr)?$", re.IGNORECASE)
41	
42	
43	def _is_txn_line(texts: list[str]) -> bool:
44	    """A transaction row carries a date *and* a money amount (drops headers/summaries/address)."""
45	    return any(_SAVINGS_DATE.match(t) for t in texts) and any(_MONEY_TOKEN.match(t) for t in texts)

47	
48	def _first_match(text: str, patterns: list[re.Pattern]) -> str | None:
49	    for rx in patterns:
50	        m = rx.search(text)
51	        if m:
52	            return m.group(1).strip()
53	    return None

68	def parse_axis_summary(text: str) -> StatementConcepts:

72	    """

77	        closing_balance=parse_amount(closing),

79

81	def _savings_oracle(opening: float | None, closing: float | None):
82	    """Trust a parse if the per-row balance walks, else if the totals reconcile to the bookends."""
83	
84	    def check(rows: list[ConceptRow]) -> bool:
85	        if reconcile_running_balance([(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]).ok:
86	            return True
87	        if rows and opening is not None and closing is not None:
88	            return reconcile_total(rows, opening, closing)
89	        return False
90	
91	    return check

98	    ``extract_tables()`` rows, and keeps the first mapping the reconciliation oracle endorses.

101	        words_by_page = [

103	        ]

110	
111	    # Strategy 1 — per-table. A statement splits its ledger across several extracted tables (page
112	    # breaks); concatenating them all misaligns columns. Instead map each table on its own and keep
113	    # the rows from tables the oracle endorses, then re-check the *combined* set (catches a cross-
114	    # table balance break). This is "extraction proposes, reconciliation disposes" at table scope.

116	    for table in tables:

118	        if res and res.verified:
119	            confirmed.extend(res.rows)
120	    if confirmed and oracle(confirmed):
121	        return AxisSavings(account_number, confirmed, True)
122

124	    # mis-segments, e.g. a date merged into the narration cell).

### 346. assistant (2026-05-31T09:43:13.524Z)

"""AU Small Finance Bank savings / transaction-account statement parser.

Runs on the **shared engine-driven path** (:mod:`muneem.parsers.savings`): per-table +
coordinate-clustered candidates → generic engine mapping → reconciliation oracle, **oracle-gated** so
a vintage it can't read stores nothing rather than mislabeled rows. The AU specifics are entirely in
``AUBANK_PROFILE`` — this parser is mostly the DB wiring. No statement content is logged here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

from muneem import db as dbmod

from . import register_parser
from .base import StatementParser
from .profiles import AUBANK_PROFILE
from .savings import SavingsParse, parse_savings
from .validation import Confidence


def parse_au_savings(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> SavingsParse:
    """Parse an AU Bank savings statement via the shared engine-driven path."""
    return parse_savings(pdf_path, AUBANK_PROFILE, password)


@register_parser
class AUParser(StatementParser):
    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
        if sender and "aubank" in sender.lower():
            return True
        low = (text or "").lower()
        return "au small finance bank" in low or "aubank" in low

    def parse_and_insert(
        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
    ) -> int:
        """Persist a savings parse **only if it reconciles**; otherwise store nothing."""
        try:
            parsed = parse_au_savings(pdf_path, password)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse AU statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc

        if not parsed.verified:
            conn.execute(
                "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
                (Confidence.UNRECONCILED.value, doc_id),
            )
            return 0

        for r in parsed.rows:
            dbmod.insert_au_transaction(
                conn,
                document_id=doc_id,
                account_number=parsed.account_number,
                txn_date=r.txn_date,
                value_date=r.value_date or "",
                particulars=r.narration,
                debit=r.debit,
                credit=r.credit,
                balance=r.balance,
            )
        conn.execute(
            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
            (Confidence.RECONCILED.value, doc_id),
        )
        return len(parsed.rows)

### 348. assistant (2026-05-31T09:43:30.421Z)

"""Tests for the AU Bank parser.

AU runs on the shared engine-driven savings path (covered in depth by test_axis / test_engine), so
here we check AU detection, an end-to-end per-row reconcile via a pdfplumber mock, and the
store-iff-reconciled gate. Real statements run under local_statements (test_local_au.py).
"""

from pathlib import Path
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

import muneem.db as dbmod
from muneem.parsers import au
from muneem.parsers.au import AUParser, parse_au_savings
from muneem.parsers.concepts import ConceptRow
from muneem.parsers.savings import SavingsParse


def test_can_handle():
    assert AUParser().can_handle("...customercare@aubank.in...")
    assert AUParser().can_handle("AU Small Finance Bank Statement")
    assert AUParser().can_handle("anything", sender="statements@aubank.in")
    assert not AUParser().can_handle("HDFC Bank savings account details")
    assert not AUParser().can_handle("Axis Bank Statement of Account")


def test_parse_au_savings_per_row_reconcile():
    table = [
        ["Date", "Transaction", "Debit", "Credit", "Balance"],
        ["01-02-2026", "UPI", "500.00", "", "9500.00"],
        ["02-02-2026", "Salary", "", "2000.00", "11500.00"],
    ]
    page = MagicMock()
    page.extract_tables.return_value = [table]
    page.extract_text.return_value = "aubank statement"
    page.extract_words.return_value = []
    pdf = MagicMock()
    pdf.pages = [page]
    with patch("pdfplumber.open") as m:
        m.return_value.__enter__.return_value = pdf
        r = parse_au_savings(Path("x.pdf"), password="")
    assert r.verified and len(r.rows) == 2
    assert r.rows[0].debit == 500.0 and r.rows[1].credit == 2000.0


def test_parse_and_insert_stores_when_verified(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    rows = [ConceptRow(txn_date="01-02-2026", narration="UPI", debit=500.0, balance=9500.0)]
    monkeypatch.setattr(au, "parse_au_savings", lambda *a, **k: SavingsParse("AU1", rows, True))
    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
        dbmod.init_db(conn)
        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256="au1", status="parsed")
        n = AUParser().parse_and_insert(Path("x.pdf"), conn, doc_id)
        assert n == 1
        stored = dbmod.list_au_transactions(conn)
        assert len(stored) == 1 and stored[0]["account_number"] == "AU1" and stored[0]["debit"] == 500.0
        assert dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, "au1")["status"] == "reconciled"


def test_parse_and_insert_refuses_when_unverified(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    rows = [ConceptRow(txn_date="01-02-2026", narration="UPI", debit=500.0, balance=1.0)]
    monkeypatch.setattr(au, "parse_au_savings", lambda *a, **k: SavingsParse("AU1", rows, False))
    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
        dbmod.init_db(conn)
        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256="au2", status="parsed")
        n = AUParser().parse_and_insert(Path("x.pdf"), conn, doc_id)
        assert n == 0
        assert dbmod.list_au_transactions(conn) == []
        assert dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, "au2")["status"] == "unreconciled"

### 350. assistant (2026-05-31T09:43:50.654Z)

"""Local-only checks for the AU Bank parser against real encrypted statements.

Skipped unless an AU password is available (``password_rules.yaml`` or ``MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW``); never
runs in CI. The parser is **oracle-gated**, so this asserts the safety invariant: it either stored
nothing (a vintage it can't yet read) or everything it stored reconciled — never mislabeled rows.
Surfaces counts / booleans / parser names only — no statement content.
"""

import os
from pathlib import Path

import pdfplumber
import pytest

from muneem import db as dbmod
from muneem.parsers import get_parser
from muneem.password_rules import get_password_for_bank

AU_PDFS = sorted(Path("testdata/aubank-statements").glob("*.pdf"))


@pytest.mark.local_statements
@pytest.mark.parametrize("pdf_path", AU_PDFS, ids=lambda p: p.name)
def test_local_au_never_stores_unreconciled(pdf_path, tmp_path):
    pw = (
        get_password_for_bank("aubank")
        or get_password_for_bank("au")
        or os.environ.get("MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW")
    )
    if not pw:
        pytest.skip("needs the AU password (password_rules.yaml or MUNEEM_TEST_AU_PW)")

    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=pw) as pdf:
        text = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(2, len(pdf.pages))))
    parser = get_parser(text)
    if parser is None or type(parser).__name__ != "AUParser":
        pytest.skip(f"{pdf_path.name}: not routed to AUParser")

    with dbmod.connect(tmp_path / "t.db") as conn:
        dbmod.init_db(conn)
        doc_id = dbmod.insert_document(conn, source="file", sha256=pdf_path.name, status="parsed")
        n = parser.parse_and_insert(pdf_path, conn, doc_id, password=pw)
        status = dbmod.get_document_by_sha(conn, pdf_path.name)["status"]
        stored = dbmod.list_au_transactions(conn)

    assert (n == 0 and stored == []) or status == "reconciled", (
        f"{pdf_path.name}: stored {n} row(s) with status={status} — must not persist unreconciled"
    )

21	
22	## 2. Statement Parsers (concept-core architecture)
23	Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).
24	
25	The parser core is **concept-first and verification-first** (see [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md)): an extractor maps an issuer's columns onto a bank-agnostic **concept schema** (`concepts.py`); a generic **engine** (`engine.py`) does the column→concept mapping (L0 header lexicon → profile column-order → L2 content search); and a shared **verifier** (`validation.py`) decides trust by *arithmetic* — per-row balance walk + total reconcile + SUMMARY bookend + a recorded confidence verdict. The engine is **family-agnostic**: parameterized by a `FamilySpec` + an injected reconciliation oracle, so a future **instrument family** (equity/MF/ETF/bond holdings + buy/sell/dividend/fee events) reuses the same control flow with its own concepts + oracle. Issuer knowledge lives in `profiles.py` as **data, not code**.
26	
27	**Active issuers** — we have real statements + passwords, so these get validated on real data:
28	
29	* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). **Self-validating:** running-balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Refactored onto the concept core as the **reference implementation**. ✅ **Verified on the user's 4 real statements** — all reconcile *and* bookend, without inspecting any value.
30	* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings + Credit Card. ⚠️ The old savings `extract_tables()` mapping was **unreliable across real layouts** (column counts and date placement vary between vintages — e.g. Jun vs Oct 2025). **Rewrite in progress (step 3):** HDFC-style **coordinate clustering → generic engine mapping → total reconcile** (`opening + ΣCr − ΣDr == closing`), oracle-gated so it can never persist unreconciled rows. Credit-card has no running balance (parse/persist split + count only).
31	* **AU Bank** (planned, step-3 sibling): engine-driven profile on the same coordinate-clustering + total-reconcile path as Axis. 4 real statements on hand.
32	
33	> _Dropped: **ICICI** — no statements on hand to validate against, so it was removed to focus effort on the issuers we can actually verify (HDFC/Axis/AU). It can return as a profile when data exists._

| Issuer | Result | Transactions parsed |
|---|---|---|

**10 of 12 real statements fully reconcile.** The 2 Axis misses aren't wrong data — `extract_tables` mangles those vintages into an *incomplete* ledger, the total-reconcile oracle catches it, and they **store nothing** rather than mislabeled rows. The safety invariant is verified on all 12. HDFC's are the strongest (they also bookend against the printed Dr/Cr counts + opening/closing).
