### 382. user (2026-05-31T09:50:08.547Z)

1	# Current State
2	
3	> **Last Updated:** 2026-06 (concept-core engine + issuer refocus: HDFC / Axis / AU Bank)
4	> Summarizes current capabilities, completed features, and near-term next steps for `muneem`.
5	> See [`ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) for milestone tracking and [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md) for the parser design.
6	
7	> **Scope reminder — muneem is a _whole-finance_ bookkeeper, not a bank tool.** Bank & credit-card
8	> statements are the first vertical slice; **equities, mutual funds, ETFs, and bonds are core
9	> targets**, parsed into the same local ledger, with transaction/instrument-event classification to
10	> follow. The parser core is deliberately **family-agnostic** (see §2), so adding an instrument
11	> family is *new data + a reconciliation rule*, not a new pipeline.
12	
13	## 1. Core Ingestion Pipeline
14	The ingestion pipeline supports a generic `SearchCriteria` interface and multiple providers (Track B plumbing):
15	
16	* **Gmail** (`muneem.ingestion.gmail`): Full OAuth2 read-only + search + attachment download implemented.
17	* **IMAP** (`muneem.ingestion.imap`): Basic auth + search implemented; `get_headers` / attachment methods are NotImplemented (experimental).
18	* **Outlook** (`muneem.ingestion.outlook`): Device-flow auth + basic search implemented; attachment/header/download NotImplemented (experimental).
19	
20	Companion one-time auth tools live in `tools/`. No high-level `muneem fetch` CLI command yet (see Roadmap B.4).
21	
22	## 2. Statement Parsers (concept-core architecture)
23	Deterministic parsers live under `muneem.parsers`, registered via decorator and selected by `get_parser` on text heuristics + sender. Submodules are **auto-loaded on package import** (`_autoload_parsers`), so the registry is populated in the production path (not just under tests).
24	
25	The parser core is **concept-first and verification-first** (see [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md)): an extractor maps an issuer's columns onto a bank-agnostic **concept schema** (`concepts.py`); a generic **engine** (`engine.py`) does the column→concept mapping (L0 header lexicon → profile column-order → L2 content search); and a shared **verifier** (`validation.py`) decides trust by *arithmetic* — per-row balance walk + total reconcile + SUMMARY bookend + a recorded confidence verdict. The engine is **family-agnostic**: parameterized by a `FamilySpec` + an injected reconciliation oracle, so a future **instrument family** (equity/MF/ETF/bond holdings + buy/sell/dividend/fee events) reuses the same control flow with its own concepts + oracle. Issuer knowledge lives in `profiles.py` as **data, not code**. Bank **savings** parsers share one engine-driven path — `savings.py` (per-table + coordinate-clustered candidates → engine → oracle), so adding a savings issuer is mostly a profile; HDFC keeps bespoke coordinate clustering (the documented `extract_tables` exception).
26	
27	**Active issuers** — we have real statements + passwords, so these get validated on real data:
28	
29	* **HDFC Bank** (`hdfc.py`): Savings / transaction-account statements via **coordinate clustering** (deliberately *not* `extract_tables()` — HDFC's table is semi-ruled). **Self-validating:** running-balance reconciliation + a cross-check against the bank's own SUMMARY (Dr/Cr counts, opening/closing balance); records `documents.status` = `reconciled`/`unreconciled`. Refactored onto the concept core as the **reference implementation**. ✅ **Verified on the user's 4 real statements** — all reconcile *and* bookend, without inspecting any value.
30	* **Axis Bank** (`axis.py`): Savings + Credit Card. **Rewrite landed (step 3):** savings runs on the shared engine-driven path (`savings.py`) — per-table + coordinate-clustered candidates → engine mapping → reconciliation oracle (per-row walk, else total reconcile), oracle-gated so it never persists unreconciled rows. ✅ **2/4 real statements reconcile + persist** (Aug 2024, Oct 2025); the other 2 (May/June 2025) are vintages `extract_tables` mangles into an incomplete ledger — they store **nothing** (safety invariant verified on all 4), pending better extraction. Credit-card has no running balance (count only).
31	* **AU Bank** (`au.py`): engine-driven savings via the shared `savings.py` path — same recipe, just `AUBANK_PROFILE`. ✅ **4/4 real statements reconcile + persist** (the cleanest issuer so far).
32	
33	> _Dropped: **ICICI** — no statements on hand to validate against, so it was removed to focus effort on the issuers we can actually verify (HDFC/Axis/AU). It can return as a profile when data exists._
34	
35	All balance-bearing parsers share the verifier and split parsing from DB-insert, so row logic is unit-testable without a PDF/DB and a parse is validated before it's trusted. **Concept-core steps 1–3 have landed** (the verifier + concept schema; the generic mapping engine + profiles; the shared savings path with Axis + AU Bank on it). Parsers use `pdfplumber` (with optional password). **Password resolution** (password_rules + hybrid unlock in `unlock.py`) is **not yet wired** into the main `muneem ingest --doc-type bank_statement` path; it is exercised via the `@pytest.mark.local_statements` tests on real (gitignored) statements.
36	
37	See `password_rules.py` (prefers user config dir) and `cli.py:ingest`.
38	
39	## 3. Database & CLI
40	* Schema v4 with `documents`, `offers`, and per-bank txn tables (`axis_*`, `hdfc_transactions`). Dedup on sha256. No versioned migration scripts yet (IF NOT EXISTS + user_version pragma). A unified `transactions` table (plus `holdings` for instruments) is planned once the per-bank schemas stabilize (parser-architecture.md §9).
41	* CLI: `ingest <pdf>` (promo or bank_statement via parser), `offers list`, `txns list <bank>`.
42	* Config uses platformdirs for local dirs; secrets via keyring.
43	
44	## 4. Testing & Hygiene
45	* Strong offline regression on `testdata/promo-emails/` + synthetic parser/engine fixtures (default CI).
46	* Security guards in `test_repo_hygiene.py` + license allowlist test.
47	* Local-only tests for real statements (gitignored data; passwords via env/keyring, never committed).
48	
49	## 5. Next Steps
50	Near-term: finish the **Axis + AU** engine-driven parsers (step 3) and precision-test them on real statements alongside HDFC; wire password unlock into the main ingest path; then harden (Milestone D). **Beyond banks (core scope, not backlog):** a second **instrument concept family** for broker / MF / ETF / bond statements → `holdings` + buy/sell/dividend/fee classification. Parked: DESIGN §8.3 (local LLM for sensitive categories), §8.5 (merchant canonicalization).
51	
52	The project stays **local-first**, secrets in the OS keychain, promo corpus safe for CI/LLM experiments.
53

### 383. user (2026-05-31T09:50:08.851Z)

1	# muneem — Project Plan & Roadmap
2	
3	> Status: **active plan, pre-implementation.** This is the "how and in what order" companion to [`DESIGN.md`](../DESIGN.md) (the "what and why"). DESIGN.md owns the architecture and the threat model; this doc owns sequencing, milestones, and concrete tasks. Where they disagree, DESIGN.md wins and this doc should be corrected.
4	>
5	> Created 2026-05-30.
6	>
7	> **Scope:** muneem is a _whole-finance_ bookkeeper. Bank/CC statements are the first slice;
8	> **equity, mutual-fund, ETF, and bond statements are core scope** (not merely the § 6 "generalize"
9	> backlog), enabled by the family-agnostic concept core. Sequencing still leads with the bank slice,
10	> but the data model and parser core are built to host an instrument family + instrument-event
11	> classification (buy / sell / dividend / fees).
12	
13	---
14	
15	## ⚠️ Known issues — must-fix
16	
17	> **Update (2026-06):** the concept-core rework has landed (parser-architecture.md steps 1–2); the
18	> Axis rewrite onto it is **in progress (step 3)**, with **AU Bank** as a sibling issuer on the same
19	> coordinate-clustering + total-reconcile path. **ICICI was removed** (no statements on hand to
20	> validate). HDFC verifies on the user's 4 real statements.
21	
22	- **Axis savings parser needs a thorough rewrite.** The current `extract_tables()`-based
23	  mapping is **wrong and unreliable on real Axis layouts**: the real columns are
24	  `Txn Date | Transaction | Withdrawals | Deposits | Balance | Other Information` (not the
25	  assumed `Debit/Credit`), the column count and date placement **vary between statements**
26	  (Jun 2025 = 8 cols, per-row balance, date merged into the narration cell; Oct 2025 = 9
27	  cols, clean date in col 0, no per-row balance), `can_handle` matches "Statement of Account"
28	  but the text says "Account Statement", and the account-number regex misses. **Stop-gapped
29	  in PR #19** — `AxisParser` now refuses to persist a parse that doesn't reconcile (stores 0
30	  rows, marks the document `unreconciled`), so no mislabeled data is saved, but it parses
31	  nothing useful yet. **Real fix:** an HDFC-style **coordinate-clustering** rewrite, validated
32	  by **total reconciliation** (`opening + ΣDeposits − ΣWithdrawals == closing`, confirmed TRUE
33	  on the Oct 2025 statement) plus the opening/closing bookend. Validate against the June +
34	  October 2025 statements and any future Axis files. Credit-card (`AxisCreditCardParser`) is
35	  separate and unverified on real data. **The rewrite should land on the new concept-core
36	  architecture — see [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md) (it's step 3 of that
37	  plan and the first real test of "generic-first").**
38	
39	---
40	
41	## 1. Decisions locked (2026-05-30)
42	
43	These resolve open decisions from DESIGN.md § 8. They were made by the user explicitly; this section is the start of the decision log.
44	
45	| DESIGN.md ref | Decision | Choice |
46	|---|---|---|
47	| § 8.1 Language | Implementation language for v1 | **Python-only.** Accept the heavier deployment story in exchange for the best PDF/OCR/LLM ecosystem and fastest path on the hard parsing work. Revisit only if the ingestion daemon outgrows Python. |
48	| § 8.4 First slice | What to build first | **Both tracks in parallel** — the promo-corpus parse→store→query spine *and* the Gmail ingestion plumbing, developed as two decoupled tracks that converge at the first bank slice. |
49	| § 8.2 Passwords | Password sourcing strategy | **Hybrid: candidate dictionary → per-sender rule template → interactive prompt.** Cache the winning method per sender. Secrets never touch the repo or logs. |
50	| (practical) | First real bank parser target | **HDFC** — born-digital statements, name+DDMM-style PDF password (per CLAUDE.md), and the issuer with the longest consistent statement history to test against. |
51	
52	**Still parked** (do not pick silently — see § 9 below): § 8.3 LLM model for *sensitive* categories, § 8.5 merchant canonicalization, and the timing of encryption-at-rest (SQLCipher).
53	
54	---
55	
56	## 2. Guiding principles (inherited from DESIGN.md § 5 + CLAUDE.md)
57	
58	1. **Local by default.** All raw statement data and the DB stay on the machine. OCR/LLM work runs locally for any sensitive category. No cloud sync of staging or DB, ever.
59	2. **No cloud LLM on sensitive contents** (bank, CC, broker, MF, insurance) without explicit per-category approval in-session. The **promo corpus is the one non-sensitive playground** where cloud LLMs are policy-permitted — so it's where we validate any LLM-extraction pattern *before* it could ever see a statement.
60	3. **Test on safe, committed data wherever possible.** `testdata/promo-emails/` is the regression corpus and runs in CI. Statement parsing gets CI coverage via **synthetic/redacted fixtures** in the issuer's layout — never real statements.
61	4. **Secrets discipline.** OAuth tokens → OS keychain (Windows Credential Manager). Nothing sensitive in git; don't weaken `.gitignore`. Logs are redacted (no account numbers, balances, line items, or statement text).
62	5. **Decisions parked for humans stay parked.** Don't write code that silently locks in § 9's open items.
63	
64	---
65	
66	## 3. Proposed Python stack
67	
68	User-locked is Python-only. The libraries below are *recommendations* (well-known OSS, per CLAUDE.md's "batteries-included stdlib + well-known OSS over novel dependencies") — open to override, not parked decisions. Anything that is a cloud **service** is flagged and needs a nod before adoption.
69	
70	| Concern | Proposed | Notes |
71	|---|---|---|
72	| Packaging / deps / venv | **uv** + `pyproject.toml` | Fast, modern. Poetry is an equally fine fallback. |
73	| Lint + format | **ruff** | One tool, fast. |
74	| Tests | **pytest** | Regression suite runs offline in CI on the promo corpus + synthetic fixtures. |
75	| PDF text + image extraction | **PyMuPDF (`fitz`)** | Carried from Email-Mining; confirmed the right library. |
76	| Table recovery (born-digital statements) | **pdfplumber** | Coordinate/word-level extraction for HDFC's transaction tables. |
77	| PDF decryption | **pikepdf** (libqpdf) | Robust standard-security-handler support. `pypdf` is the fallback. |
78	| Image dedup | **Pillow + scikit-image SSIM** | Carried from Email-Mining (MD5 hash + SSIM ≥ 0.95). |
79	| OCR (image PDFs, later) | **Tesseract** via `pytesseract` | Local. Windows needs the Tesseract binary installed (setup task). |
80	| Gmail | **google-api-python-client + google-auth-oauthlib** | Read-only scope `gmail.readonly`. |
81	| Secret storage | **keyring** | Uses Windows Credential Manager on win32. |
82	| DB | **sqlite3 (stdlib)** + lightweight versioned migrations | Encryption-at-rest (SQLCipher via `sqlcipher3`) deferred to Milestone D per DESIGN.md. |
83	| CLI | **Typer** | Clean ergonomics; `argparse` (stdlib) is the zero-dependency fallback. |
84	| LLM (promo only, non-sensitive) | deterministic-first; cloud LLM **only if needed and approved** | See § 5 Track A. Sensitive-doc LLM is parked (§ 9 / DESIGN § 8.3). |
85	
86	---
87	
88	## 4. Build order at a glance
89	
90	```
91	Milestone 0 — Decisions & scaffolding  (no sensitive data)
92	        │
93	        ├──────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
94	        ▼                               ▼                               
95	  TRACK A: Promo spine            TRACK B: Gmail ingestion         (run in parallel,
96	  parse → store → query           fetch → stage → catalog          fully decoupled)
97	  (committed corpus, CI)          (real email, local only)
98	        │                               │
99	        └───────────────┬───────────────┘
100	                        ▼
101	        Milestone C — HDFC vertical slice  (CONVERGENCE)
102	        unlock (hybrid passwords) → HDFC parser → transactions → CLI
103	        ▷ completes DESIGN.md § 7
104	                        │
105	                        ▼
106	        Milestone D — Harden & evaluate  (the pre-generalize checkpoint)
107	                        │
108	                        ▼
109	        Generalize  (more issuers, local-LLM fallback, holdings, items, analytics, UI)
110	```
111	
112	Tracks A and B are genuinely independent: A needs no OAuth and no secrets; B needs no parsing. As a single developer you can interleave them in any order — neither blocks the other. **Both must land before Milestone C**, where a fetched-and-unlocked HDFC PDF flows through the parser for the first time.
113	
114	Effort tags below are rough (S / M / L), for relative sequencing only — not calendar estimates.
115	
116	---
117	
118	## 5. Milestones & tasks
119	
120	### Milestone 0 — Decisions & scaffolding · no sensitive data
121	
122	- [ ] **0.1 (S)** Project skeleton: `pyproject.toml` (uv), package dir (name TBD, e.g. `muneem/`), `ruff` + `pytest` configured. *Do not over-materialize the source tree (DESIGN § 9) — create only what 0.x needs.*
123	- [ ] **0.2 (S)** Offline CI: GitHub Actions (or local pre-commit) that installs deps and runs `pytest` against committed data only. A trivial `test_smoke` proves the loop is green.
124	- [ ] **0.3 (S)** Config + secrets foundation: a config loader (env + a gitignored `config.toml`), and a `keyring`-backed secret accessor stub. Confirm `.gitignore` covers `config.toml` and any new secret paths.
125	- [ ] **0.4 (S)** `gitignore` audit: verify the proposed local-only dirs (`testdata/statements/`, staging dir, `*.db`) are blocked. Add a `testdata/statements/.gitkeep`-style note or README so the expected-but-absent dir is documented.
126	- **Exit:** `uv run pytest` is green in CI on a clean checkout; repo layout agreed.
127	
128	### Track A — Promo spine: parse → store → query · committed corpus, fully CI-testable
129	
130	> Resurrects the Email-Mining deals feature as muneem's first working output. Zero secrets, zero OAuth.
131	
132	- [ ] **A.1 (M)** Port Email-Mining's extraction into a real module: PyMuPDF per-page `get_text` + `get_images`, size filter (≥300×300), MD5 + SSIM dedup. Replace the edit-line-55 hardcoded path with proper arguments/config. Replace one-shot stdout with a return value.
133	- [ ] **A.2 (S)** Minimal schema slice + migration v1: `documents` (id, source, external_id, sender, subject, received_date, doc_type, sha256, path, status, created_at) and `offers` (id, document_id, merchant, promo_code, description, expiry_date, redeemed, source_text). Full schema is future work (`docs/schema.md`).
134	- [ ] **A.3 (M)** Promo extraction, **deterministic-first**: regex/heuristics for promo codes + expiry dates over the unified text. Where deterministic extraction misses, an **LLM fallback** is permitted *because promo is non-sensitive* — but that pulls in a cloud service, so it's gated on a quick okay (reuse Email-Mining's Gemini path, go local via Ollama, or use Anthropic — your call when we get there).
135	- [ ] **A.4 (M)** `muneem ingest <pdf>` writes a `documents` + `offers` row; `muneem offers list` reads them back. **Ground-truth + regression test:** establish the known promo codes for each of the 10 corpus PDFs, then a `pytest` that runs the pipeline over `testdata/promo-emails/` and asserts recovery. (Building the ground-truth answer key is itself part of this task.)
136	- **Exit:** `ingest → SQLite → offers list` works end-to-end; green regression test on the committed corpus. Stages 3→4→5 proven with zero risk.
137	
138	### Track B — Gmail ingestion: fetch → stage → catalog · real email, local only
139	
140	> First contact with real (sensitive) email. Everything it writes is gitignored.
141	
142	- [ ] **B.1 (M)** Gmail OAuth2: register a Google Cloud project, `gmail.readonly` scope, store the token in `keyring`. Document the one-time consent flow.
143	- [ ] **B.2 (M)** Financial-document detection v1: a **sender allowlist** + server-side query (`has:attachment filename:pdf from:… newer_than:…`). Start narrow and precise; broaden later. Pull matching messages + PDF attachments into a gitignored staging dir.
144	- [ ] **B.3 (S)** Incremental sync: persist Gmail `historyId`; on re-run, fetch only what's new. Idempotent (re-running never duplicates `documents`, keyed by message id + attachment hash).
145	- [ ] **B.4 (S)** Catalog fetched attachments as `documents` rows (status `locked`/`unparsed`), with sha256 + staging path. `muneem fetch --since 90d` is the entry point.
146	- **Exit:** `muneem fetch` populates the staging dir + `documents` rows from a narrow real-Gmail query. (Manual/local verification — not CI; it touches a real account.)
147	
148	### Milestone C — HDFC vertical slice · CONVERGENCE · completes DESIGN.md § 7
149	
150	> A fetched HDFC statement gets unlocked and parsed into real transactions. This is the moment every stage runs once on real data the user cares about.
151	
152	- [ ] **C.1 (M)** Unlocking (Stage 2) with the **hybrid** strategy: candidate dictionary derived from user attributes (e.g. `first4(name).upper() + DDMM(dob)`) → per-sender rule template → interactive prompt. Cache the winning method per sender in the keychain. **No password material in repo or logs.** Decrypt with pikepdf; advance `documents.status` to `unlocked`.
153	- [ ] **C.2 (L)** HDFC parser (Stage 3, the hard 80%): born-digital text-layer + pdfplumber table recovery → normalized transactions (txn_date, value_date, amount, direction, narration, running balance). **Synthetic HDFC-layout fixtures** committed for CI; real statements stay gitignored locally.
154	- [ ] **C.3 (M)** Schema migration v2: `accounts` (issuer, account_type, last4, holder) + `transactions` (account_id, document_id, txn fields, category, merchant_id-nullable). Populate from the parser. `muneem txns list --last-month` prints real parsed transactions.
155	- **Exit:** **DESIGN.md § 7 vertical slice complete** — Gmail → HDFC statement → unlocked → parsed → SQLite → CLI lists last month's transactions, on real data, locally.
156	
157	### Milestone D — Harden & evaluate · the pre-generalize checkpoint
158	
159	- [ ] **D.1 (M)** Redacted structured logging (assert no statement text / account numbers leak); idempotent, resumable re-runs across all stages.
160	- [ ] **D.2 (S)** Encryption-at-rest decision (DESIGN § 5.4): adopt SQLCipher now or schedule it. (Parked item — bring evidence, then decide.)
161	- [ ] **D.3 (M)** **Evaluation writeup:** how well did the deterministic HDFC parser hold across statement vintages? Where does it break? Which categories will need the local-LLM fallback? This is the evidence DESIGN § 7 asks for *before* generalizing. Capture in `docs/parser-notes-hdfc.md`.
162	- **Exit:** the slice is robust and re-runnable; a written go/no-go on generalization with real evidence.
163	
164	---
165	
166	## 6. Generalize (post-checkpoint backlog — scope after Milestone D)
167	
168	Ordered roughly by leverage, not committed:
169	
170	- Second/third bank parsers + the first **credit-card bill** parser (reuses unlock + parser scaffolding).
171	- **Local-LLM long-tail fallback** (Ollama) for issuers that don't justify a bespoke parser — **local, because these are sensitive** (resolves DESIGN § 8.3).
172	- Broker / MF statements → `holdings` (point-in-time positions; the investment-timeline use-case).
173	- Itemized receipts (Swiggy/Amazon/BigBasket/etc.) → `items` (the price-intelligence + catalog use-cases).
174	- **Merchant canonicalization** → `merchants` (DESIGN § 8.5 — defer until real data exposes the actual mess, then decide rules-file vs. library vs. learned).
175	- Analytics surfaces for the DESIGN § 2 use-cases (CLI views first; optional small **local** web UI later).
176	
177	---
178	
179	## 7. Cross-cutting workstreams (run throughout, not a phase)
180	
181	- **Security & secrets hygiene** — keychain for tokens + cached passwords; redacted logs; gitignore audits each milestone; encryption-at-rest tracked to D.2.
182	- **Schema migrations from day one** — versioned migration scripts; the schema *will* churn as new issuers land. Keep `docs/schema.md` current.
183	- **Test-corpus curation** — ground-truth answer key for the promo corpus (A.4); synthetic/redacted statement fixtures for CI (C.2). Never commit real statements.
184	- **Decision log** — append resolutions to § 1 here and mark the corresponding DESIGN.md § 8 item resolved.
185	
186	---
187	
188	## 8. Risks & mitigations
189	
190	| Risk | Mitigation |
191	|---|---|
192	| Sensitive data leaks into git, logs, or a cloud LLM | Local-by-default; promo-only LLM playground; redacted logging (D.1); gitignore audits; the § 2 principles are non-negotiable. |
193	| HDFC parser is brittle across statement vintages | Synthetic fixtures + the D.3 evaluation gate *before* generalizing; deterministic-first with a planned local-LLM fallback for the long tail. |
194	| Password sourcing fails noisily on backfill | Hybrid strategy degrades to interactive prompt; cache per sender so it's a one-time cost. |
195	| Gmail OAuth / quota friction | Read-only scope, server-side queries (cheap), incremental `historyId` sync; narrow allowlist first. |
196	| Scope creep into the full § 2 vision before the slice works | Hard checkpoint at Milestone D; generalization is explicitly post-checkpoint. |
197	| Two parallel tracks diverge / integration surprises at C | Both write through the *same* `documents` table and schema-migration discipline from A.2/B.4, so convergence is a join, not a rewrite. |
198	
199	---
200	
201	## 9. Still-open decisions (do NOT pick silently)
202	
203	Carried from DESIGN.md § 8, not yet resolved — surface and ask when a milestone forces them:
204	
205	- **§ 8.3 — LLM model for sensitive categories.** Coupled to your hardware (GPU? RAM?) and latency tolerance. Forced at the start of the generalize phase (local-LLM fallback). Ollama + Llama/Qwen/Mistral is the likely shape, but unconfirmed.
206	- **§ 8.5 — Merchant canonicalization.** Deferred until real receipt data exposes the mess. Forced when `items`/`merchants` land.
207	- **Encryption-at-rest timing** (D.2) — SQLCipher now vs. later.
208	- **Promo-track LLM** (A.3) — only reached if deterministic extraction underperforms; cloud is policy-permitted for promo but still needs a quick okay on which vendor.
209	
210	---
211	
212	## 10. Definition of done for v1
213	
214	The DESIGN.md § 7 slice, hardened: **`muneem fetch` pulls HDFC statements from Gmail → unlocks them with the hybrid password strategy → the HDFC parser writes normalized `transactions` to SQLite → `muneem txns list` shows last month's activity — all locally, with redacted logs, idempotent re-runs, green CI on synthetic fixtures, and a written evaluation of where to go next.** The promo "forgotten deals" feature ships alongside as the Track A deliverable.
215

98	## 7. Failsafes — the "traps" (loud drift, never silent)
99	
100	The Axis lesson: a broken parse must *announce itself*. Built-in tripwires, each → flag + provenance,
101	never store-and-pretend:
102	
103	- header label matches no lexicon entry → "unknown field, mapped by structure — review"
104	- reconciliation breaks, or parsed counts ≠ printed summary → **quarantine** (store nothing or store
105	  flagged; today's stop-gap already refuses to persist unreconciled rows)
106	- a profile's expected anchor is missing → "format may have changed for <bank>"
107	- _(L3)_ VLM output won't reconcile, or disagrees with a deterministic parse → reject the VLM

5	dev) with a DeprecationWarning. Never commit real rules or the file itself.
6	
7	See DESIGN.md §8.2 and CLAUDE.md security rules. Create the file in your config dir
8	with bank: password-or-rule entries (never commit it).
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import warnings
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	import yaml
17	
18	from muneem.config import CONFIG_DIR
19	
20	
21	def get_password_for_bank(bank_name: str) -> str | None:
22	    """Return the password (or derived rule string) for a bank, or None.
23	
24	    Resolution order:
25	    1. <config_dir>/password_rules.yaml  (recommended location)
26	    2. ./password_rules.yaml  (legacy, emits DeprecationWarning)
27	    """
28	    candidates = [
29	        (CONFIG_DIR / "password_rules.yaml", False),
30	        (Path("password_rules.yaml"), True),
31	    ]
32	    for path, is_legacy in candidates:
33	        if path.exists():
34	            if is_legacy:
35	                warnings.warn(
36	                    "Loading password_rules from CWD (./password_rules.yaml) is deprecated. "
37	                    f"Move it to {CONFIG_DIR / 'password_rules.yaml'} for future-proofing. "
38	                    "See docs and CLAUDE.md (never commit real password material).",
39	                    DeprecationWarning,
40	                    stacklevel=2,
41	                )
42	            with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as f:
43	                rules = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
44	            val = rules.get(bank_name)
45	            return str(val) if val else None
46	    return None

### 423. user (2026-05-31T09:57:33.972Z)

1	"""PDF text extraction (the deterministic, born-digital path).
2	
3	Uses **pdfminer.six (MIT)** — a permissive, commercially-safe, pure-Python text
4	extractor. We deliberately do *not* use PyMuPDF/``fitz``: it is **AGPL-3.0**,
5	incompatible with muneem's commercial-safety principle (see docs/DEPENDENCIES.md).
6	
7	NFKC normalization is applied because the real corpus returns ligatures
8	(e.g. U+FB00 'ﬀ') that would otherwise break naive matching. Image extraction +
9	OCR (for codes rasterized into banners, e.g. Swiggy's CTSWIGGYUX) is the image
10	tier and lands with the OCR work (pypdfium2 + Tesseract, both permissive).
11	"""
12	
13	from __future__ import annotations
14	
15	import hashlib
16	import unicodedata
17	from dataclasses import dataclass
18	from pathlib import Path
19	
20	from pdfminer.high_level import extract_text
21	
22	
23	@dataclass
24	class Extracted:
25	    """The result of reading a PDF's text layer."""
26	
27	    path: Path
28	    sha256: str
29	    text: str
30	
31	
32	def _normalize(text: str) -> str:
33	    """NFKC-normalize so ligatures fold to ASCII (U+FB00 'ﬀ' -> 'ff')."""
34	    return unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)
35	
36	
37	def file_sha256(path: Path | str) -> str:
38	    """Stream the file and return its hex SHA-256 (used for document dedup)."""
39	    digest = hashlib.sha256()
40	    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
41	        for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(65536), b""):
42	            digest.update(chunk)
43	    return digest.hexdigest()
44	
45	
46	def extract(pdf_path: Path | str) -> Extracted:
47	    """Extract and NFKC-normalize the full text layer of a PDF."""
48	    path = Path(pdf_path)
49	    text = extract_text(str(path))
50	    return Extracted(path=path, sha256=file_sha256(path), text=_normalize(text))
51

1	"""Local paths and configuration.
2	
3	muneem is local-first: config lives in the OS config dir, data (DB + staging)
4	in the OS data dir. Nothing here touches the repository or the network, and no
5	secrets are stored here — those go through :mod:`muneem.secrets` (the keychain).
6	"""
7	
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import tomllib
11	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	from platformdirs import PlatformDirs
16	
17	_dirs = PlatformDirs(appname="muneem", appauthor=False)
18	
19	CONFIG_DIR = Path(_dirs.user_config_dir)
20	DATA_DIR = Path(_dirs.user_data_dir)
21	CONFIG_FILE = CONFIG_DIR / "config.toml"
22	DB_PATH = DATA_DIR / "muneem.db"
23	STAGING_DIR = DATA_DIR / "staging"
24	
25	
26	@dataclass(frozen=True)
27	class Config:
28	    """Resolved local paths plus any values from ``config.toml``."""
29	
30	    config_dir: Path = CONFIG_DIR
31	    data_dir: Path = DATA_DIR
32	    config_file: Path = CONFIG_FILE
33	    db_path: Path = DB_PATH
34	    staging_dir: Path = STAGING_DIR
35	    values: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
36	
37	
38	def load_config() -> Config:
39	    """Load configuration, reading ``config.toml`` if it exists. Read-only."""
40	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
41	    if CONFIG_FILE.is_file():
42	        with CONFIG_FILE.open("rb") as fh:
43	            values = tomllib.load(fh)
44	    return Config(values=values)
45	
46	
47	def ensure_dirs() -> None:
48	    """Create the local config/data/staging dirs if missing. Never touches the repo."""
49	    for directory in (CONFIG_DIR, DATA_DIR, STAGING_DIR):
50	        directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

### 437. assistant (2026-05-31T10:00:59.634Z)

"""Password rule loader for statement decryption (hybrid strategy support).

Prefers the user config dir (via platformdirs, e.g. ~/.config/muneem/password_rules.yaml
on Linux) for real rules. Falls back to legacy CWD "password_rules.yaml" (common during
dev) with a DeprecationWarning. **Never commit real rules or the file itself** — `.gitignore`
blocks `password_rules.*`.

See DESIGN.md §8.2, CLAUDE.md security rules, and docs/ingesting-statements.md for the file
format. Entries are either ``<bank>: <password>`` or a top-level ``candidates: [..]`` list.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import warnings
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import yaml

from muneem.config import CONFIG_DIR


def _load_rules() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Load the rules dict from the config dir (preferred) or legacy CWD file; ``{}`` if absent.

    Resolution order:
    1. ``<config_dir>/password_rules.yaml``  (recommended location)
    2. ``./password_rules.yaml``  (legacy, emits DeprecationWarning)
    """
    for path, is_legacy in (
        (CONFIG_DIR / "password_rules.yaml", False),
        (Path("password_rules.yaml"), True),
    ):
        if path.exists():
            if is_legacy:
                warnings.warn(
                    "Loading password_rules from CWD (./password_rules.yaml) is deprecated. "
                    f"Move it to {CONFIG_DIR / 'password_rules.yaml'} for future-proofing. "
                    "See docs and CLAUDE.md (never commit real password material).",
                    DeprecationWarning,
                    stacklevel=3,
                )
            with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as f:
                return yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
    return {}


def get_password_for_bank(bank_name: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the password (or derived rule string) for a bank, or ``None``."""
    val = _load_rules().get(bank_name)
    return str(val) if val else None


def candidate_passwords() -> list[str]:
    """Every configured password to try as an unlock candidate, **bank-agnostic**.

    The ingest path resolves the password *before* it knows the issuer (it needs the decrypted
    text to pick a parser), so it tries all configured passwords. Collects each per-bank value
    plus any entries under a top-level ``candidates:`` list; order preserved, de-duplicated.
    Quote numeric passwords in the YAML so leading zeros survive.
    """
    rules = _load_rules()
    collected: list[str] = []
    for key, val in rules.items():
        if key == "candidates" and isinstance(val, list):
            collected.extend(str(v) for v in val if v not in (None, ""))
        elif isinstance(val, str | int | float):
            collected.append(str(val))
    seen: set[str] = set()
    unique: list[str] = []
    for pw in collected:
        if pw and pw not in seen:
            seen.add(pw)
            unique.append(pw)
    return unique

### 440. assistant (2026-05-31T10:01:12.814Z)

"""Resolve a statement PDF's password for the ingest path — the hybrid strategy, wired.

DESIGN.md §8.2 / ROADMAP C.1. Resolution order when a statement is encrypted:

1. **Candidate dictionary** — passwords cached in the OS keychain (from earlier successful
   unlocks) plus those configured in ``password_rules.yaml`` (see
   :mod:`muneem.password_rules`).
2. **Interactive prompt** — a hidden ``getpass`` prompt (skipped with ``interactive=False``,
   e.g. automation / CI).

A password that works **via the prompt** is cached in the keychain so the next ingest is
friction-free. Nothing here logs a password, writes one to the repo, or echoes it; the cache is
the OS keychain (:mod:`muneem.secrets`), never a file.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import getpass
import json
from pathlib import Path

from muneem import secrets
from muneem.password_rules import candidate_passwords
from muneem.unlock import is_encrypted, unlock

# Keychain entry holding a JSON list of known-good statement passwords (the learned candidates).
_CACHE_KEY = "statement_unlock_candidates"


def _cached_candidates() -> list[str]:
    raw = secrets.get_secret(_CACHE_KEY)
    if not raw:
        return []
    try:
        return [p for p in json.loads(raw) if isinstance(p, str) and p]
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        return []


def _remember(password: str) -> None:
    """Add a working password to the keychain candidate cache (deduped). Never logged."""
    if not password:
        return
    current = _cached_candidates()
    if password not in current:
        secrets.set_secret(_CACHE_KEY, json.dumps([*current, password]))


def resolve_password(pdf_path: Path | str, *, interactive: bool = True) -> str | None:
    """Return a working password for ``pdf_path``.

    ``""`` means the PDF is not encrypted (no password needed). ``None`` means it is encrypted and
    no candidate (or prompt) unlocked it. A prompt-resolved password is cached for next time.
    """
    path = Path(pdf_path)
    if not is_encrypted(path):
        return ""

    candidates = [*_cached_candidates(), *candidate_passwords()]
    prompt_fn = None
    if interactive:

        def prompt_fn() -> str | None:  # noqa: E306 — local prompt; no echo, never logged
            return getpass.getpass(f"Password for {path.name}: ") or None

    result = unlock(path, candidates=candidates, prompt_fn=prompt_fn)
    if not result.ok:
        return None
    if result.method == "prompt" and result.password:
        _remember(result.password)
    return result.password or ""

"""Tests for the ingest-path password resolver (muneem.statement_unlock).

Synthetic encrypted PDFs (pypdf), no real statements. The OS keychain is replaced with an in-memory
dict so tests never touch the real keyring (and run cleanly in headless CI). No real password.
"""

import json
from pathlib import Path

import pypdf
import pytest

from muneem import statement_unlock


def _pdf(path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> Path:
    writer = pypdf.PdfWriter()
    writer.add_blank_page(width=200, height=200)
    if password:
        writer.encrypt(password)
    with open(path, "wb") as fh:
        writer.write(fh)
    return path


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def fake_keychain(monkeypatch):
    """Replace muneem.secrets with an in-memory store (no real keyring, CI-safe)."""
    store: dict[str, str] = {}
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock.secrets, "get_secret", store.get)
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock.secrets, "set_secret", lambda k, v: store.__setitem__(k, v))
    # default: no configured candidates unless a test overrides
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock, "candidate_passwords", list)
    return store


def test_plaintext_pdf_needs_no_password(tmp_path):
    assert statement_unlock.resolve_password(_pdf(tmp_path / "plain.pdf")) == ""


def test_resolves_via_configured_candidate(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock, "candidate_passwords", lambda: ["wrong", "secret123"])
    pdf = _pdf(tmp_path / "enc.pdf", password="secret123")
    assert statement_unlock.resolve_password(pdf, interactive=False) == "secret123"


def test_no_prompt_failure_returns_none(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock, "candidate_passwords", lambda: ["wrong"])
    pdf = _pdf(tmp_path / "enc.pdf", password="secret123")
    assert statement_unlock.resolve_password(pdf, interactive=False) is None


def test_prompt_resolves_then_caches_for_next_time(tmp_path, monkeypatch, fake_keychain):
    pdf = _pdf(tmp_path / "enc.pdf", password="secret123")
    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock.getpass, "getpass", lambda prompt="": "secret123")
    assert statement_unlock.resolve_password(pdf, interactive=True) == "secret123"
    # the winning password is cached in the (fake) keychain
    assert "secret123" in json.loads(fake_keychain[statement_unlock._CACHE_KEY])

    # a *new* file with the same password now unlocks from the cache — no prompt
    def _no_prompt(prompt=""):
        raise AssertionError("should not prompt once the password is cached")

    monkeypatch.setattr(statement_unlock.getpass, "getpass", _no_prompt)
    pdf2 = _pdf(tmp_path / "enc2.pdf", password="secret123")
    assert statement_unlock.resolve_password(pdf2, interactive=True) == "secret123"

### 457. assistant (2026-05-31T10:05:49.365Z)

# Ingesting bank statements (with password unlock)

`muneem ingest` reads a bank-statement PDF, **unlocks it if it's password-protected**, parses it
into normalized transactions, and stores only a parse that **reconciles** (the running balance
walks, or the totals match the printed opening/closing). Supported issuers today: **HDFC, Axis, AU
Bank**.

```bash
muneem ingest /path/to/statement.pdf --doc-type bank_statement
```

If the PDF is encrypted, muneem resolves the password with a **hybrid strategy** (no password is ever
written to the repo or logged):

1. **Cached candidates** — passwords from earlier successful unlocks, stored in your **OS keychain**.
2. **Configured candidates** — entries in `password_rules.yaml` (see below).
3. **Interactive prompt** — a hidden prompt (`getpass`). A password entered here that works is
   **cached in the keychain**, so the next ingest of any statement with the same password is
   friction-free.

On success you'll see, e.g.:

```
ingested statement.pdf (doc 1); parsed 37 transaction(s) via HDFCParser [reconciled]
```

`[reconciled]` means the parse is trusted; `[unreconciled]` means it couldn't be verified, so
**nothing was stored** (muneem never persists mislabeled rows). Read parsed rows back with
`muneem txns list hdfc` (or `axis` / `au`).

## Enable unattended unlocking (recommended)

For batch/backfill or `--no-prompt` runs, put your statement passwords in a **gitignored** rules
file so you're never prompted.

1. Find your config directory:

   ```bash
   muneem info        # → "config dir : /home/you/.config/muneem" (varies by OS)
   ```

2. Create `<config dir>/password_rules.yaml`. **Never commit it** — `.gitignore` already blocks
   `password_rules.*`, and it lives in your OS config dir (outside the repo) anyway.

   ```yaml
   # <config-dir>/password_rules.yaml   —   NEVER commit this file.
   # Quote every password (keeps leading zeros; avoids YAML parsing "0106" as a number).

   # Per-issuer passwords:
   hdfc: "<your-hdfc-pdf-password>"
   axis: "<your-axis-pdf-password>"
   aubank: "<your-au-pdf-password>"

   # …or a bank-agnostic list of passwords to try (handy when the issuer label varies):
   candidates:
     - "<password-a>"
     - "<password-b>"
   ```

   muneem tries **all** of these (it has to pick the password before it can read the PDF to know
   the issuer), so either form works. A legacy `./password_rules.yaml` in the current directory is
   still read but warns — prefer the config dir.

3. Ingest without prompts:

   ```bash
   muneem ingest statement.pdf --doc-type bank_statement --no-prompt
   ```

   `--no-prompt` uses only cached + configured candidates and **never** opens an interactive prompt
   (so it's safe in cron/CI). Without it, an unresolved password falls back to the hidden prompt.

## Security

- **Nothing sensitive is committed or logged.** `password_rules.*` is gitignored; resolved passwords
  live only in memory and the **OS keychain** (Windows Credential Manager / macOS Keychain / Linux
  Secret Service), via `muneem.secrets`.
- **No decrypted copy is written to disk** — the password is handed straight to the text extractor
  and the parser in memory.
- Transaction parses are **reconciliation-gated**: an unverifiable parse stores nothing rather than
  risk mislabeled rows.

## Troubleshooting

| Message | Meaning / fix |
|---|---|
| `Could not unlock <file>: no working password` | None of the cached/configured candidates worked. Run without `--no-prompt` to type it, or add it to `password_rules.yaml`. |
| `No parser recognizes <file> as a supported bank statement` | The issuer isn't supported yet (today: HDFC, Axis, AU Bank), or the text didn't match. |
| `… [unreconciled]` (0 transactions) | The parser couldn't verify the parse (e.g. an Axis vintage `extract_tables` mangles); nothing was stored. See [`parser-architecture.md`](./parser-architecture.md). |

## Headless Linux note

Keychain storage needs a backend. Use a desktop keyring (GNOME Keyring / KWallet), or
`pip install keyrings.alt` for a file-based backend. Until then, `password_rules.yaml` + `--no-prompt`
works without any keychain (you just won't get prompt-caching).

### 487. user (2026-05-31T10:09:44.063Z)

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""muneem sensitive-file guard (invoked by the pre-commit hook).
3	
4	Refuses to *stage* files that policy says must never be committed: real
5	statements (any PDF, or anything under ``testdata/`` outside the allowed
6	corpora), OAuth secrets, password rules, local databases, key material, and
7	``.env`` files. This is the PRE-commit backstop to ``.gitignore`` — it stops the
8	``git add -f`` / ``git add -A`` mistake before it becomes a commit (the very
9	thing that caused an earlier statement leak). The post-commit complement is
10	``tests/test_repo_hygiene.py``.
11	
12	Exit 0 when nothing sensitive is staged, 1 otherwise. A deliberate, reviewed
13	exception can be committed with ``git commit --no-verify``.
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	import fnmatch
19	import os
20	import subprocess
21	import sys
22	
23	# Only these subtrees of testdata/ are non-sensitive and may be committed.
24	ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ("testdata/promo-emails/", "testdata/synthetic/")
25	SECRET_NAMES = {"credentials.json", "token.json", "token.pickle", "passwords.txt"}
26	
27	
28	def is_sensitive(path: str) -> str | None:
29	    """Return a human-readable reason if ``path`` must not be committed, else None."""
30	    p = path.replace("\\", "/").strip()
31	    if not p or p.startswith(ALLOWED_PREFIXES):
32	        return None
33	    name = p.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
34	    low = p.lower()
35	    nlow = name.lower()
36	    if p.startswith("testdata/"):
37	        return "under testdata/ — sensitive by default (only promo-emails/ and synthetic/ allowed)"
38	    if low.endswith(".pdf"):
39	        return "PDF outside the allowed corpora (real statements must stay local)"
40	    if low.endswith((".db", ".sqlite", ".sqlite3")):
41	        return "local database (may contain parsed statement data)"
42	    if low.endswith((".pem", ".key")):
43	        return "key material"
44	    if nlow in SECRET_NAMES:
45	        return "OAuth / credential secret"
46	    if nlow.startswith("oauth_token"):
47	        return "OAuth token"
48	    if fnmatch.fnmatch(nlow, "password_rules.*"):
49	        return "password rules"
50	    if (nlow == ".env" or nlow.startswith(".env.")) and nlow != ".env.example":
51	        return ".env file (may contain secrets)"
52	    return None
53	
54	
55	def _staged_paths() -> list[str]:
56	    # Tests inject a newline-separated list via PRECOMMIT_TEST_FILES (no git needed).
57	    override = os.environ.get("PRECOMMIT_TEST_FILES")
58	    if override is not None:
59	        return [ln for ln in override.replace("\r\n", "\n").split("\n") if ln.strip()]
60	    # -z keeps paths with spaces/unicode intact (the real statements have spaces).
61	    out = subprocess.run(
62	        ["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only", "-z", "--diff-filter=ACM"],
63	        capture_output=True,
64	        text=True,
65	        check=True,
66	    ).stdout
67	    return [p for p in out.split("\0") if p.strip()]
68	
69	
70	def main() -> int:
71	    offenders = [(p, reason) for p in _staged_paths() if (reason := is_sensitive(p))]
72	    if not offenders:
73	        return 0
74	    print("muneem pre-commit: refusing to commit sensitive file(s):", file=sys.stderr)
75	    for p, reason in offenders:
76	        print(f"  x {p}\n      {reason}", file=sys.stderr)
77	    print(
78	        "\nThese must never reach git (CLAUDE.md security rules; see .gitignore).\n"
79	        "If this is a deliberate, reviewed exception: git commit --no-verify",
80	        file=sys.stderr,
81	    )
82	    return 1
83	
84	
85	if __name__ == "__main__":
86	    raise SystemExit(main())
87

✅ **Done, CI green, clean slate.** `muneem ingest` now unlocks and parses encrypted statements from the CLI.

## What shipped — [muneem#28](https://github.com/avidullu/muneem/pull/28) (C.1)
- **Wired the hybrid unlock into `muneem ingest --doc-type bank_statement`**: keychain-cached + `password_rules.yaml` candidates → hidden prompt, with a prompt-resolved password **cached in the OS keychain** for next time. The password is resolved before extraction, passed in-memory to the parser, and **never logged or written to the repo**; no decrypted copy touches disk.
- **`--no-prompt`** flag for unattended/cron runs; output shows the `[reconciled]`/`[unreconciled]` verdict.
- **Setup doc: [`docs/ingesting-statements.md`](docs/ingesting-statements.md)** — how to use it, how to enable unattended unlocking (the `password_rules.yaml` format + location), security notes, and troubleshooting. Linked from README + CURRENT_STATE.
- **Bonus fix:** the pre-commit guard was false-positiving on `src/muneem/password_rules.py` (the loader *module*) — narrowed it to block only the secret `password_rules.{yaml,yml,json}`, regression-tested.

Verified **end-to-end on your real statements** (password in-process; counts/status only): HDFC 37 / Axis Aug'24 6 / AU 17 transactions, all `[reconciled]`.

### 510. user (2026-05-31T10:14:59.269Z)

Can we add some github action tests which avoid regression when working from other branches of this repo using the hdfc, aubank statements that we have? The repo will always be private so privacy can be a bit relaxed.

### 511. assistant (2026-05-31T10:16:47.967Z)

Happy to set this up — running the real HDFC/AU statements in CI is the strongest regression guard. But this is the one decision in this project I don't want to make for you, because it's **hard to reverse**: the real statements are exactly what the local-first design + the repo's own guards (`.gitignore`, the pre-commit hook, `test_repo_hygiene`) exist to keep off any cloud.

The thing "private repo" doesn't soften: **git history is permanent and lives on GitHub's servers.** Once a real (even encrypted) statement is committed, it's on GitHub's cloud forever — recoverable if the password ever leaks, if the repo is later made public/forked, or if a collaborator/token is compromised. That's different from "only my machine has it."

The good news: there's a way to get **real-statement CI on every branch without any of that** — so let me get your call on the approach.
